## Federal Courts Rules {#federal-courts-rules .Title-of-Act}

[SOR]{.abbr title="Statutory Orders and Regulations"}/98-106
:::

[FEDERAL COURTS ACT](/eng/acts/F-7)

Registration 1998-02-05

Rules for Regulating the Practice and Procedure in the Federal Court of
Appeal and the Federal Court

P.C. 1998-125 1998-02-05
::::

::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: {.section .order}
Whereas, pursuant to subsection 46(4)[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^a^](#footnotea_e){#footnotea_e-ID0EHABA} of the Federal
Court Act, a copy of the proposed Federal Court Rules, 1998 was
published in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) Part I on
September 20, 1997 and interested persons were invited to make
representations with respect to the proposed Rules;

Therefore, His Excellency the Governor General in Council, on the
recommendation of the Minister of Justice, pursuant to subsection
46(1)[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^b^](#footnoteb_e){#footnoteb_e-ID0EFCAA} of the Federal
Court Act, hereby approves the annexed Federal Court Rules, 1998, made
by the rules committee of the Federal Court of Canada on January 26,
1998.

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^a^](#footnotea_e-ID0EHABA){#footnotea_e}S.C. 1990, c.
  8, s. 14(4)
  :::

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^b^](#footnoteb_e-ID0EFCAA){#footnoteb_e}S.C. 1990, c.
  8, s. 14(1)
  :::
:::

## [Short Title]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1012658 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short title

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** These Rules may be cited as
the [Federal Courts Rules](/eng/regulations/SOR-98-106).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 2
:::

## [PART 1]{.HLabel1}[Application and Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1012663 .Part}

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1012664 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[1.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} These Rules apply to all proceedings
  in the Federal Court of Appeal and the Federal Court unless otherwise
  provided by or under an Act of Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inconsistency with Act

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the event of any inconsistency between these Rules
  and an Act of Parliament or a regulation made under such an Act, that
  Act or regulation prevails to the extent of the inconsistency.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 2
:::

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1012672 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
apply in these Rules.

[[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the [Federal Courts
    Act](/eng/acts/F-7). ([Loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[action]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[action]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a proceeding referred to in
    rule 169. ([action]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[address for service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[address for service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a party's address
    for service under rule 126.1. ([adresse aux fins de
    signification]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Administrator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Administrator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Chief Administrator
    appointed under section 5 of the [Courts Administration Service
    Act](/eng/acts/C-45.5), or a person acting on his or her
    behalf. ([administrateur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Admiralty action]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Admiralty action]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an action in which
    the Court exercises jurisdiction under section 22 of the
    Act. ([action en matière d'amirauté]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[appeal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[appeal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a proceeding referred to in
    rule 335. ([appel]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[applicant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[applicant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} except in the case of an application that has
      been certified as a class proceeding, includes a person on whose
      behalf an application is commenced; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an application that has been
      certified as a class proceeding, means

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in respect of the common questions of law or
        fact, the representative applicant, and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in respect of individual questions, the member
        to whom those questions apply. ([demandeur]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

[[application]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[application]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a proceeding referred to
    in rule 300. ([demande]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[assessment officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[assessment officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an officer of the
    Registry designated by an order of the Court, a judge or a
    prothonotary, and includes, in respect of a reference, the referee
    presiding in the reference. ([officier taxateur]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[business day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[business day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, SOR/2015-21, s.
    1\]]{.Repealed}

[[case management judge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[case management judge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a judge assigned
    under paragraph 383(a) or rule 383.1 and includes a prothonotary
    assigned under paragraph 383(b). ([juge responsable de la gestion de
    l'instance]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[certified copy]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[certified copy]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a document in
    the custody of the Registry, means a copy of the document certified
    by an officer of the Registry. ([copie certifiée
    conforme]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Christmas recess]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Christmas recess]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
    SOR/2021-244, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

[[Court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, as the circumstances require,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Federal Court of Appeal, including, in
      respect of a motion, a single judge of that court; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Federal Court, including a prothonotary
      acting within the jurisdiction conferred under these
      Rules. ([Cour]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Court file]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Court file]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the file maintained
    pursuant to rule 23 or 24. ([dossier de la Cour]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[dispute resolution conference]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[dispute resolution conference]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    conference ordered under rule 386. ([conférence de règlement des
    litiges]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[filed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[filed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a document, means
    accepted for filing under rule 72. ([déposé]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[garnishee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[garnishee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person in respect of whom
    an order attaching a debt to a judgment debtor has been made under
    rule 449. ([tiers saisi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Hague Convention]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Hague Convention]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Convention on the
    Service Abroad of Judicial and Extrajudicial Documents in Civil or
    Commercial Matters signed at The Hague on November 15,
    1965. ([Convention de La Haye]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[hearing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[hearing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a conference held under
    these Rules. ([audience]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[holiday]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[holiday]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a Saturday;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a [[holiday]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in
      subsection 35(1) of the [Interpretation Act](/eng/acts/I-21);

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if New Year's Day, Canada Day or Remembrance Day
      falls on a Saturday or Sunday, the following Monday;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if Christmas Day falls on a Saturday or Sunday,
      the following Monday and Tuesday; and

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if Christmas Day falls on a Friday, the following
      Monday. ([jour férié]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[intervener]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[intervener]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who has been
    granted status as an intervener under rule
    109. ([intervenant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[issued]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[issued]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an originating document, dated,
      signed, sealed with the seal of the Court and assigned a Court
      file number by the Administrator; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of any other document, dated, signed
      and sealed with the seal of the Court by the
      Administrator. ([délivré]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[local office]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[local office]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an office of the Registry
    of the Court established by the Administrator other than the
    principal office. ([bureau local]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[motion]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[motion]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a request to the Court under,
    or to enforce, these Rules. ([requête]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[oath]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[oath]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a solemn affirmation within
    the meaning of subsection 14(1) of the [Canada Evidence
    Act](/eng/acts/C-5). ([serment]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[order]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[order]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a judgment;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a decision or other disposition of a tribunal;
      and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a determination of a reference under section 18.3
      of the Act. ([ordonnance]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[originating document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[originating document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a document
    referred to in rule 63. ([acte introductif
    d'instance]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[party]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[party]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an action, a plaintiff, defendant
      or third party;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an application,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} where a tribunal brings a reference under
        section 18.3 of the Act, a person who becomes a party in
        accordance with rule 323,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the Attorney General of Canada brings a
        reference under section 18.3 of the Act, the Attorney General of
        Canada and any other person who becomes a party in accordance
        with rule 323, and

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, an applicant or
        respondent;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an appeal, an appellant or
      respondent; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a motion, the person bringing the
      motion or a respondent thereto. ([parties]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a tribunal, an
    unincorporated association and a
    partnership. ([personne]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[plaintiff]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[plaintiff]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} except in the case of an action that has been
      certified as a class proceeding, includes a person on whose behalf
      an action is commenced; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an action that has been certified
      as a class proceeding, means

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in respect of the common questions of law or
        fact, the representative plaintiff, and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in respect of individual questions, the member
        to whom those questions apply. ([demandeur]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

[[pleading]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[pleading]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a document in a proceeding in
    which a claim is initiated, defined, defended or answered. ([acte de
    procédure]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[principal office]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[principal office]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the head office of
    the Registry of the Court established by the Administrator. ([bureau
    principal]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[referee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[referee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person to whom a matter has
    been referred under rule 153. ([arbitre]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[Registry]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Registry]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, SOR/2004-283, s.
    3\]]{.Repealed}

[[seasonal recess]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[seasonal recess]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the period beginning
    on December 21 in a year and ending on January 7 in the following
    year. ([vacances judiciaires saisonnières]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[sheriff]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[sheriff]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a marshal, peace officer or
    other person to whom a writ, warrant or other process is directed
    and, in the Province of Quebec, a member of the Ordre professionnel
    des huissiers de justice du Québec. ([shérif]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[simplified action]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[simplified action]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an action referred
    to in rule 292. ([action simplifiée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[solicitor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[solicitor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person referred to in
    subsection 11(3) of the Act. ([avocat]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[solicitor of record]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[solicitor of record]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a solicitor of
    record as described in rule 123 or 124. ([avocat inscrit au
    dossier]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[specially managed proceeding]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[specially managed proceeding]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    proceeding managed in accordance with rules 383 to 385. ([instance à
    gestion spéciale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[statement of claim]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[statement of claim]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a document by which
    an action is commenced. ([déclaration]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[summer recess]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[summer recess]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the months of July and
    August in each year. ([vacances judiciaires d'été]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[swear]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[swear]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of an oath, includes
    affirm. ([Version anglaise seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

[[third party]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[third party]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a fourth or subsequent
    party. ([tierce partie]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[tribunal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[tribunal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as [[federal
    board, commission or other tribunal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in the
    Act. ([Version anglaise seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

[[unincorporated association]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[unincorporated association]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an
    organization of two or more persons, other than a partnership, that
    operates under a common name for a common purpose or
    undertaking. ([association sans personnalité
    morale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[writ of execution]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[writ of execution]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a writ of seizure
    and sale, a writ of possession, a writ of delivery and a writ of
    sequestration, and any further writ in aid thereof. ([bref
    d'exécution]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 8, s. 182
- SOR/2002-417, s. 1
- SOR/2004-283, s. 3
- SOR/2007-301, s. 1
- SOR/2015-21, s. 1

  role="button"}

  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-2-20150130.html "Link to previous version of section 2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General principle

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** These Rules shall be
interpreted and applied

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} so as to secure the just, most expeditious and least
  expensive outcome of every proceeding; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} with consideration being given to the principle of
  proportionality, including consideration of the proceeding's
  complexity, the importance of the issues involved and the amount in
  dispute.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 2](#1331679-1336672){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331679-1336672.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-3-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 3"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Matters not provided for

**[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** On motion, the Court may
provide for any procedural matter not provided for in these Rules or in
an Act of Parliament by analogy to these Rules or by reference to the
practice of the superior court of the province to which the
subject-matter of the proceeding most closely relates.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Forms

**[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** Where these Rules require
that a form be used, the form may incorporate any variations that the
circumstances require.

### [Computation, Extension and Abridgement of Time]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1012756 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Interpretation Act](/eng/acts/I-21)

- **[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject
  to subsections (2) and (3), the computation of time under these Rules,
  or under an order of the Court, is governed by sections 26 to 30 of
  the [Interpretation Act](/eng/acts/I-21).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period of less than seven days

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a period of less than seven days is provided
  for in these Rules or fixed by an order of the Court, a day that is a
  holiday shall not be included in computing the period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seasonal recess

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise directed by the Court, a day that
  falls within the seasonal recess shall not be included in the
  computation of time for filing, amending, transmitting or serving a
  document.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 3](#1331681-1336676){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331681-1336676.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-6-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 6"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension by consent

- **[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject
  to subsections (2) and (3), a period provided by these Rules may be
  extended once by filing the consent in writing of all parties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An extension of a period under subsection (1) shall
  not exceed one half of the period sought to be extended.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No extension may be made on consent of the parties in
  respect of a period fixed by an order of the Court or under subsection
  203(1), 304(1) or 339(1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension or abridgement

- **[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  motion, the Court may extend or abridge a period provided by these
  Rules or fixed by an order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When motion may be brought

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A motion for an extension of time may be brought
  before or after the end of the period sought to be extended.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2021-244, s. 4\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 32
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 4](#1331683-1336678){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331683-1336678.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 8"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 2]{.HLabel1}[Administration of the Court]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1012780 .Part}

### [Officers of the Court]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1012781 .Subheading}

**[[9 to 11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9_to_11 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2004-283, s. 4\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court registrars

- **[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Administrator shall arrange that there be in attendance at every
  sitting of the Court a duly qualified person to act as court registrar
  for the sitting, who shall, subject to the direction of the Court,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} make all arrangements necessary to conduct the
    sitting in an orderly, efficient and dignified manner;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} keep a record of every material event that
    transpires during the sitting;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} keep and be responsible for all books and records
    of the Court used at the sitting; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} keep and be responsible for all exhibits filed
    during the sitting and mark them, record them and indicate by whom
    they were filed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other officers of the Court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Administrator shall arrange for the attendance at
  every sitting of the Court of all persons who are necessary for the
  proper conduct of the Court at the sitting.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 2
:::

### [Court Seals]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1012794 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court seals

- **[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  seal of each court --- the Federal Court of Appeal and the Federal
  Court --- shall be approved by its Chief Justice and shall be kept in
  the principal office.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Facsimiles of seal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Justice of each court may authorize one or
  more facsimiles of the seal of their court to be kept at the Registry.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 5
:::

### [Registry]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1012802 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registry functions

**[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** Registry functions may be
performed by an officer of the Registry at any place and at any time.

**[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, SOR/2013-18,
s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 15"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recommendation boxes

**[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** To provide the public
with an opportunity to make comments regarding the administration or
rules of the Court, two small locked boxes --- one for the Federal Court
of Appeal and one for the Federal Court --- shall be maintained in every
office of the Registry, located and constructed so that members of the
public can conveniently insert envelopes into them and identified by a
sign indicating

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} "RECOMMENDATIONS FOR THE FEDERAL COURT OF APPEAL" or
  "RECOMMENDATIONS FOR THE FEDERAL COURT", as the case may be;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} that recommendations are invited for amendments to
  the Federal Courts Rules and improvements in the administration of the
  Court; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} that every recommendation will be sent unopened
  directly to the Chief Justice of the court in question.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 6
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Principal office and local offices

**[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** The principal office is
located in Ottawa and local offices are located in Calgary,
Charlottetown, Edmonton, Fredericton, Halifax, Iqaluit, Montreal, Quebec
City, Regina, Saint John, Saskatoon, St. John's, Toronto, Vancouver,
Whitehorse, Winnipeg and Yellowknife.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-219, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 17"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requests and requisitions

**[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** A request or requisition
to the Administrator under these Rules shall be made in Form 18.

### [Fees]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1012819 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registry fees

**[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** A party shall pay to the
Registry for a service or procedure set out in Tariff A the fees set out
in that Tariff.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sheriff's fees

- **[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a sheriff
  is entitled to the fees for service and disbursements set out in
  Tariff A.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Modification by Court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On motion, the Court may increase or decrease the fee
  payable to a sheriff on execution.

### [Court Files]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1012827 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2021-151, s. 1(E)

\]
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Records

**[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** The Administrator shall
keep all records necessary for documenting the proceedings of the Court
and enter in them all orders, directions, foreign judgments ordered to
be registered, pleadings and other documents filed in a proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-21-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 21"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Caveat register

- **[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Administrator shall keep in the Registry a caveat register, in which
  all caveats, withdrawals of caveats and orders affecting caveats shall
  be entered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entry of caveat

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On the filing of a caveat under subsection
  493(1), (2) or (3), the Administrator shall enter the caveat in the
  caveat register.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court file

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  each proceeding of the Court, the Administrator shall keep a file that
  is composed of the following documents, each marked with its date and
  time of filing, and that is organized by order of filing:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} every document filed under these Rules, an order of
    the Court or an Act of Parliament, other than affidavits or other
    material filed in support of a motion or as evidence at trial;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all correspondence between a party and the
    Registry;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} all orders;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} copies of all writs issued in the proceeding; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} such other documents relating to the proceeding as
    the Court may direct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annexes

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Administrator shall keep an annex to each Court
  file that is comprised of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} all affidavits;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all exhibits; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} all other documents and material in the possession
    of the Court or the Registry that are not required by these Rules to
    be kept in the Court file.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-23-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 23"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention period

**[[23.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23.1 .sectionLabel}** The Administrator
shall retain all files, annexes --- other than the exhibits --- and
records that are required by these Rules to be kept for the period of
time specified in the retention schedule established by the Court.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Files for notices of motion

- **[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where,
  in respect of an action, application or appeal that has not yet been
  commenced, a notice of motion for an extension of time, for leave to
  appeal or for any other order under a statute, rule or other enactment
  is filed, the notice of motion, any affidavits filed in respect
  thereof and any order made pursuant to the motion shall be kept in
  Court files maintained for notices of motion of that category.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies on file or annex

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the proceeding is subsequently commenced, a copy
  of the order and of the other documents related to the motion shall be
  added to the Court file or annex for the proceeding, as applicable.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-24-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 24"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transmitting paper copies filed at local
office

**[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** When a document is filed
in paper copy at a local office, the Administrator shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} transmit that paper copy, without delay, to the
  principal office;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} keep a certified copy of the document at the local
  office; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} transmit a copy of the document to any other local
  office where a copy is required for the business of the Court.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-25-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 25"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection of files

- **[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  necessary facilities are available, a person may, with supervision and
  without interfering with the business of the Court, inspect a Court
  file or annex that is available to the public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal or deletion of documents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Nothing shall be removed or deleted from a Court file
  or annex except

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} under an order of the Court;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by an officer of the Registry acting in the course
    of his or her duties; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with rule 26.1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal of files

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise ordered by the Court, no Court file
  or annex to a Court file shall be removed from the Registry by any
  person other than

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a judge, prothonotary or referee; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an officer of the Registry acting in the course of
    his or her duties.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 3
- SOR/2015-21, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-26-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 26"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition

- **[[26.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this rule, [[appeal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes an appeal of an
  order of a prothonotary, an application for leave to appeal and an
  appeal to the Supreme Court of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal of exhibits from file

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), exhibits put into evidence
  shall remain part of the annex to the Court file either

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} until the time for an appeal has expired, if no
    appeal has been taken, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} until the appeal is disposed of, if an appeal has
    been taken.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of exhibits

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On the expiry of the time for appeal or on the
  disposition of the appeal, the Administrator shall return the exhibits
  to the respective solicitors or the parties who put the exhibits in
  evidence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return on consent

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} At any time following judgment, on requisition by the
  solicitor or party who put an exhibit in evidence or the person who
  produced it and on the filing of the consent of all parties, the
  Administrator shall return the exhibit to the person making the
  requisition.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 4
- SOR/2015-21, s. 8
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-26.1-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 26.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Unclaimed Exhibits]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1012897 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions

- **[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  exhibits are not returnable to a party, solicitor or person or have
  not been claimed by a party, solicitor or person within one year after
  the expiry of the time for appeal or the disposition of the appeal
  referred to in subsection 26.1(3), the Administrator may seek
  directions from the Court as to their disposition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposal of exhibits

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Court may, at the request of the Administrator,
  order that any exhibits not claimed by, or returnable to, a party,
  solicitor or other person be vested in Her Majesty in right of Canada
  or be destroyed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 5
:::

### [Hearings]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1012905 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sitting of Court

**[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** The Court may sit at any
time and at any place.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public hearings

- **[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2) and rule
  30, hearings of the Court, other than pre-trial or dispute resolution
  conferences, shall be open and accessible to the public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing *in camera*

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On motion, the Court may direct that all or part of a
  proceeding be heard *in camera* if it is satisfied that the hearing
  should not be open to the public.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders out of court

- **[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  judge or prothonotary who is not sitting in court may make an order on
  a motion if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the judge or prothonotary is satisfied that all
    parties affected have consented thereto;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the motion was brought in accordance with rule 369
    or 369.2; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for any other reason the judge or prothonotary
    considers that the order can be made without a hearing without
    prejudice to any party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On motion, the Court may set aside or vary an order
  made under paragraph (1)(a) on the ground that a party did not consent
  to it.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 5](#1331684-1336681){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331684-1336681.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-30-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 30"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpreter

**[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** A request by a party
under the [Official Languages Act](/eng/acts/O-3.01) for an interpreter
at a hearing shall be made in writing and be sent to the Administrator
as soon as is practicable before the hearing begins.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remote conferencing

**[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** The Court may order that
a hearing be conducted in whole or in part by means of a telephone
conference call, video-conference or any other form of electronic
communication.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Technological assistance

**[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** The Court may give
directions to facilitate the conduct of a hearing by the use of any
electronic or digital means of communication or storage or retrieval of
information, or any other technology it considers appropriate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General Sittings of Trial Division

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} General Sittings of the Federal
  Court for the hearing of motions shall be held, except during the
  seasonal or summer recess or on a holiday,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at Ottawa, every Wednesday and on any other day
    fixed by the Chief Justice of the Federal Court;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at Toronto and Vancouver, every Tuesday and on any
    other day fixed by the Chief Justice of the Federal Court;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in Quebec,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at Montreal, every Tuesday and on any other day
      fixed by the Chief Justice of the Federal Court, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on a day and at any other place fixed by the
      Chief Justice of the Federal Court; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in every province other than Ontario, Quebec and
    British Columbia, at least once per month, on a day and at a place
    fixed by the Chief Justice of the Federal Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} General Sittings may be cancelled by the Chief
  Justice of the Federal Court if no notice of a motion to be presented
  at the Sittings has been filed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in Ottawa, Montreal, Toronto or Vancouver, at least
    two days before the day of the Sittings; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other place, at least one week before the
    day of the Sittings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Summer recess

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} General Sittings during the summer recess will be
  announced by the Chief Justice of the Federal Court before June 15
  each year.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, ss. 33, 34
- SOR/2013-18, s. 2
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 6](#1331686-1336683){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331686-1336683.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-34-20130808.html "Link to previous version of section 34"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing dates

- **[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to rule 298 and paragraph
  385(1)(b), motions that can conveniently be heard at the General
  Sittings of the Federal Court may be made returnable accordingly.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special hearing dates

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A request may be made informally to the Judicial
  Administrator of the Federal Court of Appeal or the Federal Court, as
  the case may be, for an appointment of a special time and place

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for sittings of the Federal Court of Appeal or of a
    judge of that court to hear a motion; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for sittings of a judge of the Federal Court or of
    a prothonotary to hear a motion that is likely to exceed two hours
    or a motion that is to be heard other than at General Sittings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion record

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The request shall be accompanied by a motion record.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, ss. 7, 33
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 2](#1303062-1305139){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303062-1305139.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-35-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 35"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjournment

- **[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  hearing may be adjourned by the Court from time to time on such terms
  as the Court considers just.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjournment to fixed day

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a hearing is adjourned to a fixed day, a party
  who appeared at the hearing is deemed to have had notice of the
  adjournment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice dispensed with

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a party has failed to appear at a hearing, that
  party need not be served with notice of an adjournment of the hearing.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to give notice

**[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** Where at a hearing the
Court considers that any person to whom notice of the hearing has not
been given ought to have had such notice, the Court may adjourn the
hearing or dismiss the proceeding or motion.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence of party

**[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** Where a party fails to
appear at a hearing, the Court may proceed in the absence of the party
if the Court is satisfied that notice of the hearing was given to that
party in accordance with these Rules.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inability to continue

**[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** If a judge or referee of
the Federal Court of Appeal or a Judge, prothonotary or referee of the
Federal Court is for any reason unable to continue in a proceeding or to
render a judgment that has been reserved, the Chief Justice of the court
in question may order that the proceeding be reheard or retried, on any
terms that the Chief Justice considers just.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rota of Judges for Vancouver

- **[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On or
  before July 1 in each year, the Chief Justice of the Federal Court
  shall, in consultation with the other judges of that court, establish
  a rota of judges for Vancouver for the twelve months commencing on
  September 1 of that year, excluding the seasonal recess.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Chief Justice of the Federal
  Court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Justice of the Federal Court may make
  changes to the Vancouver rota, including the substitution of one judge
  for another during all or part of the judge's period of assignment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibilities of judges

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A judge assigned to Vancouver shall reside in
  Vancouver for the period of the assignment and hold sittings and
  otherwise transact the judicial business of the Federal Court in
  Vancouver and in such other places as may be required.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assignment period

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Except with a judge's consent, the Chief Justice of
  the Federal Court shall not

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} assign the judge to Vancouver for a period
    exceeding two months; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reassign the judge to Vancouver for a second
    assignment within two months after the end of the first.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, ss. 9, 33, 34
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 7](#1331688-1336685){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331688-1336685.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-40-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 40"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Summoning of Witnesses or Other Persons]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1012981 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Subpoena]{.otherLang lang="la"} for
witness

- **[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), on
  receipt of a written request, the Administrator shall issue, in Form
  41, a [subpoena]{.otherLang lang="la"} for the attendance of a witness
  or the production of a document or other material in a proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance in blank

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A [subpoena]{.otherLang lang="la"} may be issued in
  blank and completed by a solicitor or party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multiple names

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any number of names may be included in one
  [subpoena]{.otherLang lang="la"}.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where leave required

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No [subpoena]{.otherLang lang="la"} shall be issued
  without leave of the Court

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the production of an original record or of an
    original document, if the record or document may be proven by a copy
    in accordance with an Act of Parliament or of the legislature of a
    province;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to compel the appearance of a witness who resides
    more than 800 km from the place where the witness will be required
    to attend under the [subpoena]{.otherLang lang="la"}; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to compel the attendance of a witness at a hearing
    other than a trial or a reference under rule 153.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} motion

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Leave may be granted under subsection (4) on an [ex
  parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} motion.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal service of [subpoena]{.otherLang
lang="la"}

**[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** No witness is required to
attend under a [subpoena]{.otherLang lang="la"} unless the
[subpoena]{.otherLang lang="la"} has been personally served on the
witness in accordance with paragraph 128(1)(a) and witness fees and
travel expenses have been paid or tendered to the witness in the amount
set out in Tariff A.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 6
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Witness fees

**[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43 .sectionLabel}** Where a witness is
required under these Rules to attend a proceeding other than pursuant to
a [subpoena]{.otherLang lang="la"}, the witness is entitled to witness
fees and travel expenses in the amount set out in Tariff A.

**[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2002-417, s. 7\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compelling attendance of detainee

**[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** On motion, the Court may
make an order in Form 45 requiring that any person who is in the custody
of a prison or penitentiary be brought before the Court.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to obey

**[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** Where a witness who is
required to attend at a hearing fails to do so, on motion, the Court
may, by a warrant in Form 46, order that the witness be apprehended
anywhere in Canada, brought before the Court and

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} detained in custody until the witness's presence is
  no longer required; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} released on a recognizance, with or without sureties,
  on condition that the witness attend to give evidence.

## [PART 3]{.HLabel1}[Rules Applicable to All Proceedings]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1013009 .Part}

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1013010 .Subheading}

#### [Powers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013011 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discretionary powers

- **[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless
  otherwise provided by these Rules, if these Rules grant a
  discretionary power to the Court, a judge or prothonotary has
  jurisdiction to exercise that power on his or her own initiative or on
  motion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exercise of powers on motion

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where these Rules provide that powers of the Court
  are to be exercised on motion, they may be exercised only on the
  bringing of a motion.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-130, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-47-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 47"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2004-283, s. 10\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfer of proceedings

**[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** If a proceeding has been
commenced in the Federal Court of Appeal or the Federal Court, a judge
of that court may order that the proceeding be transferred to the other
court.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 10
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prothonotaries

- **[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  prothonotary may hear, and make any necessary orders relating to, any
  motion under these Rules other than a motion

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of which these Rules or an Act of
    Parliament has expressly conferred jurisdiction on a judge;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the Federal Court of Appeal;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for summary judgment or summary trial other than

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in an action referred to in subsection (2), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a claim referred to in subsection
      (3);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to hold a person in contempt at a hearing referred
    to in paragraph 467(1)(a);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} for an injunction;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} relating to the liberty of a person;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} to stay, set aside or vary an order of a judge,
    other than an order made under paragraph 385(a), (b) or (c);

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} to stay execution of an order of a judge;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to appoint a receiver;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} for an interim order under section 18.2 of the Act;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} to appeal the findings of a referee under rule 163;
    or

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} for the certification of an action or an
    application as a class proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Actions not over \$100,000

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A prothonotary may hear an action exclusively for
  monetary relief, or an action [in rem]{.otherLang lang="la"} claiming
  monetary relief, in which no amount claimed by a party exceeds
  \$100,000 exclusive of interest and costs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class proceedings

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A prothonotary may hear a claim in respect of one or
  more individual questions in a class proceeding in which the amount
  claimed by a class member does not exceed \$100,000 exclusive of
  interest and costs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign judgment or arbitral award

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A prothonotary may hear an application made under
  rule 327 for registration of a foreign judgment or recognition and
  enforcement of an arbitral award.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Matters on consent

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Despite paragraphs (1)(c) and (k), a prothonotary may
  render any final judgment that could be rendered by a judge of the
  Federal Court, except in a proceeding in respect of which an Act of
  Parliament expressly confers jurisdiction on a judge, if the
  prothonotary is satisfied that all of the parties that will be
  affected by the judgment have given their consent.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 8
- SOR/2004-283, s. 32
- SOR/2007-130, s. 2
- SOR/2007-301, s. 2
- SOR/2009-331, s. 1
- [SOR/2021-150, s. 1](#1302222-1305039){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1302222-1305039.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2021-245, s. 1](#1333989-1336766){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1333989-1336766.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-50-20210617.html "Link to previous version of section 50"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Appeals of Prothonotaries' Orders]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013051 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal

- **[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  order of a prothonotary may be appealed by a motion to a judge of the
  Federal Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notice of the motion shall be served and filed within
  10 days after the day on which the order under appeal was made and at
  least four days before the day fixed for the hearing of the motion.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 33
- SOR/2007-130, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-51-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 51"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Assessors]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013059 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Role of assessor

- **[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Court may call on an assessor

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to assist the Court in understanding technical
    evidence; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to provide a written opinion in a proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees and disbursements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (1) shall provide for
  payment of the fees and disbursements of the assessor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Communications with assessor

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} All communications between the Court and an assessor
  shall be in open court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form and content of question

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Before requesting a written opinion from an assessor,
  the Court shall allow the parties to make submissions in respect of
  the form and content of the question to be asked.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Answer by assessor

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Before judgment is rendered, the Court shall provide
  the parties with the questions asked of, and any opinion given by, an
  assessor and give them an opportunity to make submissions thereon.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2010-176, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-176, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-52-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 52"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Expert Witnesses]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013076 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to name expert

- **[[52.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party to a proceeding may name an expert witness whether or not an
  assessor has been called on under rule 52.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expert named jointly

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Two or more of the parties may jointly name an expert
  witness.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-176, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expert's affidavit or statement

- **[[52.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  affidavit or statement of an expert witness shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} set out in full the proposed evidence of the
    expert;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} set out the expert's qualifications and the areas
    in respect of which it is proposed that he or she be qualified as an
    expert;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be accompanied by a certificate in Form 52.2 signed
    by the expert acknowledging that the expert has read the Code of
    Conduct for Expert Witnesses set out in the schedule and agrees to
    be bound by it; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a statement, be in writing, signed
    by the expert and accompanied by a solicitor's certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to comply

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an expert fails to comply with the Code of Conduct
  for Expert Witnesses, the Court may exclude some or all of the
  expert's affidavit or statement.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-176, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception for certain medical
professionals

**[[52.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52.3 .sectionLabel}** The rules governing
expert witnesses do not apply to a medical professional who has given or
is giving medical treatment or advice to a person if the evidence in
relation to the person is limited to one or more of the following
subjects:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the results of an examination;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the treatment or advice;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the reason the treatment or advice was or is being
  given; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the results of the treatment or advice.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-176, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limit on number of experts

- **[[52.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52.4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party intending to call more than five expert witnesses in a
  proceeding shall seek leave of the Court in accordance with section 7
  of the [Canada Evidence Act](/eng/acts/C-5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave considerations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In deciding whether to grant leave, the Court shall
  consider all relevant matters, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the litigation, its public
    significance and any need to clarify the law;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the number, complexity or technical nature of the
    issues in dispute; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the likely expense involved in calling the expert
    witnesses in relation to the amount in dispute in the proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-176, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objection to expert

- **[[52.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52.5 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party to a proceeding shall, as early as possible in the proceeding,
  raise any objection to an opposing party's proposed expert witness
  that could disqualify the witness from testifying.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner of raising objection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An objection may be raised

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by serving and filing a document containing the
    particulars of and basis for the objection; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with subsection 262(2) or
    subparagraph 263(c)(i) if, in the case of an action, the objection
    is known prior to the pre-trial conference.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-176, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expert conference

- **[[52.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Court may order expert witnesses
  to confer with one another in advance of the hearing of the proceeding
  in order to narrow the issues and identify the points on which their
  views differ.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presence of parties and counsel

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not preclude the parties and
  their counsel from attending an expert conference but the conference
  may take place in their absence if the parties agree.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presence of judge or prothonotary

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Court may order that an expert conference take
  place in the presence of a judge or prothonotary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Joint statement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A joint statement prepared by the expert witnesses
  following an expert conference is admissible at the hearing of the
  proceeding . Discussions in an expert conference and documents
  prepared for the purposes of a conference are confidential and shall
  not be disclosed to the judge or prothonotary presiding at the hearing
  of the proceeding unless the parties consent.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-176, s. 2
:::

#### [Orders and Directions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013133 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders on terms

- **[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  making an order under these Rules, the Court may impose such
  conditions and give such directions as it considers just.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other orders

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where these Rules provide that the Court may make an
  order of a specified nature, the Court may make any other order that
  it considers just.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion for directions

**[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** A person may at any time
bring a motion for directions concerning the procedure to be followed
under these Rules.

#### [Varying Rules and Dispensing with Compliance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013141 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Varying rule and dispensing with
compliance

**[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** In special circumstances,
in a proceeding, the Court may vary a rule or dispense with compliance
with a rule.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 11
:::

#### [Failure to Comply with Rules]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013146 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of non-compliance

**[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56 .sectionLabel}** Non-compliance with any
of these Rules does not render a proceeding, a step in a proceeding or
an order void, but instead constitutes an irregularity, which may be
addressed under rules 58 to 60.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Wrong originating document

**[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57 .sectionLabel}** An originating document
shall not be set aside only on the ground that a different originating
document should have been used.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion to attack irregularity

- **[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party may by motion challenge any step taken by another party for
  non-compliance with these Rules.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When motion to be brought

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A motion under subsection (1) shall be brought as
  soon as practicable after the moving party obtains knowledge of the
  irregularity.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders on motion

**[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59 .sectionLabel}** Subject to rule 57,
where, on a motion brought under rule 58, the Court finds that a party
has not complied with these Rules, the Court may, by order,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} dismiss the motion, where the motion was not brought
  within a sufficient time after the moving party became aware of the
  irregularity to avoid prejudice to the respondent in the motion;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} grant any amendments required to address the
  irregularity; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} set aside the proceeding, in whole or in part.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-compliance with Rules or gap in case

**[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** At any time before
judgment is given in a proceeding, the Court may draw the attention of a
party to any gap in the proof of its case or to any non-compliance with
these Rules and permit the party to remedy it on such conditions as the
Court considers just.

### [Commencement of Proceedings]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1013163 .Subheading}

#### [Manner of Bringing Proceeding]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013164 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Actions

- **[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), a
  proceeding referred to in rule 169 shall be brought as an action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applications

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), a proceeding referred to
  in rule 300 shall be brought as an application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeals

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A proceeding referred to in rule 335 shall be brought
  as an appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where by or under an Act of Parliament a person is
  given the option of bringing a proceeding referred to in rule 169 or
  300 as either an action or an application, the person may commence the
  proceeding as an action or as an application.

#### [Originating documents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013174 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commencement of proceedings

- **[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), all
  actions, applications or appeals shall be commenced by the issuance of
  an originating document.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A counterclaim or third party claim in an action
  brought only against persons who are already parties to the action
  shall be commenced by the service and filing of the counterclaim or
  third party claim.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Types of originating documents

- **[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless
  otherwise provided by or under an Act of Parliament, the originating
  document for the commencement of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an action, including an appeal by way of an action,
    is a statement of claim;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a counterclaim against a person who is not yet a
    party to the action is a statement of defence and counterclaim;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a third party claim against a person who is not yet
    a party to the action is a third party claim;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an application is a notice of application; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an appeal is a notice of appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other originating documents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where by or under an Act of Parliament a proceeding
  is to be commenced by way of a document different from the originating
  document required under these Rules, the rules applicable to the
  originating document apply in respect of that document.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaratory relief available

**[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** No proceeding is subject
to challenge on the ground that only a declaratory order is sought, and
the Court may make a binding declaration of right in a proceeding
whether or not any consequential relief is or can be claimed.

### [Court Documents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1013192 .Subheading}

#### [Form]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013193 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Format of printed documents

**[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** A printed document that
is prepared for use in a proceeding shall be legible, the print ---
including all references in the document --- shall be in 12-point Times
New Roman, Arial or Tahoma font and each page of the document shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} be on good quality white or off-white paper,
  measuring 21.5 cm by 28 cm (8½ in. by 11 in.);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} have top and bottom margins of not less than 2.5 cm
  and left and right margins of not less than 3.5 cm;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} be printed on one side of the paper only, unless the
  document is a book of authorities; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} have no more than 30 lines, exclusive of headings.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 12
- SOR/2013-18, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-65-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 65"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Heading

- **[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  first page of a document prepared for use in a proceeding shall have a
  heading in Form 66 that sets out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the court and the Court file number;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the style of cause in accordance with rule 67.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of document

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A document prepared for use in a proceeding shall
  contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the title of the document;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} its date;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name, address, telephone number and, if any,
    fax number of the solicitor or party who is filing the document;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} the party's address for service; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if there is consent to the electronic service of
    documents, the electronic address set out in Form 141A.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Signature

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A document prepared for use in a proceeding shall be
  signed by the solicitor or party filing it.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 36
- SOR/2015-21, s. 9
- [SOR/2021-246, s. 2](#1333948-1337132){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1333948-1337132.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66-20150130.html "Link to previous version of section 66"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Style of cause in originating document

- **[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  originating document shall contain a style of cause that sets out the
  names of all parties and the capacity of any party that is not acting
  in its personal capacity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Style of cause in action

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The style of cause in an action shall name each party
  commencing the action as a plaintiff and each adverse party as a
  defendant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Style of cause in application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The style of cause in an application shall name each
  party commencing the application as an applicant and each adverse
  party as a respondent and state any legislative provision or rule
  under which the application is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Style of cause in appeal

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The style of cause in an appeal shall name each party
  bringing the appeal as an appellant and each adverse party as a
  respondent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short style of cause

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In a document other than an originating document or
  order, where there are more than two parties to the proceeding, a
  short style of cause may be used, showing the names of the first party
  on each side followed by the expression "and others".

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motions prior to commencement of
  proceeding

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) to (4) apply, with such modifications
  as are required, to a motion brought prior to the commencement of an
  action, application or appeal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Language of documents

- **[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), all
  documents required under these Rules to be filed in a proceeding shall
  be in English or French or be accompanied by a translation in English
  or French and an affidavit attesting to the accuracy of the
  translation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Language of pleadings, etc.

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} All pleadings, memoranda of fact and law and written
  representations on motions shall be in English or French.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of constitutional question

**[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** A notice of a
constitutional question referred to in section 57 of the Act shall be in
Form 69.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Memorandum of fact and law

- **[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  memorandum of fact and law shall contain, in consecutively numbered
  paragraphs,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a concise statement of fact, as Part I of the
    memorandum;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a statement of the points in issue, as Part II of
    the memorandum;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a concise statement of submissions, as Part III of
    the memorandum;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a concise statement of the order sought, including
    any order concerning costs, as Part IV of the memorandum;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a list of the authorities to be referred to, as
    Part V of the memorandum;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in a proceeding other than an appeal, the
    provisions of any statutes or regulations cited or relied on that
    have not been reproduced in another party's memorandum, as Appendix
    A to the memorandum; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} in a proceeding other than an appeal, a book of the
    authorities to be referred to that have not been included in another
    party's book of authorities, as Appendix B to the memorandum.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Enactments in both official languages

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Extracts of federal statutes and regulations in
  Appendix A to a memorandum of fact and law shall be reproduced in both
  official languages.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Book of authorities

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} In respect of reasons for judgment, the book of
  authorities shall contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case where the book is filed in paper copy
    and the reasons are available from an electronic database that is
    accessible to the public at no charge, the relevant extracts of the
    reasons --- including the head note, if any, and the paragraphs
    immediately preceding and following the extracts --- with a
    reference to the database clearly marked on the page containing the
    extract; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the reasons for judgment in full
    with the relevant extracts clearly marked.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appendices

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a memorandum of fact and law is filed in paper
  copy, the appendices may be bound separately from the memorandum.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Length

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise ordered by the Court, a memorandum
  of fact and law, exclusive of Part V and appendices, shall not exceed
  30 pages in length.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- combined memorandum

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (4), if a respondent serves and
  files a single memorandum of fact and law as appellant by cross-appeal
  as part of the respondent's memorandum of fact and law, it shall not
  exceed 60 pages in length exclusive of Part V and appendices.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 9
- SOR/2015-21, s. 10
- [SOR/2021-150, s. 2](#1302224-1305044){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1302224-1305044.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 3](#1303067-1305142){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303067-1305142.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-70-20150130.html "Link to previous version of section 70"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Filing of Documents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013255 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sending documents for filing

- **[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  document may be sent to the Registry for the purpose of filing by
  delivery, mail, fax or electronic transmission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sending by fax --- prior consent
  required

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Administrator's consent is required before any of
  the following documents may be sent by fax:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a motion record, application record, trial record,
    appeal book or book of authorities; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other document that is longer than 20 pages.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fax cover page

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A document that is sent by fax shall include a cover
  page that sets out the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name, address and telephone number of the
    sender;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the date and time of the transmission;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the total number of pages being transmitted,
    including the cover page;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a fax number at which the sender may receive
    documents; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name and telephone number of the person that is
    to be contacted in the event of a transmission problem.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sending by electronic transmission ---
  document format

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A document that is sent by electronic transmission
  shall be in PDF (Portable Document Format) or any other format that is
  approved by the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Originating documents sent by
  electronic transmission

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A person who sends an originating document by
  electronic transmission shall provide the Registry with paper copies
  for issuance or arrange for the Registry to prepare those copies.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-71-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 71"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Document submitted for filing

- **[[71.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  document that was sent to the Registry in accordance with rule 71 is
  submitted for filing when

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is received and dated by the Registry; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if a fee for its issuance or filing is payable
    under Tariff A, the fee is paid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time of receipt --- electronic
  transmission

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a document that was sent by electronic
  transmission to the Registry for filing, the time of its receipt by
  the Registry is that time in the Eastern time zone.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission on holiday

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A document that is submitted for filing on a holiday
  is deemed to have been submitted for filing on the next day that is
  not a holiday.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Irregular documents

- **[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a document is submitted for filing, the Administrator shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} accept the document for filing; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the Administrator is of the opinion that the
    document is not in the form required by these Rules or that other
    conditions precedent to its filing have not been fulfilled, refer
    the document without delay to a judge or prothonotary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acceptance, rejection or conditional
  filing

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a document referred under paragraph
  (1)(b), the judge or prothonotary may direct the Administrator to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} accept or reject the document; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} accept the document subject to conditions as to the
    making of any corrections or the fulfilling of any conditions
    precedent.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2015-21, s. 12\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 12
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-72-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 72"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time of filing

**[[72.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72.1 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court
directs otherwise, a document that is accepted for filing is deemed to
have been filed at the time the document was submitted for filing.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 13
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Paper copies --- fax or electronic
transmission

**[[72.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72.2 .sectionLabel}** A person who files a
document by fax or electronic transmission shall, if required by the
Court, provide the Registry with the same number of paper copies of the
document as would have been required had the document been filed in
paper copy.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 13
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention and provision of paper copy

**[[72.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72.3 .sectionLabel}** A person who, by
electronic transmission, files a document that is originally in paper
copy and that bears a signature shall retain the paper copy of the
document for the duration of any appeal and for 30 days after the expiry
of all appeal periods and, if required by the Court, provide that paper
copy to the Registry.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 13
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing --- National Capital Region

- **[[72.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72.4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party that files paper copies of documents under subsection 309(1.1),
  310(1.1), 345(2), 348(1) or 353(1), rule 354 or 355 or subsection
  364(1) or 365(1) may file one fewer copy than set out in those
  provisions if the Court has been notified in writing that all parties
  to the proceeding are based in the National Capital Region and that
  they all agree that the proceeding and all matters related to it will
  proceed and be disposed of in the National Capital Region.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification to Court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The written notification may accompany the documents
  being filed.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 4](#1303069-1305149){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303069-1305149.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of service

**[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** No document required to
be served, other than an originating document, shall be filed without
proof that it has been served within the time and in the manner provided
for by these Rules.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of constitutional question

**[[73.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73.1 .sectionLabel}** A notice of a
constitutional question referred to in section 57 of the Act shall be
filed, along with proof that it has been served in accordance with that
section, without delay after service.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 5](#1303071-1305153){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303071-1305153.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal of documents

- **[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the Court
  may, at any time, order that a document be removed from the Court file
  if the document

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} was not filed in accordance with these Rules, an
    order of the Court or an Act of Parliament;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is scandalous, frivolous, vexatious or clearly
    unfounded; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is otherwise an abuse of the process of the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Opportunity to make submissions
