  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Court may only make an order under subsection (1)
  if all interested parties have been given an opportunity to make
  submissions.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 8](#1331690-1336695){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331690-1336695.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-74-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 74"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Amendments]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013318 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendments with leave

- **[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2) and rule
  76, the Court may, on motion, at any time, allow a party to amend a
  document, on such terms as will protect the rights of all parties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No amendment shall be allowed under subsection (1)
  during or after a hearing unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the purpose is to make the document accord with the
    issues at the hearing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a new hearing is ordered; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the other parties are given an opportunity for any
    preparation necessary to meet any new or amended allegations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave to amend

**[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** With leave of the Court,
an amendment may be made

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to correct the name of a party, if the Court is
  satisfied that the mistake sought to be corrected was not such as to
  cause a reasonable doubt as to the identity of the party, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to alter the capacity in which a party is bringing a
  proceeding, if the party could have commenced the proceeding in its
  altered capacity at the date of commencement of the proceeding,

unless to do so would result in prejudice to a party that would not be
compensable by costs or an adjournment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment after expiration of limitation
period

**[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** The Court may allow an
amendment under rule 76 notwithstanding the expiration of a relevant
period of limitation that had not expired at the date of commencement of
the proceeding.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of amendment

**[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court orders
otherwise, where these Rules provide for doing an act or taking a step
in a proceeding within a prescribed period after the service or filing
of a document and that document is subsequently amended in accordance
with these Rules, the period shall be calculated from the day of service
or filing of the amended document, as the case may be.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner of amending

- **[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  filed document may be amended

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the amendment does not require the insertion of
    more than 10 words in any one page, by writing the amendment on the
    document, serving a copy of the amended document on all other
    parties and filing proof of its service; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, by serving and filing an amended
    document in which the amendments are underlined and filing proof of
    its service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An amendment made under subsection (1) shall indicate
  the rule or Court order under which the amendment is made and the date
  on which it is made.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 6](#1303073-1305162){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303073-1305162.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-79-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 79"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Affidavit Evidence and Examinations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1013343 .Subheading}

#### [Affidavits]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013344 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form of affidavits

- **[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Affidavits shall be drawn in the
  first person, in Form 80A.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Affidavit by blind or illiterate person

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an affidavit is made by a deponent who is blind
  or illiterate, the person before whom the affidavit is sworn shall
  certify that the affidavit was read to the deponent and that the
  deponent appeared to understand it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Affidavit by deponent who does not
  understand an official language

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Where an affidavit is written in an official
  language for a deponent who does not understand that official
  language, the affidavit shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be translated orally for the deponent in the
    language of the deponent by a competent and independent interpreter
    who has taken an oath, in Form 80B, as to the performance of his or
    her duties; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} contain a jurat in Form 80C.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exhibits

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where an affidavit refers to an exhibit, the exhibit
  shall be accurately identified by an endorsement on the exhibit or on
  a certificate attached to it, signed by the person before whom the
  affidavit is sworn.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 10
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of affidavits

- **[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Affidavits shall be confined to
  facts within the deponent's personal knowledge except on motions,
  other than motions for summary judgment or summary trial, in which
  statements as to the deponent's belief, with the grounds for it, may
  be included.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Affidavits on belief

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an affidavit is made on belief, an adverse
  inference may be drawn from the failure of a party to provide evidence
  of persons having personal knowledge of material facts.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-331, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-81-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 81"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of solicitor's affidavit

**[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** Except with leave of the
Court, a solicitor shall not both depose to an affidavit and present
argument to the Court based on that affidavit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cross-examination on affidavits

**[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** A party to a motion or
application may cross-examine the deponent of an affidavit served by an
adverse party to the motion or application.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When cross-examination may be made

- **[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party seeking to cross-examine the deponent of an affidavit filed in a
  motion or application shall not do so until the party has served on
  all other parties every affidavit on which the party intends to rely
  in the motion or application, except with the consent of all other
  parties or with leave of the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing of affidavit after
  cross-examination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party who has cross-examined the deponent of an
  affidavit filed in a motion or application may not subsequently file
  an affidavit in that motion or application, except with the consent of
  all other parties or with leave of the Court.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Due diligence

**[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** A party who intends to
cross-examine the deponent of an affidavit shall do so with due
diligence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transcript of cross-examination on
affidavit

**[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court orders
otherwise, a party who conducts a cross-examination on an affidavit
shall order and pay for a transcript thereof and send a copy to each
other party.

#### [Examinations out of Court]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013378 .Subheading}

##### [General]{.HTitleText4} {#h-1013379 .Subheading}

Definition of [[examination]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** In rules 88 to 100,
[[examination]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an examination for discovery;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the taking of evidence out of court for use at trial;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a cross-examination on an affidavit; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} an examination in aid of execution.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope or duration of examination

- **[[87.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the Court
  may, on its own initiative or on motion, order that the scope or
  duration of an examination be limited.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Opportunity to be heard

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Court may only make an order under subsection (1)
  on its own initiative if all interested parties have been given an
  opportunity to make submissions.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 9](#1331692-1336702){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331692-1336702.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner of examination

- **[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to rules 234 and 296, an
  examination may be conducted orally or in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electronic communications

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Court may order that an examination out of court
  be recorded by video recording or conducted by video-conference or any
  other form of electronic communication.

##### [Oral Examinations]{.HTitleText4} {#h-1013391 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oral examination

- **[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party requesting an oral examination shall pay the fees and
  disbursements related to recording the examination in accordance with
  Tariff A.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination in Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An oral examination that takes place in Canada shall
  be recorded by a person authorized to record examinations for
  discovery under the practice and procedure of a superior court in
  Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination outside Canada

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An oral examination that takes place in a
  jurisdiction outside Canada shall be recorded by a person authorized
  to record

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} court proceedings in that jurisdiction; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} examinations for discovery under the practice and
    procedure of a superior court in Canada, if the parties consent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination to be recorded

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person who records an oral examination shall record
  it word for word, including any comment made by a solicitor, other
  than statements that the attending parties agree to exclude from the
  record.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Place of oral examination

- **[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a person to be examined on an oral examination resides in Canada and
  the person and the parties cannot agree on where to conduct the oral
  examination, it shall be conducted in the place closest to the
  person's residence where a superior court sits.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person residing outside Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a person to be examined on an oral examination
  resides outside Canada, the time, place, manner and expenses of the
  oral examination shall be as agreed on by the person and the parties
  or, on motion, as ordered by the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Travel expenses

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person is required to attend an oral examination
  unless reasonable travel expenses have been paid or tendered to the
  person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Direction to attend

- **[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party who intends to conduct an oral examination shall serve a
  direction to attend, in Form 91, on the person to be examined and a
  copy thereof on every other party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Production for inspection at
  examination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A direction to attend may direct the person to be
  examined to produce for inspection at the examination

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an examination for discovery, all
    documents and other material in the possession, power or control of
    the party on behalf of whom the person is being examined that are
    relevant to the matters in issue in the action;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of the taking of evidence for use at
    trial, all documents and other material in that person's possession,
    power or control that are relevant to the matters in issue in the
    action;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a cross-examination on an affidavit,
    all documents and other material in that person's possession, power
    or control that are relevant to the application or motion; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an examination in aid of execution,
    all documents and other material in that person's possession, power
    or control that are relevant to the person's ability to satisfy the
    judgment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of direction to attend

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A direction to attend an oral examination shall be
  served

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the person to be examined is an adverse
    party, at least six days before the day of the proposed examination;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the person to be examined is not a party to
    the proceeding, at least 10 days before the day of the proposed
    examination; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the person is to be cross-examined on an
    affidavit filed in support of a motion, at least 24 hours before the
    hearing of the motion.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Swearing

**[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** A person to be examined
on an oral examination shall be sworn before being examined.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examining party to provide interpreter

- **[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a person to be examined on an oral examination understands neither
  French nor English or is deaf or mute, the examining party shall
  arrange for the attendance and pay the fees and disbursements of an
  independent and competent person to accurately interpret everything
  said during the examination, other than statements that the attending
  parties agree to exclude from the record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Administrator to provide interpreter

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an interpreter is required because the
  examining party wishes to conduct an oral examination in one official
  language and the person to be examined wishes to be examined in the
  other official language, on the request of the examining party made at
  least six days before the examination, the Administrator shall arrange
  for the attendance and pay the fees and disbursements of an
  independent and competent interpreter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oath of interpreter

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before aiding in the examination of a witness, an
  interpreter shall take an oath, in Form 93, as to the performance of
  his or her duties.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 3(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-93-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 93"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Production of documents on examination

- **[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a person
  who is to be examined on an oral examination or the party on whose
  behalf that person is being examined shall produce for inspection at
  the examination all documents and other material requested in the
  direction to attend that are within that person's or party's
  possession and control, other than any documents for which privilege
  has been claimed or for which relief from production has been granted
  under rule 230.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Relief from production

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On motion, the Court may order that a person to be
  examined or the party on whose behalf that person is being examined be
  relieved from the requirement to produce for inspection any document
  or other material requested in a direction to attend, if the Court is
  of the opinion that the document or other material requested is
  irrelevant or, by reason of its nature or the number of documents or
  amount of material requested, it would be unduly onerous to require
  the person or party to produce it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objections

- **[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who objects to a question that is asked in an oral examination
  shall briefly state the grounds for the objection for the record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preliminary answer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person may answer a question that was objected to
  in an oral examination subject to the right to have the propriety of
  the question determined, on motion, before the answer is used at
  trial.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Improper conduct

- **[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person being examined may adjourn an oral examination and bring a
  motion for directions if the person believes that he or she is being
  subjected to an excessive number of questions or to improper
  questions, or that the examination is being conducted in bad faith or
  in an abusive manner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjournment to seek directions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person conducting an oral examination may adjourn
  the examination and bring a motion for directions if the person
  believes answers to questions being provided are evasive or if the
  person being examined fails to produce a document or other material
  requested under rule 94.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sanctions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On a motion under subsection (1) or (2), the Court
  may sanction, through costs, a person whose conduct necessitated the
  motion or a person who unnecessarily adjourned the examination.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to attend or misconduct

**[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** Where a person fails to
attend an oral examination or refuses to take an oath, answer a proper
question, produce a document or other material required to be produced
or comply with an order made under rule 96, the Court may

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} order the person to attend or re-attend, as the case
  may be, at his or her own expense;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} order the person to answer a question that was
  improperly objected to and any proper question arising from the
  answer;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} strike all or part of the person's evidence,
  including an affidavit made by the person;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} dismiss the proceeding or give judgment by default,
  as the case may be; or

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} order the person or the party on whose behalf the
  person is being examined to pay the costs of the examination.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contempt order

**[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** A person who does not
comply with an order made under rule 96 or 97 may be found in contempt.

##### [Written Examinations]{.HTitleText4} {#h-1013461 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Written examination

- **[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party who intends to examine a person by way of a written examination
  shall serve a list of concise, separately numbered questions in Form
  99A for the person to answer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objections

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who objects to a question in a written
  examination may bring a motion to have the question struck out.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Answers to written examination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person examined by way of a written examination
  shall answer by way of an affidavit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of answers

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An affidavit referred to in subsection (3) shall be
  in Form 99B and be served on every other party within 30 days after
  service of the written examination under subsection (1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of oral examination rules

**[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** Rules 94, 95, 97 and 98
apply to written examinations, with such modifications as are necessary.

### [Joinder, Intervention and Parties]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1013473 .Subheading}

#### [Joinder]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013474 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Joinder of claims

- **[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to rule 302, a party to a
  proceeding may request relief against another party to the same
  proceeding in respect of more than one claim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Separate capacity

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party may request relief in a separate capacity in
  respect of different claims in a single proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest in all relief not essential

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Not all parties to a proceeding need have an interest
  in all relief claimed in the proceeding.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multiple persons joined as parties

**[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102 .sectionLabel}** Two or more persons who
are represented by the same solicitor may join in one proceeding as
plaintiffs, applicants or appellants where

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} if separate proceedings were brought by each of them,
  a common question of law or fact would arise in all of the
  proceedings; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the relief claimed, whether joint, several or
  alternative, arises from substantially the same facts or matter.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Misjoinder and nonjoinder

- **[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  proceeding shall be defeated by reason of the misjoinder or nonjoinder
  of a person or party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issues to be determined

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In a proceeding in which a proper person or party has
  not been joined, the Court shall determine the issues in dispute so
  far as they affect the rights and interests of the persons who are
  parties to the proceeding.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order for joinder or relief against
joinder

- **[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At
  any time, the Court may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} order that a person who is not a proper or
    necessary party shall cease to be a party; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} order that a person who ought to have been joined
    as a party or whose presence before the Court is necessary to ensure
    that all matters in dispute in the proceeding may be effectually and
    completely determined be added as a party, but no person shall be
    added as a plaintiff or applicant without his or her consent,
    signified in writing or in such other manner as the Court may order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (1) shall contain
  directions as to amendment of the originating document and any other
  pleadings.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consolidation of proceedings

**[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105 .sectionLabel}** The Court may order, in
respect of two or more proceedings,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} that they be consolidated, heard together or heard
  one immediately after the other;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} that one proceeding be stayed until another
  proceeding is determined; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} that one of the proceedings be asserted as a
  counterclaim or cross-appeal in another proceeding.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Separate determination of claims and
issues

**[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106 .sectionLabel}** Where the hearing of
two or more claims or parties in a single proceeding would cause undue
complication or delay or would prejudice a party, the Court may order
that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} claims against one or more parties be pursued
  separately;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} one or more claims be pursued separately;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a party be compensated for, or relieved from,
  attending any part of the proceeding in which the party does not have
  an interest; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the proceeding against a party be stayed on condition
  that the party is bound by any findings against another party.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Separate determination of issues

- **[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Court may, at any time, order the trial of an issue or that issues in
  a proceeding be determined separately.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court may stipulate procedure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In an order under subsection (1), the Court may give
  directions regarding the procedures to be followed, including those
  applicable to examinations for discovery and the discovery of
  documents.

#### [Interpleader]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013514 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpleader

- **[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where two or more persons make
  conflicting claims against another person in respect of property in
  the possession of that person and that person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} claims no interest in the property, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is willing to deposit the property with the Court
    or dispose of it as the Court directs,

  that person may bring an [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} motion for
  directions as to how the claims are to be decided.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On a motion under subsection (1), the Court shall
  give directions regarding

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} notice to be given to possible claimants and
    advertising for claimants;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the time within which claimants shall be required
    to file their claims; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the procedure to be followed in determining the
    rights of the claimants.

#### [Intervention]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013526 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave to intervene

- **[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Court may, on motion, grant leave to any person to intervene in a
  proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notice of motion

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notice of a motion under subsection (1) shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} set out the full name and address of the proposed
    intervener and of any solicitor acting for the proposed intervener;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} describe how the proposed intervener wishes to
    participate in the proceeding and how that participation will assist
    the determination of a factual or legal issue related to the
    proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In granting a motion under subsection (1), the Court
  shall give directions regarding

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the service of documents; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the role of the intervener, including costs, rights
    of appeal and any other matters relating to the procedure to be
    followed by the intervener.

#### [Questions of General Importance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013538 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Attorney General

**[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110 .sectionLabel}** Where a question of
general importance is raised in a proceeding, other than a question
referred to in section 57 of the Act,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any party may serve notice of the question on the
  Attorney General of Canada and any attorney general of a province who
  may be interested;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Court may direct the Administrator to bring the
  proceeding to the attention of the Attorney General of Canada and any
  attorney general of a province who may be interested; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Attorney General of Canada and the attorney
  general of a province may apply for leave to intervene.

#### [Parties]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013544 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unincorporated associations

**[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111 .sectionLabel}** A proceeding may be
brought by or against an unincorporated association in the name of the
association.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Partnerships

**[[111.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111.1 .sectionLabel}** A proceeding by or
against two or more persons as partners may be brought in the name of
the partnership.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sole proprietorships

**[[111.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111.2 .sectionLabel}** A proceeding by or
against a person carrying on business as a sole proprietor may be
brought in the name of the sole proprietorship.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Estates and trusts

- **[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  proceeding may be brought by or against the trustees, executors or
  administrators of an estate or trust without joining the beneficiaries
  of the estate or trust.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order binding on beneficiaries

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise, beneficiaries of
  an estate or trust are bound by an order against the estate or trust.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where deceased has no representative

- **[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a party to a proceeding is
  deceased and the estate of the deceased is not represented, the Court
  may appoint a person to represent the estate of the deceased or order
  that the proceeding continue without representation of the estate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before making an order under subsection (1), the
  Court may require that notice be given to all persons who have an
  interest in the estate of the deceased.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representative proceedings

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite rule 302, a proceeding,
  other than a proceeding referred to in section 27 or 28 of the Act,
  may be brought by or against a person acting as a representative on
  behalf of one or more other persons on the condition that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the issues asserted by or against the
    representative and the represented persons

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} are common issues of law and fact and there are
      no issues affecting only some of those persons, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} relate to a collective interest shared by those
      persons;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the representative is authorized to act on behalf
    of the represented persons;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the representative can fairly and adequately
    represent the interests of the represented persons; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the use of a representative proceeding is the just,
    most efficient and least costly manner of proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of the Court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} At any time, the Court may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determine whether the conditions set out in
    subsection (1) are being satisfied;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} require that notice be given, in a form and manner
    directed by it, to the represented persons;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} impose any conditions on the settlement process of
    a representative proceeding that the Court considers appropriate;
    and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} provide for the replacement of the representative
    if that person is unable to represent the interests of the
    represented persons fairly and adequately.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders in representative proceeding

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An order in a representative proceeding is binding on
  the represented persons unless otherwise ordered by the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of discontinuance or
  settlement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The discontinuance or settlement of a representative
  proceeding is not effective unless it is approved by the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Style of cause

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every document in a proceeding commenced under
  subsection (1) shall be prefaced by the heading "Representative
  Proceeding".

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-114-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 114"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of representatives

- **[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Court may appoint one or more persons to represent

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} unborn or unascertained persons who may have a
    present, future, contingent or other interest in a proceeding; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person under a legal disability against or by
    whom a proceeding is brought.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Who may be appointed

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Court may appoint as a representative under
  subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person who has already been appointed as such a
    representative under the laws of a province; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person eligible to act as a representative in the
    jurisdiction in which the person to be represented is domiciled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order binding on represented person

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise, a person for whom
  a representative is appointed under subsection (1) is bound by any
  order made in the proceeding.

#### [Transmission of Interest]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013599 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proceeding not to terminate

**[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** A proceeding is not
terminated only by reason that a party to a proceeding dies or becomes
bankrupt or, in the case of a corporation, ceases to exist.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assignment, transmission or devolution of
interest or liability

- **[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), where an
  interest of a party in, or the liability of a party under, a
  proceeding is assigned or transmitted to, or devolves upon, another
  person, the other person may, after serving and filing a notice and
  affidavit setting out the basis for the assignment, transmission or
  devolution, carry on the proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objection to person continuing

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a party to a proceeding objects to its continuance
  by a person referred to in subsection (1), the person seeking to
  continue the proceeding shall bring a motion for an order to be
  substituted for the original party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court may give directions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In an order given under subsection (2), the Court may
  give directions as to the further conduct of the proceeding.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to continue

**[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118 .sectionLabel}** Where an interest of a
party in, or the liability of a party under, a proceeding has been
assigned or transmitted to, or devolves upon, a person and that person
has not, within 30 days, served a notice and affidavit referred to in
subsection 117(1) or obtained an order under subsection 117(2), any
other party to the proceeding may bring a motion for default judgment or
to have the proceeding dismissed.

### [Representation of Parties]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1013611 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013612 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Individuals

- **[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to rule 121, an individual
  may act in person or be represented by a solicitor in a proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limited-scope representation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except in respect of a party referred to in rule 121,
  representation by a solicitor may be limited in scope to only those
  aspects of the proceeding that are within a solicitor's mandate that
  is agreed to by the individual and the solicitor.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-246, s. 3](#1333950-1337139){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1333950-1337139.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-119-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 119"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corporations or unincorporated
associations

**[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** A corporation,
partnership or unincorporated association shall be represented by a
solicitor in all proceedings, unless the Court in special circumstances
grants leave to it to be represented by an officer, partner or member,
as the case may be.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Parties under legal disability or acting
in representative capacity

**[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court in
special circumstances orders otherwise, a party who is under a legal
disability or who acts or seeks to act in a representative capacity,
including in a representative proceeding or a class proceeding, shall be
represented by a solicitor.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 13
- SOR/2007-301, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-121-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 121"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights and obligations

**[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122 .sectionLabel}** Subject to paragraphs
146(1)(b) and 152(2)(a) and unless the Court orders otherwise,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a party who is not represented by a solicitor, or a
  person who is authorized under rule 120 to represent a party, shall do
  everything required, and may do anything permitted, to be done by a
  solicitor under these Rules; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a party who is represented by a solicitor who is
  providing limited-scope representation shall do everything required,
  and may do anything permitted, to be done by a solicitor under these
  Rules in respect of those aspects of the proceeding that are not
  within the solicitor's mandate.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-246, s. 4](#1333952-1337145){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1333952-1337145.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-122-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 122"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Solicitor of Record]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013623 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Solicitor of record

- **[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  party takes a step in a proceeding by filing or serving a document
  signed by a solicitor, that solicitor is the solicitor of record for
  the party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limited-scope representation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a solicitor is providing limited-scope
  representation to a party, the solicitor is the solicitor of record
  only in respect of those aspects of the proceeding that are within the
  solicitor's mandate.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-246, s. 4](#1333952-1337152){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1333952-1337152.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-123-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 123"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment, change and removal of
solicitor of record

- **[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  a party may appoint a solicitor, or change or remove its solicitor of
  record, by serving and filing a notice in Form 124A, 124B or 124C, as
  the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limited-scope representation --- notice
  of appointment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party may appoint a solicitor to provide
  limited-scope representation by serving and filing a notice of
  limited-scope representation, in Form 124D, that is signed by the
  party and the solicitor and that sets out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the scope of the solicitor's mandate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whether it is the party or the solicitor who is to
    be served with documents relating to the mandate; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if it is the solicitor who is to be served, the
    solicitor's address for service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limited-scope representation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} However, with leave of the Court, a party may appoint
  a solicitor to provide limited-scope representation before serving and
  filing the notice referred to in subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for leave

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The request for leave shall be made in open court by
  the solicitor and shall summarize the scope of their mandate. If the
  request is granted, the party shall file the notice referred to in
  subsection (2) within two days after the day on which leave is
  granted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ceasing limited-scope representation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A solicitor who is providing limited-scope
  representation to a party may cease representation of the party by
  serving a notice to cease limited-scope representation, in Form 124E
  that is signed by the solicitor, on that party and all other parties
  to the proceeding and by filing the notice.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-246, s. 4](#1333952-1337168){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1333952-1337168.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-124-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 124"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion for removal of solicitor of record

- **[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a solicitor of record ceases
  to act for a party and the party has not changed its solicitor of
  record in accordance with rule 124, the Court may, on a motion of the
  solicitor, order that the solicitor be removed from the record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner of service

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice of motion under subsection (1) shall be
  served on the party formerly represented by the solicitor

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by personal service; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where personal service cannot practicably be
    effected,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} by mailing the notice of motion to the party at
      the party's last known address, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if no mailing address of the party is known, by
      depositing the notice of motion at the Registry office where the
      proceeding was initiated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to be served

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (1) removing a
  solicitor of record of a party shall be served on the party in the
  manner set out in subsection (2) and on all other parties to the
  proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of service

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An order under subsection (1) does not take effect
  until proof of its service has been filed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cessation of representation

**[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** A solicitor is deemed
not to represent a party if the solicitor dies or ceases to act for the
party for any of the following reasons:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} appointment to a public office incompatible with the
  solicitor's profession;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} suspension or disbarment as a solicitor;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} an order made under rule 125; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the solicitor serves and files a notice under
  subsection 124(5).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-246, s. 5](#1333954-1337176){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1333954-1337176.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-126-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 126"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Service of Documents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1013648 .Subheading}

#### [Address for Service]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1337189 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Party's address for service

- **[[126.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  a party's address for service is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the party is not represented by a solicitor, the
    address shown on the last document filed by the party that indicates
    an address in Canada; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the party is represented by a solicitor of
    record, the solicitor's address that is set out in the last document
    filed by the solicitor in the proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- limited-scope
  representation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a party is represented by a solicitor who is
  providing limited-scope representation and who has agreed to accept
  the service of documents relating to their mandate, the party's
  address for service in relation to those documents is the address set
  out for that purpose in the notice of limited-scope representation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- Crown or Attorney General
  of Canada

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The address for service for the Crown or the Attorney
  General of Canada is the address of the office of the Deputy Attorney
  General of Canada in Ottawa.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-246, s. 6](#1333956-1337200){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1333956-1337200.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Personal Service]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013649 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of originating documents

- **[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  originating document that has been issued, other than in an appeal
  from the Federal Court to the Federal Court of Appeal or an [ex
  parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} application under rule 327, shall be
  served personally.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party who has already participated in the
  proceeding need not be personally served.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of notice of appeal on the
  Crown

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (1) and (2), in the case of an
  appeal from Federal Court to the Federal Court of Appeal, if the
  Crown, the Attorney General of Canada or any other minister of the
  Crown is a respondent, the notice of appeal shall be served personally
  on them in accordance with rule 133.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 13
- SOR/2010-177, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-127-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 127"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal service on individual

- **[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Personal service of a document on an
  individual, other than an individual under a legal disability, is
  effected

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by leaving the document with the individual;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by leaving the document with an adult person
    residing at the individual's place of residence, and mailing a copy
    of the document to the individual at that address;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the individual is carrying on a business in
    Canada, other than a partnership, in a name or style other than the
    individual's own name, by leaving the document with the person
    apparently having control or management of the business at any place
    where the business is carried on in Canada;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} by mailing the document to the individual's last
    known address, accompanied by an acknowledgement of receipt form in
    Form 128, if the individual signs and returns the acknowledgement of
    receipt card or signs a post office receipt;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} by mailing the document by registered mail to the
    individual's last known address, if the individual signs a post
    office receipt; or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in any other manner provided by an Act of
    Parliament applicable to the proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective day of service

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Service under paragraph (1)(b) is effective on the
  tenth day after the copy is mailed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective day of service

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Service under paragraph (1)(d) or (e) is effective on
  the day of receipt indicated on the acknowledgement of receipt form or
  post office receipt, as the case may be.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal service on individual under
legal disability

**[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129 .sectionLabel}** Personal service of a
document on an individual under a legal disability is effected by
serving the individual in such a manner as the Court may order, having
regard to the manner in which the interests of the person will be best
protected.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal service on corporation

- **[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), personal
  service of a document on a corporation is effected

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by leaving the document

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} with an officer or director of the corporation or
      a person employed by the corporation as legal counsel, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} with the person apparently in charge, at the
      time of the service, of the head office or of the branch or agency
      in Canada where the service is effected;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the manner provided by any Act of Parliament
    applicable to the proceeding; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the manner provided for service on a corporation
    in proceedings before a superior court in the province in which the
    service is being effected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal service on municipal
  corporation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Personal service of a document on a municipal
  corporation is effected by leaving the document with the chief
  executive officer or legal counsel of the municipality.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal service on partnership

**[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131 .sectionLabel}** Personal service of a
document on a partnership is effected by leaving the document with

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the partnership is a limited partnership, a
  general partner; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, a partner or the person who has
  the control or management of the partnership business at its principal
  place of business in Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal service on sole proprietorship

**[[131.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131.1 .sectionLabel}** Personal service of
a document on a sole proprietorship is effected by leaving the document
with

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the sole proprietor; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person apparently in charge, at the time of the
  service, of the place of business of the sole proprietorship in Canada
  where the service is effected.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 14
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal service on unincorporated
association

**[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132 .sectionLabel}** Personal service of a
document on an unincorporated association is effected by leaving the
document with

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an officer of the association; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person who has the control or management of the
  affairs of the association at any office or premises occupied by the
  association.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal service of originating document
on the Crown

- **[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Personal service of an originating
  document on the Crown, the Attorney General of Canada or any other
  Minister of the Crown is effected by filing the original and two paper
  copies of it at the Registry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to Deputy Attorney General

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Administrator shall forthwith transmit a
  certified copy of an originating document filed under subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where it was filed at the principal office of the
    Registry, to the office of the Deputy Attorney General of Canada in
    Ottawa; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where it was filed at a local office, to the
    Director of the regional office of the Department of Justice
    referred to in subsection 4(2) of the [Crown Liability and
    Proceedings (Provincial Court)
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-91-604).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When service is effective

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Service under subsection (1) is effective at the time
  the document is filed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 14
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-133-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 133"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acceptance of service by solicitor

**[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** Personal service of a
document on a party may be effected by the acceptance of service by the
party's solicitor.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed personal service on a person
outside Canada

**[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** Where a person

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is resident outside Canada and, in the ordinary
  course of business, enters into contracts or business transactions in
  Canada in connection with which the person regularly makes use of the
  services of a person resident in Canada, and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} made use of such services in connection with a
  contract or business transaction,

in a proceeding arising out of the contract or transaction, personal
service of a document on the person resident outside Canada is effected
by personally serving the person resident in Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Substituted service or dispensing with
service

- **[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where service of a document that is
  required to be served personally cannot practicably be effected, the
  Court may order substitutional service or dispense with service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion may be made [ex
  parte]{.otherLang lang="la"}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A motion for an order under subsection (1) may be
  made [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"}.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to be served

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A document served by substitutional service shall
  make reference to the order that authorized the substitutional
  service.

#### [Service outside Canada]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013723 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service outside Canada

- **[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a
  document to be personally served outside Canada may be served in the
  manner set out in rules 127 to 136 or in the manner prescribed by the
  law of the jurisdiction in which service is to be effected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hague Convention

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where service is to be effected in a contracting
  state to the Hague Convention, service shall be as provided by the
  Convention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of service

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Service of documents outside Canada may be proven

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the manner set out in rule 146;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the manner provided by the law of the
    jurisdiction in which service was effected; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with the Hague Convention, if service
    is effected in a contracting state.

#### [Other Forms of Service]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013734 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal service of originating documents

**[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138 .sectionLabel}** Unless otherwise
provided in these Rules, personal service is required only for
originating documents.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-138-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 138"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner of service --- other documents

- **[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  document is not required to be served personally, service on a party
  is to be effected by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} personal service;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} leaving it at the party's address for service;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} mailing it or delivering it by courier to the
    party's address for service;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} transmitting it by fax to the party's solicitor of
    record or to the party, as the case may be;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} transmitting it to the electronic address set out
    by the party in Form 141A; or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any other means that the Court may direct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service on other parties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 36(3) and rules 145 and 204.1,
  the document shall be served on all other parties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If no address for service

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a party has no address for service at the time of
  service, the document may be served by leaving it at, or by sending it
  by registered mail or courier to,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the party is an individual, the party's usual or
    last known address; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the party is an unincorporated body, a group of
    persons or a corporation, the principal or last known address of the
    body, group or corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If no known address

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a party has no known address at the time of
  service, the document may be served by depositing it at the office of
  the Registry where the proceeding was commenced.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 15
- [SOR/2021-150, s. 3](#1302226-1305047){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1302226-1305047.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2021-246, s. 7](#1333958-1337202){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1333958-1337202.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-139-20210617.html "Link to previous version of section 139"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service by fax

- **[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  recipient's consent is required before any of the following documents
  may be served by fax:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a motion record, application record, trial record,
    appeal book or book of authorities; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other document that is longer than 20 pages.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fax cover page

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A document that is served by fax shall include a
  cover page that sets out the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name, address and telephone number of the
    sender;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person on whom the document is
    being served;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the date and time of the transmission;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the total number of pages being transmitted,
    including the cover page;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a fax number at which the sender may receive
    documents; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the name and telephone number of the person that is
    to be contacted in the event of a transmission problem.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-140-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 140"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent to electronic service

- **[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party consents to the electronic service of documents by serving and
  filing a notice of consent in Form 141A.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When consent is effective

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The consent is effective on the day on which the
  notice is served.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawal of consent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A party withdraws their consent by serving and filing
  a notice of withdrawal of consent in Form 141B.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When withdrawal is effective

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The withdrawal of consent is effective on the day on
  which the notice is served.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A party shall not serve a document by electronic
  service prior to being served with the recipient's notice of consent
  or after the withdrawal of that consent.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-141-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 141"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013786 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When service may be effected

**[[142]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142 .sectionLabel}** Service of a document
under these Rules may be effected at any time.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-142-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 142"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date --- evening or holiday
service

- **[[143]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Service of a document, other than an
  originating document or a warrant, on a holiday or after 5:00 p.m. at
  the recipient's local time is effective on the next day that is not a
  holiday.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date --- ordinary mail

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Service of a document by ordinary mail is effective
  on the 10th day after the day on which it is mailed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date --- registered mail or
  courier

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Service of a document by registered mail or courier
  is effective on the day of delivery that is indicated on the post
  office or courier delivery receipt, as the case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-143-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 143"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing before service effective

**[[144]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144 .sectionLabel}** A document that is
served by ordinary mail may be filed before the day on which its service
is effective.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-144-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 144"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When no further service required

**[[145]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145 .sectionLabel}** Subject to subsection
207(2) or unless the Court orders otherwise, a party who has been served
with an originating document is not required to be served with any
further documents in the proceeding prior to final judgment if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the party has not filed a notice of appearance or a
  defence within the time set out in these Rules; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the party has no address for service and has not
  served and filed a notice of consent to electronic service in Form
  141A.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-145-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 145"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of service

- **[[146]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Service of a document is proven by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an affidavit of service in Form 146A or, if the
    service is effected in the Province of Quebec, a certificate of
    service of a sheriff, bailiff or other authorized person in
    accordance with the Code of Civil Procedure of that Province;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the document is not an originating document, a
    solicitor's certificate of service in Form 146B;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the service is effected by leaving a copy of the
    document at a solicitor's office, by an acknowledgement of service
    that is signed and dated by the solicitor or another person on
    behalf of the solicitor; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the service is effected under rule 134, an
    acceptance of service that is signed and dated by the party's
    solicitor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acknowledgement of service -- signature

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an acknowledgement of service under paragraph
  (1)(c) is signed by a person on behalf of a solicitor, the person
  shall sign his or her own name.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-146-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 146"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validating service

**[[147]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147 .sectionLabel}** If a document has been
served in a manner that is not authorized by these Rules or by an order
of the Court, the Court may validate the service if it is satisfied that
the document came to the notice of the person to be served or that it
would have come to that person's notice except for the person's
avoidance of service.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-147-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 147"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where document does not reach person
served

**[[148]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148 .sectionLabel}** On the motion of a
party who did not have notice of a served document or did not obtain
notice of it at the time of service, the Court may set aside the
consequences of default or grant an extension of time or an adjournment,
notwithstanding that the party was served with the document in
accordance with these Rules.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service --- limited-scope representation

**[[148.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148.1 .sectionLabel}** If a party is
represented by a solicitor who is providing limited-scope representation
and who has agreed to accept the service of documents relating to their
mandate, all documents relating to the mandate shall be served on the
solicitor. All other documents shall be served on the party.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-246, s. 8](#1333960-1337206){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1333960-1337206.html"
  role="button"}
:::

### [Payments]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1013827 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payments into court

- **[[149]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who pays money into court shall deliver to the Registry

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a certified cheque or other bill of exchange drawn
    on a bank, trust company, credit union or caisse populaire or any
    other bill of exchange authorized by order of the Court, payable to
    the order of the Receiver General; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} three paper copies of a tender of payment into
    court in Form 149.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date of payment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Payment into court by a certified cheque or other
  bill of exchange that is paid on presentation for payment is effective
  on the day on which it was delivered to the Registry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Receipt for payment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} When a certified cheque or other bill of exchange is
  paid, the Administrator shall endorse or acknowledge receipt on a copy
  of the tender of payment into court and return it to the person who
  made the payment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-18, s. 4
- SOR/2015-21, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-149-20130208.html "Link to previous version of section 149"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment out of court

**[[150]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150 .sectionLabel}** Where an order has been
made by the Court for payment out of court of money that is in the
Consolidated Revenue Fund, a requisition shall be made by the
Administrator to the Receiver General for an instrument for the amount
to be paid out.

### [Filing of Confidential Material]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1013841 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criminal proceeding under [Competition
Act](/eng/acts/C-34)

**[[150.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150.1 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court
orders otherwise, all documents that have been submitted for filing,
filed or added to the annex to the Court file in relation to a criminal
proceeding instituted under the [Competition Act](/eng/acts/C-34) shall
be treated as confidential before the trial of that proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-150, s. 4](#1302228-1305051){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1302228-1305051.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion for order of confidentiality

- **[[151]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  motion, the Court may order that material to be filed shall be treated
  as confidential.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Demonstrated need for confidentiality

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before making an order under subsection (1), the
  Court must be satisfied that the material should be treated as
  confidential, notwithstanding the public interest in open and
  accessible court proceedings.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Marking of confidential material

- **[[152]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where the material is required by
  law to be treated confidentially or where the Court orders that
  material be treated confidentially, a party who files the material
  shall separate and clearly mark it as confidential, identifying the
  legislative provision or the Court order under which it is required to
  be treated as confidential.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to confidential material

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise ordered by the Court,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} only a solicitor of record, or a solicitor
    assisting in the proceeding, who is not a party is entitled to have
    access to confidential material;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} confidential material shall be given to a solicitor
    of record for a party only if the solicitor gives a written
    undertaking to the Court that he or she will

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} not disclose its content except to solicitors
      assisting in the proceeding or to the Court in the course of
      argument,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} not permit it to be reproduced in whole or in
      part, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} destroy the material and any notes on its
      content and file a certificate of their destruction or deliver the
      material and notes as ordered by the Court, when the material and
      notes are no longer required for the proceeding or the solicitor
      ceases to be solicitor of record;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} only one copy of any confidential material shall be
    given to the solicitor of record for each party; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} no confidential material or any information derived
    therefrom shall be disclosed to the public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to continue

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (1) continues in
  effect until the Court orders otherwise, including for the duration of
  any appeal of the proceeding and after final judgment.

### [References]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1013861 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order for reference

- **[[153]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-153 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Court may, for the purpose of making an inquiry and report, refer any
  question of fact in a proceeding to a judge or other person designated
  by the Chief Justice of the court before which the proceeding is
  pending.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions on reference

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding rules 155 to 160, the Court may at
  any time give directions regarding the conduct of a reference.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 14
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay of related proceedings

**[[154]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-154 .sectionLabel}** Where a reference is
made under rule 153, on motion, the Court may stay any proceeding
related to the reference, including a proceeding that has previously
been stayed, for a period of not more than six months.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requisition to fix time and place of
reference

- **[[155]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-155 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On a
  reference made under rule 153, the referee shall, on the requisition
  of a party, fix a time and place for the hearing of the reference.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents to be provided to referee

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party who makes a requisition under subsection (1)
  shall provide the referee with a statement of the issues and copies of
  the pleadings and order of reference.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conduct of reference

**[[156]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-156 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court orders
otherwise, a referee shall adopt the simplest, least expensive and most
expeditious manner of conducting the reference.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order for examination or production

**[[157]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-157 .sectionLabel}** A referee may order
that parties be examined for discovery and order the production for
inspection and copying by a party of any document or other material
relevant to a matter in issue, at the time and place and in the manner
set out in the order.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attendance of witnesses

- **[[158]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  attendance of witnesses to give evidence at a reference shall be
  enforced by [subpoena]{.otherLang lang="la"}.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recording of evidence on reference

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The testimony of a witness at a reference shall be
  recorded.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of referee

- **[[159]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a referee
  shall have the same power and authority in matters of practice and
  procedure as would a judge of the Court presiding at the trial of an
  action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A referee shall not commit a person to prison or
  enforce an order for attachment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral of question to Court

- **[[160]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  referee may, before the conclusion of a reference or by a report on
  the reference, submit any question for determination by the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Response to referral

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a submission under subsection (1), the
  Court may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} require any explanations or reasons from the
    referee; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} remit the matter, or any part thereof, for further
    inquiry to the same or another referee.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referee's report

- **[[161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  report of a referee shall include the findings of the referee in the
  same form as an order of the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing of report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The report of a referee, the record of any evidence
  taken at the hearing of the reference and any exhibits or other
  documents provided to the referee shall be filed as soon as possible
  after the report is signed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of report

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On the filing of a referee's report, the
  Administrator shall send without delay a copy of it to all parties

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by registered mail;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by electronic means, including facsimile and
    electronic mail; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} by any other means, as directed by the Chief
    Justice, likely to bring the report to the attention of the party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of receipt

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a report is transmitted by electronic means, the
  Administrator shall confirm receipt by the party and place proof of
  that receipt on the Court file.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-177, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-161-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 161"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report of referee who is a judge

**[[162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162 .sectionLabel}** The report of a referee
who is a judge is final and becomes a judgment of the Court when it is
filed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal of referee's findings

- **[[163]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party may appeal the findings of a report of a referee who is not a
  judge on motion to the court that ordered the reference.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notice of a motion under subsection (1) shall be
  served and filed within 30 days after filing of the report of a
  referee and at least 10 days before the day fixed for hearing of the
  motion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Court on appeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On an appeal under subsection (1), the Court may
  confirm, vary or reverse the findings of the report and deliver
  judgment or refer it back to the referee, or to another referee, for
  further inquiry and report.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 15
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report final if not appealed

- **[[164]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  report of a referee who is not a judge that is not appealed becomes
  final 30 days after it is filed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Final report deemed judgment of Court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A report of a referee, once final, becomes a judgment
  of the Court.

### [Summary Disposition]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1013927 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discontinuances

**[[165]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165 .sectionLabel}** A party may discontinue
all or part of a proceeding by serving and filing a notice of
discontinuance.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of discontinuance

**[[166]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166 .sectionLabel}** A party shall file a
declaration of settlement or a notice of discontinuance in Form 166 in a
proceeding that has been concluded other than by a judgment or
discontinuance on consent.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dismissal for delay

**[[167]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167 .sectionLabel}** The Court may, at any
time, on the motion of a party who is not in default of any requirement
of these Rules, dismiss a proceeding or impose other sanctions on the
ground that there has been undue delay by a plaintiff, applicant or
appellant in prosecuting the proceeding.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dismissal where continuation impossible

**[[168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-168 .sectionLabel}** Where following an
order of the Court it is not possible to continue a proceeding, the
Court may dismiss the proceeding.

## [PART 4]{.HLabel1}[Actions]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1013936 .Part}

### [Application of this Part]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1013937 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies to
all proceedings that are not applications or appeals, including

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2021-151, s. 7\]]{.Repealed}

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} applications under subsection 33(1) of the [Marine
  Liability Act](/eng/acts/M-0.7); and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} any other proceedings required or permitted by or
  under an Act of Parliament to be brought as an action.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 37
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 7](#1303075-1305164){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303075-1305164.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-169-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 169"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules applicable to counterclaims and
third parties

**[[170]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170 .sectionLabel}** Except as provided in
rules 189 to 199, the rules in this Part applicable to plaintiffs and
defendants apply, with such modifications as are necessary, to parties
bringing or defending counterclaims and third party claims.

### [Pleadings in an Action]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1013947 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013948 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pleadings

**[[171]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-171 .sectionLabel}** The following pleadings
may be filed:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an action,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a statement of claim, in Form 171A,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a statement of defence, in Form 171B, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a reply, in Form 171C;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a counterclaim,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a counterclaim, in Form 171D or 171E,,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a defence to counterclaim, in Form 171F, and
