  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a reply to a defence to counterclaim, in Form
    171G; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a third party claim,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a third party claim, in Form 171H or 171I,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a third party defence, in Form 171J, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a reply to a third party defence, in Form 171K.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pleading after a reply

**[[172]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172 .sectionLabel}** No pleading may be
filed after a reply without leave of the Court.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form of pleadings

- **[[173]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Pleadings shall be divided into
  consecutively numbered paragraphs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Allegations set out separately

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every allegation in a pleading shall, as far as is
  practicable, be set out in a separate paragraph.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Material facts

**[[174]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174 .sectionLabel}** Every pleading shall
contain a concise statement of the material facts on which the party
relies, but shall not include evidence by which those facts are to be
proved.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pleading law

**[[175]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-175 .sectionLabel}** A party may raise any
point of law in a pleading.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions precedent

- **[[176]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-176 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  performance or occurrence of a condition precedent to the assertion of
  a claim or defence need not be pleaded.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contesting condition precedent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The non-performance or non-occurrence of a condition
  precedent shall be pleaded.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents or conversations

**[[177]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-177 .sectionLabel}** A pleading shall
briefly describe any document or conversation referred to in the
pleading, but need not set out the exact words of the document or
conversation unless the words are themselves material.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternative claims or defences

**[[178]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178 .sectionLabel}** A party may plead
claims or defences in the alternative.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent facts

**[[179]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-179 .sectionLabel}** A party may plead a
fact that occurs after the commencement of an action, even though the
fact gives rise to a new claim or defence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inconsistent pleading

**[[180]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180 .sectionLabel}** A party may plead an
allegation of fact, or raise a new ground of claim in a pleading, that
is inconsistent with a previous pleading only if the party amends the
previous pleading accordingly.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Particulars

- **[[181]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  pleading shall contain particulars of every allegation contained
  therein, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} particulars of any alleged misrepresentation,
    fraud, breach of trust, wilful default or undue influence; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} particulars of any alleged state of mind of a
    person, including any alleged mental disorder or disability, malice
    or fraudulent intention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further and better particulars

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On motion, the Court may order a party to serve and
  file further and better particulars of any allegation in its pleading

#### [Statements of Claim]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1013994 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Claims to be specified

**[[182]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182 .sectionLabel}** Every statement of
claim, counterclaim and third party claim shall specify

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nature of any damages claimed;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where monetary relief is claimed, whether the amount
  claimed, exclusive of interest and costs, exceeds \$50,000;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the value of any property sought to be recovered;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other specific relief being claimed, other than
  costs; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} whether the action is being proceeded with as a
  simplified action.

#### [Subsequent Pleadings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014002 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Admissions

**[[183]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183 .sectionLabel}** In a defence or
subsequent pleading, a party shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} admit every allegation of material fact in the
  pleadings of every adverse party that is not disputed;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where it is intended to prove a version of facts that
  differs from that relied on by an adverse party, plead that version of
  the facts; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} plead any matter or fact that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} might defeat a claim or defence of an adverse
    party, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} might take an adverse party by surprise if it were
    not pleaded.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed denial

- **[[184]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All
  allegations of fact in a pleading that are not admitted are deemed to
  be denied.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof not required

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless denied by an adverse party, it is not
  necessary that a party prove

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} its right to claim in a representative capacity; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} its constitution as a partnership, association or
    corporation.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of denial

**[[185]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-185 .sectionLabel}** Where a party alleges
an agreement in a pleading, a bare denial of the agreement pleaded by
another party shall be construed only as a denial of the making of the
agreement or of the facts from which such an agreement may be implied
and not as a denial of the legality or legal sufficiency of the
agreement.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Set-off

**[[186]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186 .sectionLabel}** Where a claim to a sum
of money, including a sum that is not ascertained, is relied on as a
defence to all or part of a claim made by an adverse party, it may be
included in a defence as a set-off against the claim, whether or not it
is also added as a counterclaim.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judgment for balance

**[[187]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187 .sectionLabel}** Where judgments in an
action and in a counterclaim are given at the same time, the Court may
set off the amount of one award against the other, without prejudice as
to costs.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defence of tender

**[[188]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-188 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 31.2
of the [Crown Liability and Proceedings Act](/eng/acts/C-50), a defence
of tender before action may not be pleaded unless the defendant has paid
into court the amount alleged to have been tendered.

#### [Counterclaims]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014025 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When available

- **[[189]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  defendant who claims to be entitled to relief against a plaintiff may
  make a counterclaim instead of bringing a separate action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statement of defence and counterclaim

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A counterclaim shall be included in the same document
  as the statement of defence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Style of cause

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A statement of defence and counterclaim shall contain
  a second style of cause identifying the plaintiff by counterclaim and
  the defendants to the counterclaim.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Counterclaim may proceed independently

**[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190 .sectionLabel}** A counterclaim may be
proceeded with notwithstanding that judgment is given in the action or
that the action is stayed or discontinued.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Counterclaim against person not already a
party

- **[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a defendant who counterclaims
  alleges that a person who is not a party to the action is liable to
  the defendant along with the plaintiff in respect of the
  subject-matter of the counterclaim, the defendant may join that person
  as a defendant to the counterclaim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When counterclaim to be issued

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a defendant adds a person who is not already a
  party as a defendant to a counterclaim, the defendant's statement of
  defence and counterclaim shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} issued within the time set out in rule 204 for the
    service and filing of a statement of defence; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} served on the person and on the other parties
    within 30 days after it is issued.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defence to counterclaim

- **[[192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  defendant to a counterclaim who is already a party to the action shall
  defend the counterclaim by serving and filing a defence to
  counterclaim within 30 days after service of the statement of defence
  and counterclaim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reply and defence to counterclaim

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A reply and a defence to counterclaim by a plaintiff
  against whom a counterclaim has been made shall be included in the
  same document.

#### [Third Party Claims]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014047 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Availability as of right

**[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193 .sectionLabel}** A defendant may
commence a third party claim against a co-defendant, or against a person
who is not a party to the action, who the defendant claims is or may be
liable to the defendant for all or part of the plaintiff's claim.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where leave of Court required

**[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194 .sectionLabel}** With leave of the
Court, a defendant may commence a third party claim against a
co-defendant, or against another person who is not a defendant to the
action, who the defendant claims

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is or may be liable to the defendant for relief,
  other than that referred to in rule 193, relating to the
  subject-matter of the action; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} should be bound by the determination of an issue
  between the plaintiff and the defendant.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for third party claim

**[[195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195 .sectionLabel}** A third party claim
against a co-defendant shall be served and filed within 10 days after
the filing of the statement of defence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Third party claim against non-defendant

- **[[196]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  third party claim against a person who is not already a party to the
  action shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} issued within the time set out in rule 204 for the
    service and filing of a statement of defence; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} served within 30 days after it is issued.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of pleadings

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A third party claim served on a person who is not
  already a party to the action shall be accompanied by a copy of all
  pleadings filed in the action.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for defence to third party claim

- **[[197]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-197 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  third party shall defend the plaintiff's claim against the defendant
  by filing a statement of defence within the time set out in rule 204.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights and obligations of third party

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A third party defending the plaintiff's claim against
  the defendant has the same procedural rights and obligations in the
  action as the defendant, including those in respect of discovery,
  trial and appeal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing of third party claim

- **[[198]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-198
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise, a
  third party claim shall be heard and decided as part of the action
  from which it arose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Questions of third party liability

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Court may order the question of liability as
  between the third party and the defendant to be tried in such a
  manner, at or after the trial of the action, as is set out in the
  order.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order binding on third party

- **[[199]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-199 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  third party is bound by any order or determination made in an action
  between the plaintiff and the defendant who made the third party
  claim, whether or not the third party defended the plaintiff's claim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consequences of default of third party
  defence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A third party who defends neither the third party
  claim nor the plaintiff's claim is deemed to admit

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the validity of any judgment obtained by the
    plaintiff against the defendant, including a judgment obtained by
    consent; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the third party's liability to contribute or
    indemnify to the extent specified in the third party claim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave required to enforce default
  judgment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A judgment against a third party referred to in
  subsection (2) shall not be enforced without leave of the Court.

#### [Amendment of Pleadings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014082 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment as of right

**[[200]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200 .sectionLabel}** Notwithstanding rules
75 and 76, a party may, without leave, amend any of its pleadings at any
time before another party has pleaded thereto or on the filing of the
written consent of the other parties.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment to add new cause of action

**[[201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-201 .sectionLabel}** An amendment may be
made under rule 76 notwithstanding that the effect of the amendment will
be to add or substitute a new cause of action, if the new cause of
action arises out of substantially the same facts as a cause of action
in respect of which the party seeking the amendment has already claimed
relief in the action.

#### [Close of Pleadings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014087 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Close of pleadings

**[[202]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202 .sectionLabel}** Pleadings are closed

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where a statement of defence has not been filed
  within the period set out in rule 204, on the expiration of that
  period;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} on the filing of a reply; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} on the expiration of the time for filing a reply.

#### [Time for Service of Pleadings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014093 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statement of claim

- **[[203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  statement of claim shall be served within 60 days after it is issued.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of service

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Proof of service of a statement of claim shall be
  filed within the time set out in rule 204 for the service and filing
  of a statement of defence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defence

- **[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  defendant shall defend an action by serving and filing a statement of
  defence within

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 30 days after the day on which of the statement of
    claim is served, if the defendant is served in Canada or the United
    States; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 60 days after the day on which of the statement of
    claim is served, if the defendant is served outside Canada and the
    United States.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, if the defendant serves and files a notice
  of intention to respond in accordance with rule 204.1, the time for
  serving and filing the statement of defence is extended by 10 days.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-150, s. 5](#1302230-1305060){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1302230-1305060.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-204-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 204"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of intention to respond

**[[204.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204.1 .sectionLabel}** A defendant who is
served with a statement of claim and who intends to respond to the
action may, within 10 days after the day on which they are served, serve
on the plaintiff and file a notice of intention to respond in Form
204.1.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-150, s. 5](#1302230-1305064){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1302230-1305064.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reply

**[[205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205 .sectionLabel}** A plaintiff's reply to
a statement of defence shall be served and filed within 10 days after
service of the statement of defence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents referred to in pleadings

**[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206 .sectionLabel}** A copy of every
document referred to in a pleading shall be served with the pleading or
within 10 days after service of the pleading, unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the party being served waives its right to the copy;
  or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Court orders otherwise.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of counterclaim where no new
party added

- **[[207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a counterclaim is brought
  against a plaintiff only, or against only a plaintiff and another
  party to the action, the statement of defence and counterclaim shall
  be served and filed within the time set out in rule 204.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a defendant to a counterclaim who is also a
  defendant in the action has failed to file a statement of defence in
  the action, that defendant shall be served personally with a statement
  of defence and counterclaim.

### [Preliminary Matters]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014115 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No attornment to jurisdiction

**[[208]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208 .sectionLabel}** A party does not attorn
to the jurisdiction of the Court by

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} filing a notice of intention to respond; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} bringing a motion to object to

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any irregularity in the commencement of the action,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the service of the statement of claim,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the Court as not being a convenient forum, or

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the jurisdiction of the Court.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-150, s. 6](#1302232-1305075){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1302232-1305075.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-208-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 208"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2021-246, s. 9\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-209-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 209"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Default Proceedings]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014125 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion for default judgment

- **[[210]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a defendant fails to serve and
  file a statement of defence within the time set out in rule 204 or any
  other time fixed by an order of the Court, the plaintiff may bring a
  motion for judgment against the defendant on the statement of claim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion in writing

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 25 of the [Crown Liability and
  Proceedings Act](/eng/acts/C-50), a motion under subsection (1) may be
  brought [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} and in accordance with rule
  369.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Affidavit evidence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A motion under subsection (1) shall be supported by
  affidavit evidence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of motion

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On a motion under subsection (1), the Court may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} grant judgment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} dismiss the action; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} order that the action proceed to trial and that the
    plaintiff prove its case in such a manner as the Court may direct.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service pursuant to order for
substitutional service

**[[211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211 .sectionLabel}** Judgment shall not be
given against a defendant who is in default where service of the
statement of claim was effected pursuant to an order for substitutional
service, unless the Court is satisfied that it is just to do so having
regard to all the circumstances.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service pursuant to Hague Convention

- **[[212]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-212
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a statement of claim was sent
  abroad for service on a defendant in a contracting state to the *Hague
  Convention* and the defendant has not filed a defence, judgment shall
  not be given under rule 210 unless the Court is satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the statement of claim was

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} served by a method prescribed by the law of the
      state in which service was made, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} delivered to the defendant or to the defendant's
      residence by another method provided for in the Hague Convention;
      and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the defendant has had sufficient time after the
    service or delivery to file a defence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judgment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (1), the Court may give
  judgment under rule 210 if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the statement of claim was sent by a method
    provided for in the Hague Convention;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a period of not less than six months, or such
    longer period as the Court considers adequate in the circumstances,
    has elapsed since the day on which the statement of claim was sent;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} no certificate under article 6 of the *Hague
    Convention* was received, and every reasonable effort was made to
    obtain such a certificate through the competent authorities of the
    state to which the statement of claim was sent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interlocutory injunction or
  [mandamus]{.otherLang lang="la"}

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} This rule does not preclude the Court from making an
  order under rule 373 before service of the statement of claim.

### [Summary Judgment and Summary Trial]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014154 .Subheading}

#### [Motion and Service]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014155 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion by a party

- **[[213]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-213 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party may bring a motion for summary judgment or summary trial on all
  or some of the issues raised in the pleadings at any time after the
  defendant has filed a defence but before the time and place for trial
  have been fixed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further motion

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a party brings a motion for summary judgment or
  summary trial, the party may not bring a further motion for either
  summary judgment or summary trial except with leave of the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligations of moving party

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A motion for summary judgment or summary trial in an
  action may be brought by serving and filing a notice of motion and
  motion record at least 20 days before the day set out in the notice
  for the hearing of the motion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligations of responding party

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A party served with a motion for summary judgment or
  summary trial shall serve and file a respondent's motion record not
  later than 10 days before the day set out in the notice of motion for
  the hearing of the motion.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-331, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-213-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 213"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Summary Judgment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014167 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Facts and evidence required

**[[214]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-214 .sectionLabel}** A response to a motion
for summary judgment shall not rely on what might be adduced as evidence
at a later stage in the proceedings. It must set out specific facts and
adduce the evidence showing that there is a genuine issue for trial.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-331, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-214-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 214"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If no genuine issue for trial

- **[[215]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  on a motion for summary judgment the Court is satisfied that there is
  no genuine issue for trial with respect to a claim or defence, the
  Court shall grant summary judgment accordingly.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Genuine issue of amount or question of
  law

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Court is satisfied that the only genuine issue
  is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the amount to which the moving party is entitled,
    the Court may order a trial of that issue or grant summary judgment
    with a reference under rule 153 to determine the amount; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a question of law, the Court may determine the
    question and grant summary judgment accordingly.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Court

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Court is satisfied that there is a genuine
  issue of fact or law for trial with respect to a claim or a defence,
  the Court may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} nevertheless determine that issue by way of summary
    trial and make any order necessary for the conduct of the summary
    trial; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} dismiss the motion in whole or in part and order
    that the action, or the issues in the action not disposed of by
    summary judgment, proceed to trial or that the action be conducted
    as a specially managed proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-331, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-215-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 215"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Summary Trial]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014185 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion record for summary trial

- **[[216]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  motion record for a summary trial shall contain all of the evidence on
  which a party seeks to rely, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} affidavits;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} admissions under rule 256;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} affidavits or statements of an expert witness
    prepared in accordance with subsection 258(5); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any part of the evidence that would be admissible
    under rules 288 and 289.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further affidavits or statements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No further affidavits or statements may be served,
  except

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the moving party, if their content
    is limited to evidence that would be admissible at trial as rebuttal
    evidence and they are served and filed at least 5 days before the
    day set out in the notice of motion for the hearing of the summary
    trial; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with leave of the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conduct of summary trial

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Court may make any order required for the conduct
  of the summary trial, including an order requiring a deponent or an
  expert who has given a statement to attend for cross-examination
  before the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adverse inference

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Court may draw an adverse inference if a party
  fails to cross-examine on an affidavit or to file responding or
  rebuttal evidence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dismissal of motion

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Court shall dismiss the motion if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the issues raised are not suitable for summary
    trial; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a summary trial would not assist in the efficient
    resolution of the action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judgment generally or on issue

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the Court is satisfied that there is sufficient
  evidence for adjudication, regardless of the amounts involved, the
  complexities of the issues and the existence of conflicting evidence,
  the Court may grant judgment either generally or on an issue, unless
  the Court is of the opinion that it would be unjust to decide the
  issues on the motion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order disposing of action

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} On granting judgment, the Court may make any order
  necessary for the disposition of the action, including an order

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} directing a trial to determine the amount to which
    the moving party is entitled or a reference under rule 153 to
    determine that amount;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} imposing terms respecting the enforcement of the
    judgment; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} awarding costs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trial or specially managed proceeding

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If the motion for summary trial is dismissed in whole
  or in part, the Court may order the action, or the issues in the
  action not disposed of by summary trial, to proceed to trial or order
  that the action be conducted as a specially managed proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-331, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-216-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 216"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014216 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of plaintiff who obtains judgment

**[[217]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217 .sectionLabel}** A plaintiff who obtains
judgment under rule 215 or 216 may proceed against the same defendant
for any other relief and may proceed against any other defendant for the
same or any other relief.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-331, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-217-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 217"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Court

**[[218]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-218 .sectionLabel}** If judgment under rule
215 or 216 is refused or is granted only in part, the Court may make an
order specifying which material facts are not in dispute and defining
the issues to be tried and may also make an order

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} for payment into court of all or part of the claim;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} for security for costs; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} limiting the nature and scope of the examination for
  discovery to matters not covered by the affidavits filed on the motion
  for summary judgment or summary trial or by any cross-examination on
  them and providing for their use at trial in the same manner as an
  examination for discovery.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-331, s. 3
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 10(F)](#1331694-1336703){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331694-1336703.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-218-20091210.html "Link to previous version of section 218"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay of execution

**[[219]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-219 .sectionLabel}** On granting judgment
under rule 215 or 216, the Court may order that enforcement of the
judgment be stayed pending the determination of any other issue in the
action or in a counterclaim or third party claim.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-331, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-219-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 219"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Questions of Law]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014232 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preliminary determination of question of
law or admissibility

- **[[220]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-220 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party may bring a motion before trial to request that the Court
  determine

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a question of law that may be relevant to an
    action;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a question as to the admissibility of any document,
    exhibit or other evidence; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} questions stated by the parties in the form of a
    special case before, or in lieu of, the trial of the action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of determination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, on a motion under subsection (1), the Court
  orders that a question be determined, it shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} give directions as to the case on which the
    question shall be argued;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} fix time limits for the filing and service of
    motion records by the parties; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} fix a time and place for argument of the question.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination final

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A determination of a question referred to in
  subsection (1) is final and conclusive for the purposes of the action,
  subject to being varied on appeal.

### [Striking Out Pleadings]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014246 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion to strike

- **[[221]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-221 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  motion, the Court may, at any time, order that a pleading, or anything
  contained therein, be struck out, with or without leave to amend, on
  the ground that it

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} discloses no reasonable cause of action or defence,
    as the case may be,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is immaterial or redundant,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is scandalous, frivolous or vexatious,

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} may prejudice or delay the fair trial of the
    action,

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} constitutes a departure from a previous pleading,
    or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} is otherwise an abuse of the process of the Court,

  and may order the action be dismissed or judgment entered accordingly.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No evidence shall be heard on a motion for an order
  under paragraph (1)(a).

### [Discovery and Inspection]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014259 .Subheading}

#### [Discovery of Documents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014260 .Subheading}

Definition of [[document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

- **[[222]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  rules 223 to 232 and 295, [[document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes an
  audio recording, a video recording, a film, a photograph, a chart, a
  graph, a map, a plan, a survey and a book of account, as well as data
  that is recorded or stored on any medium in or by a computer system or
  other similar device and that can be read or perceived by a person or
  a computer system or other similar device.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of rules 223 to 232 and 295, a
  document of a party is relevant if the party intends to rely on it or
  if the document tends to adversely affect the party's case or to
  support another party's case.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 17
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-222-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 222"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for service of affidavit of
documents

- **[[223]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-223
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every party shall serve an affidavit
  of documents on every other party within 30 days after the close of
  pleadings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An affidavit of documents shall be in Form 223 and
  shall contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} separate lists and descriptions of all relevant
    documents that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} are in the possession, power or control of the
      party and for which no privilege is claimed,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} are or were in the possession, power or control
      of the party and for which privilege is claimed,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} were but are no longer in the possession, power
      or control of the party and for which no privilege is claimed, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the party believes are in the possession, power
      or control of a person who is not a party to the action;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a statement of the grounds for each claim of
    privilege in respect of a document;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of how the party lost possession,
    power or control of any document and its current location, as far as
    the party can determine;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the identity of each person referred to in
    subparagraph (a)(iv), including the person's name and address, if
    known;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a statement that the party is not aware of any
    relevant document, other than those that are listed in the affidavit
    or are or were in the possession, power or control of another party
    to the action; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} an indication of the time and place at which the
    documents referred to in subparagraph (a)(i) may be inspected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Document within party's power or
  control

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (2), a document shall
  be considered to be within a party's power or control if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the party is entitled to obtain the original
    document or a copy of it; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} no adverse party is so entitled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Bundle of documents

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A party may treat a bundle of documents as a single
  document for the purposes of an affidavit of documents if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the documents are all of the same nature; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the bundle is described in sufficient detail to
    enable another party to clearly ascertain its contents.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deponent of affidavit of documents

- **[[224]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-224 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  deponent of an affidavit of documents shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the party is an individual who is not under a
    legal disability, the party;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the party is an individual under a legal
    disability, a person appointed under rule 115;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the party is a corporation or an
    unincorporated association, an authorized representative of the
    corporation or association; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the party is the Crown, an authorized
    representative of the Crown.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligations of deponent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The deponent of an affidavit of documents shall,
  before making the affidavit, become informed by making reasonable
  inquiries of any present or former officer, servant, agent or employee
  of the party, including any who are outside Canada, who might
  reasonably be expected to have knowledge relating to any matter in
  question in the action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligations of solicitor

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The solicitor of record for a party shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} explain to the deponent of an affidavit of
    documents the necessity of making full disclosure under rule 223 and
    the possible consequences of failing to do so; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} certify on the affidavit of documents or on a
    document attached to it that those explanations have been given.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order for disclosure

**[[225]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-225 .sectionLabel}** On motion, the Court
may order a party to disclose in an affidavit of documents all relevant
documents that are in the possession, power or control of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the party is an individual, any corporation
  that is controlled directly or indirectly by the party; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the party is a corporation,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any corporation that is controlled directly or
    indirectly by the party,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any corporation or individual that directly or
    indirectly controls the party, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any corporation that is controlled directly or
    indirectly by a person who also directly or indirectly controls the
    party.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Need for continuing disclosure

- **[[226]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party who becomes aware that its affidavit of documents is inaccurate
  or deficient shall, without delay, serve a supplementary affidavit of
  documents correcting the inaccuracy or deficiency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A document produced and marked as an exhibit on an
  examination need not be included in a supplementary affidavit of
  documents.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sanctions

**[[227]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227 .sectionLabel}** On motion, where the
Court is satisfied that an affidavit of documents is inaccurate or
deficient, the Court may inspect any document that may be relevant and
may order that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the deponent of the affidavit be cross-examined;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an accurate or complete affidavit be served and
  filed;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} all or part of the pleadings of the party on behalf
  of whom the affidavit was made be struck out; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} that the party on behalf of whom the affidavit was
  made pay costs.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection of documents

- **[[228]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-228
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to rule 230, a party who has
  served an affidavit of documents on another party shall, during
  business hours, allow the other party to inspect and, where
  practicable, to copy any document referred to in the affidavit that is
  not privileged, if the document is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the possession of the party; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the power or control of the party and the other
    party requests that it be made available because the other party
    cannot otherwise inspect or copy it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies of documents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party who has served an affidavit of documents on
  another party shall, at the request of the other party, deliver to the
  other party a copy of any document referred to in subsection (1), if
  the other party pays the cost of the copies and of their delivery.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order for production and inspection

**[[229]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-229 .sectionLabel}** On motion, the Court
may order the production for inspection and copying by a party of any
document referred to in subsection 228(1), at a time and place and in a
manner set out in the order.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Relief from production

**[[230]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-230 .sectionLabel}** On motion, the Court
may relieve a party from production for inspection of any document,
having regard to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the issues in the case and the order in which they
  are likely to be resolved; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} whether it would be unduly onerous to require the
  person to produce the document.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure or production not admission

**[[231]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231 .sectionLabel}** The disclosure of a
document or its production for inspection does not constitute an
admission of its authenticity or admissibility in the action.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Undisclosed or privileged document

- **[[232]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-232
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise or
  discovery of documents has been waived by the parties, no document
  shall be used in evidence unless it has been

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} disclosed on a party's affidavit of documents as a
    document for which no privilege has been claimed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} produced for inspection by a party, or a person
    examined on behalf of one of the parties, on or subsequent to
    examinations for discovery; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} produced by a witness who is not, in the opinion of
    the Court, under control of the party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to a document that is
  used solely as a foundation for, or as a part of a question in,
  cross-examination or re-examination.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-176, s. 3(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-232-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 232"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Production from non-party with leave

- **[[233]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-233 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  motion, the Court may order the production of any document that is in
  the possession of a person who is not a party to the action, if the
  document is relevant and its production could be compelled at trial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal service on non-party

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notice of a motion for an order under subsection (1)
  shall be personally served on the person who is in possession of the
  document.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preparation of certified copy

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Court may, in an order under subsection (1), give
  directions for the preparation of a certified copy of the document to
  be used instead of the original.

#### [Examinations for Discovery]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014354 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Both oral and written examination

- **[[234]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-234 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party may conduct an examination for discovery by way of both an oral
  and a written examination only with leave of the Court or with the
  consent of the person being examined and all other parties entitled to
  examine that person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oral examination by two or more parties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where two or more parties are entitled to examine a
  person, the examination for discovery shall be by way of an oral
  examination, except with leave of the Court or with the consent of the
  person being examined and all other parties entitled to examine that
  person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Single examination

**[[235]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-235 .sectionLabel}** Except with leave of
the Court, a party may examine for discovery any adverse party only
once.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When examination may be initiated

- **[[236]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-236
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a party
  may examine an adverse party for discovery only if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the pleadings are closed and the examining party
    has served its affidavit of documents;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the pleadings are closed and the adverse party
    consents to the examination being conducted before the examining
    party has served its affidavit of documents; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the adverse party is in default of serving and
    filing its pleadings and leave of the Court has been obtained.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination by defendant

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), a defendant may examine a
  plaintiff at any time after the statement of claim is filed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multiple defendants

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where two or more defendants are represented by the
  same solicitor, none of them may examine the plaintiff before filing a
  defence unless all of them examine the plaintiff at the same time.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representative selected

- **[[237]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-237 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  corporation, partnership or unincorporated association that is to be
  examined for discovery shall select a representative to be examined on
  its behalf.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination of Crown

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Crown is to be examined for discovery, the
  Attorney General of Canada shall select a representative to be
  examined on its behalf.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order for substitution

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Court may, on the motion of a party entitled to
  examine a person selected under subsection (1) or (2), order that some
  other person be examined.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination of assignee

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where an assignee is a party to an action, the
  assignor may also be examined for discovery.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination of trustee in bankruptcy

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where a trustee in bankruptcy is a party to an
  action, the bankrupt may also be examined for discovery.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination of party under legal
  disability

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If a party intends to examine for discovery a person
  who is appointed under paragraph 115(1)(b) to represent a person under
  a legal disability, the party may, with leave of the Court, also
  examine the person under a legal disability.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination of nominal party

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Where a party intends to examine for discovery a
  person bringing or defending an action on behalf of another person who
  is not a party, with leave of the Court, the party may also examine
  that other person.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-18, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-237-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 237"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination of non-parties with leave

- **[[238]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party to an action may bring a motion for leave to examine for
  discovery any person not a party to the action, other than an expert
  witness for a party, who might have information on an issue in the
  action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal service on non-party

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On a motion under subsection (1), the notice of
  motion shall be served on the other parties and personally served on
  the person to be examined.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where Court may grant leave

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Court may, on a motion under subsection (1),
  grant leave to examine a person and determine the time and manner of
  conducting the examination, if it is satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person may have information on an issue in the
    action;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the party has been unable to obtain the information
    informally from the person or from another source by any other
    reasonable means;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} it would be unfair not to allow the party an
    opportunity to question the person before trial; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the questioning will not cause undue delay,
    inconvenience or expense to the person or to the other parties.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expenses of person examined

- **[[239]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise, a
  party who is granted leave to examine a person under rule 238 shall
  pay to the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at least 10 days before the day of the examination,
    an amount sufficient to cover reasonable travel expenses; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} within 10 days after the examination, an amount
    sufficient to cover any reasonable travel expenses in excess of the
    initial payment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assistance of solicitor

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person being examined under rule 238 is entitled to
  be assisted by a solicitor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs of solicitor

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On motion, the Court may, in special circumstances,
  order that the costs of a solicitor assisting a person to be examined
  under rule 238 be included in the amounts paid under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Questioning by other parties

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person being examined under rule 238 may also be
  questioned by any other party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cross-examination or hearsay

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A person being examined under rule 238 shall not be
  cross-examined and shall not be required to give hearsay evidence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use as evidence at trial

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The testimony of a person who was examined under rule
  238 shall not be used as evidence at trial but, if the person is a
  witness at trial, it may be used in cross-examination in the same
  manner as any written statement of a witness.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of examination

**[[240]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240 .sectionLabel}** A person being examined
for discovery shall answer, to the best of the person's knowledge,
information and belief, any question that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is relevant to any unadmitted allegation of fact in a
  pleading filed by the party being examined or by the examining party;
  or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} concerns the name or address of any person, other
  than an expert witness, who might reasonably be expected to have
  knowledge relating to a matter in question in the action.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation to inform self

**[[241]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241 .sectionLabel}** Subject to paragraph
242(1)(d), a person who is to be examined for discovery, other than a
person examined under rule 238, shall, before the examination, become
informed by making inquiries of any present or former officer, servant,
agent or employee of the party, including any who are outside Canada,
who might be expected to have knowledge relating to any matter in
question in the action.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objections permitted

- **[[242]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-242 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person may object to a question asked in an examination for discovery
  on the ground that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the answer is privileged;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the question is not relevant to any unadmitted
    allegation of fact in a pleading filed by the party being examined
    or by the examining party;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the question is unreasonable or unnecessary; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} it would be unduly onerous to require the person to
    make the inquiries referred to in rule 241.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objections not permitted

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person other than a person examined under rule 238
  may not object to a question asked in an examination for discovery on
  the ground that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the answer would be evidence or hearsay;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the question constitutes cross-examination.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limit on examination

**[[243]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-243 .sectionLabel}** On motion, the Court
may limit an examination for discovery that it considers to be
oppressive, vexatious or unnecessary.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examined party to be better informed

- **[[244]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-244
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a person being examined for
  discovery, other than a person examined under rule 238, is unable to
  answer a question, the examining party may require the person to
  become better informed and may conclude the examination, subject to
  obtaining answers to any remaining questions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further answers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person being examined who is required to become
  better informed shall provide the information sought by the examining
  party by submitting to a continuation of the oral examination for
  discovery in respect of the information or, where the parties agree,
  by providing the information in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information deemed part of examination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Information provided under subsection (2) is deemed
  to be part of the examination for discovery.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inaccurate or deficient answer

- **[[245]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-245 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who was examined for discovery and who discovers that the
  answer to a question in the examination is no longer correct or
  complete shall, without delay, provide the examining party with the
  corrected or completed information in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further examination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An examining party may require a person providing
  information under subsection (1) to continue the examination for
  discovery in respect of that information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corrections deemed part of examination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Information provided under subsection (1) is deemed
  to be part of the examination for discovery.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Answer by solicitor

- **[[246]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  solicitor of a person being examined for discovery orally may answer a
  question on behalf of the person during the examination, unless the
  examining party objects.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed answer of person examined

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An answer given by a solicitor under subsection (1)
  is deemed to be the answer of the person being examined for discovery.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Divided discovery

**[[247]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-247 .sectionLabel}** Where

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an order was made under rule 153 that an issue of
  fact be the subject of a reference after trial, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order was made under rule 107 that an issue in the
  action be determined separately,

any subsequent examination for discovery or inspection of documents
shall not extend to that issue, unless otherwise ordered by the Court.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Undisclosed information inadmissible at
trial

**[[248]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248 .sectionLabel}** Where a party examined
for discovery, or a person examined for discovery on behalf of a party,
has refused, on the ground of privilege or for any other reason, to
answer a proper question and has not subsequently answered the question,
the party may not introduce the information sought by the question at
trial without leave of the Court.

#### [Inspection of Property]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014460 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order for inspection

- **[[249]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  motion, where the Court is satisfied that it is necessary or expedient
  for the purpose of obtaining information or evidence in full, the
  Court may order, in respect of any property that is the subject-matter
  of an action or as to which a question may arise therein, that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a sample be taken of the property;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an inspection be made of the property; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an experiment be tried on or with the property.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entry on land or building

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (1) may authorize a
  person to enter any land or building where the property is located for
  the purpose of enabling the order to be carried out.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal service on non-party

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a motion is brought under subsection (1) for an
  order in respect of property that is in the possession of a person who
  is not a party to the action, that person shall be personally served
  with notice of the motion.

#### [Medical Examination of Parties]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014471 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order for medical examination

- **[[250]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  an action for damages for personal injuries, the Court may, on motion,
  order the injured person to submit to a medical examination at a place
  and by a medical practitioner appointed by the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Who may attend examination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who is required to undergo a medical
  examination under this rule is entitled to have a solicitor, medical
  advisor or person appointed under rule 115, or all of them, present at
  the examination, but no other person, other than the person being
  examined and the medical practitioner authorized to conduct the
  examination, shall be present during the examination, except with
  leave of the Court or with the consent of the parties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of examination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A medical practitioner who is examining a person
  under this rule may, in connection with that examination, ask the
  person any question that may be relevant to the purpose of the
  examination, and any statement made by the person during such an
  examination is admissible in evidence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sanctions for plaintiff's failure to
  comply

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a plaintiff fails, without reasonable excuse, to
  comply with an order under subsection (1) or to answer any questions
  referred to in subsection (3), the Court may dismiss the action.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further medical examination

**[[251]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-251 .sectionLabel}** On motion, the Court
may order further medical examinations in accordance with rule 250, on
such terms as it considers just.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Medical report

- **[[252]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-252
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After conducting a medical
  examination under rule 250, the medical practitioner shall prepare a
  written report setting out his or her observations, the results of any
  tests made and his or her conclusions, diagnosis and prognosis and
  forthwith provide the report to the party who obtained the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of medical report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party who obtains an order under rule 250 shall
  forthwith serve the report obtained pursuant to it on every other
  party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report confidential

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person who receives a medical report under this
  rule shall treat it as confidential and use it only for the purposes
  of the action.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Medical practitioner as witness

**[[253]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-253 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court orders
otherwise, a medical practitioner who has made an examination under an
order made under subsection 250(1) may, subject to rule 279, be called
as a witness at trial.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs of medical examination

**[[254]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-254 .sectionLabel}** The Court may order
that a party seeking an order under subsection 250(1) or rule 251 pay to
the person to be examined or to a representative of the person appointed
under rule 115 all necessary expenses of attending the examination.

### [Admissions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014494 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request to admit fact or document

**[[255]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-255 .sectionLabel}** A party may, after
pleadings have been closed, request that another party admit a fact or
the authenticity of a document by serving a request to admit, in Form
255, on that party.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of request to admit

**[[256]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-256 .sectionLabel}** A party who is served
with a request to admit is deemed to admit a fact or the authenticity of
a document set out in the request to admit unless that party serves a
response to the request in Form 256 within 20 days after its service and
denies the admission, setting out the grounds for the denial.

### [Pre-Trial]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014499 .Subheading}

#### [Settlement Discussions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014500 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Settlement discussions

**[[257]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257 .sectionLabel}** Within 60 days after
the close of pleadings, the solicitors for the parties shall discuss the
possibility of settling any or all of the issues in the action and of
bringing a motion to refer any unsettled issues to a dispute resolution
conference.

#### [Pre-trial Conferences]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014503 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requisition for pre-trial conference

- **[[258]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-258
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After the close of pleadings, a
  party who is not in default under these Rules or under an order of the
  Court and who is ready for trial may serve and file a requisition for
  a pre-trial conference, accompanied by a pre-trial conference
  memorandum.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of requisition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A requisition for a pre-trial conference shall be in
  Form 258 and include a certification by the solicitor of record that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} all examinations for discovery that the party
    intends to conduct have been completed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} settlement discussions have taken place in
    accordance with rule 257.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of pre-trial conference
  memorandum

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A pre-trial conference memorandum shall contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a concise statement of the nature of the
    proceeding;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any admissions of the party;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the factual and legal contentions of the party; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a statement of the issues to be determined at
    trial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A pre-trial conference memorandum shall be
  accompanied by a copy of all documents that are intended to be used at
  trial that may be of assistance at the pre-trial conference, including
  all affidavits or statements of expert witnesses.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2010-176, s. 4\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-219, s. 2
- SOR/2010-176, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-258-20060921.html "Link to previous version of section 258"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time and place for pre-trial conference

**[[259]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-259 .sectionLabel}** On the filing of a
requisition for a pre-trial conference, the Court shall fix a time, not
more than 60 days thereafter, and place for the pre-trial conference.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Participation at pre-trial conference

**[[260]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-260 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court
directs otherwise, the solicitors of record for the parties and the
parties or their authorized representatives shall participate in a
pre-trial conference.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of pre-trial conference

**[[261]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-261 .sectionLabel}** The Administrator shall
serve a notice of pre-trial conference, in Form 261, on the parties at
least 30 days before the date fixed for the conference.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pre-trial conference memoranda

- **[[262]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-262
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every party, other than the party
  who filed the requisition for a pre-trial conference, shall serve and
  file a pre-trial conference memorandum within 30 days after being
  served with the requisition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objection to expert

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The pre-trial conference memorandum shall include any
  known objection to the requisitioning party's proposed expert witness
  that could disqualify the witness from testifying and the basis for
  the objection.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-219, s. 3
- SOR/2010-176, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-262-20060921.html "Link to previous version of section 262"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of pre-trial conference

**[[263]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-263 .sectionLabel}** Participants at a
pre-trial conference must be prepared to address

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the possibility of settlement of any or all of the
  issues in the action and of referring any unsettled issues to a
  dispute resolution conference;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} simplification of the issues in the action;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} any issues arising from any affidavits or statements
  of expert witnesses, including

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any objection to an opposing party's proposed
    expert witness that could disqualify the witness from testifying and
    the basis for the objection,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any benefit to the litigation in ordering the
    experts to confer with one another in advance of trial in order to
    narrow the issues and identify the points on which their views
    differ, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the need for any additional or rebuttal expert
    witness evidence;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the possibility of obtaining admissions that may
  facilitate the trial;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the issue of liability;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the amount of damages, where damages are claimed;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the estimated duration of the trial;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the advisability of having the Court appoint an
  assessor;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} the advisability of a reference;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} suitable dates for a trial;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} the necessity for interpreters or simultaneous
  interpretation at the trial;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} whether a notice of a constitutional question needs
  to be served under section 57 of the Act;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} the content of the trial record; and

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} any other matter that may promote the timely and just
  disposition of the action.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 15
- SOR/2006-219, s. 4
- SOR/2010-176, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-263-20060921.html "Link to previous version of section 263"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trial date and place

**[[264]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-264 .sectionLabel}** If the date and place
for trial have not already been fixed, the judge or prothonotary who
conducts the pre-trial conference shall do so as soon as practicable
after the pre-trial conference.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 8](#1303076-1305168){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303076-1305168.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-264-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 264"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

- **[[265]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-265 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At a
  pre-trial conference,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a judge may make any order respecting the conduct
    of the action; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a prothonotary may make any order respecting the
    conduct of the action other than an order under a motion referred to
    in any of paragraphs 50(1)(a) to (i).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of expert's affidavit or
  statement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If applicable, the order shall set out the time for
  service of any additional or rebuttal affidavits or statements of
  expert witnesses.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-219, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-265-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 265"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pre-trial judge not to preside at trial

**[[266]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-266 .sectionLabel}** A judge or prothonotary
who conducts a pre-trial conference in an action shall not preside at
the trial of the action unless all parties consent.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No disclosure to the Court

**[[267]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-267 .sectionLabel}** No communication shall
be made to a judge or prothonotary presiding at a trial or hearing, or
on a motion or reference in an action, with respect to any statement
made at a pre-trial conference, except as may be permitted in an order
made at the conclusion of the pre-trial conference or as consented to by
the parties.

#### [Trial Record]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014571 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trial record

**[[268]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-268 .sectionLabel}** The plaintiff, or any
other party so directed by the Court at a pre-trial conference, shall
serve and file a trial record not later than 40 days before the date
fixed for trial.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of trial record

**[[269]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-269 .sectionLabel}** A trial record shall
contain the pleadings, any particulars, all orders and directions
respecting the trial and any other filed document that is necessary for
the conduct of the trial.

#### [Trial Management Conference]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014576 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of trial management conference

**[[270]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-270 .sectionLabel}** Notwithstanding rule
266, a judge or prothonotary before whom an action has been set down for
trial may, without being disqualified from presiding at the trial, hold
a conference, either before or during the trial, to consider any matter
that may assist in the just and timely disposition of the action.

#### [Taking of Trial Evidence out of Court]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014579 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence taken out of court

- **[[271]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-271 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  motion, the Court may order the examination for trial of a person out
  of court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Considerations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In making an order under subsection (1), the Court
  may consider

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the expected absence of the person at the time of
    trial;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the age or any infirmity of the person;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the distance the person resides from the place of
    trial; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the expense of having the person attend at trial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions regarding taking evidence
  before trial

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In an order under subsection (1), or on the
  subsequent motion of a party, the Court may give directions regarding
  the time, place, manner and costs of the examination, notice to be
  given to the person being examined and to other parties, the
  attendance of witnesses and the production of requested documents or
  material.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further examination

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On motion, the Court may order the further
  examination, before the Court or before a person designated by the
  Court, of any witness examined under subsection (1), and if such an
  examination is not conducted, the Court may refuse to admit the
  evidence of that witness.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commission for examination outside Canada

- **[[272]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-272
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where an examination under rule 271
  is to be made outside Canada, the Court may order the issuance of a
  commission under the seal of the Court, letters rogatory, a letter of
  request or any other document necessary for the examination in Form
  272A, 272B or 272C, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination outside Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person authorized under subsection (1) to take the
  examination of a witness in a jurisdiction outside Canada shall,
  unless the parties agree otherwise or the Court orders otherwise, take
  the examination in a manner that is binding on the witness under the
  law of that jurisdiction.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of evidence at trial

**[[273]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court orders
otherwise, evidence obtained on an examination under subsection 271(1)
or (4) may, without further proof, be used in evidence by any party.

### [Trial Procedure]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014600 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014601 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order of presentation

- **[[274]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-274
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), at the
  trial of an action, unless the Court directs otherwise,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the plaintiff shall make an opening address and
    then adduce evidence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when the plaintiff's evidence is concluded, the
    defendant shall make an opening address and then adduce evidence;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} when the defendant's evidence is concluded, the
    plaintiff may adduce reply evidence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multiple parties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Court has made an order permitting two or
  more plaintiffs to put in separate cases, or where more than one
  defendant is separately represented, the order of presentation shall
  be as directed by the Court.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions re proof or evidence

**[[275]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-275 .sectionLabel}** The Court may give
directions at trial concerning the method of proving a fact or of
adducing evidence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exhibits

**[[276]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-276 .sectionLabel}** All exhibits adduced in
evidence shall be marked and numbered.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection by Court

**[[277]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-277 .sectionLabel}** The Court may, in the
presence of solicitors for the parties, inspect any place or thing in
respect of which a question may arise at trial.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order of argument

- **[[278]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-278
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court directs otherwise,
  the parties shall be heard in argument, after all parties have been
  given full opportunity to put in their respective cases, in the order
  in which they adduced evidence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of reply

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party shall have a right of reply to the arguments
  of adverse parties and, if the party raises a new point of law, an
  adverse party may answer on that point.

#### [Expert Witnesses]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014621 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Admissibility of expert's evidence

**[[279]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-279 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court orders
otherwise, no expert witness's evidence is admissible at the trial of an
action in respect of any issue unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the issue has been defined by the pleadings or in an
  order made under rule 265;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an affidavit or statement of the expert witness
  prepared in accordance with rule 52.2 has been served in accordance
  with subsection 258(1), rule 262 or an order made under rule 265; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the expert witness is available at the trial for
  cross-examination.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-219, s. 6
- SOR/2010-176, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-279-20060921.html "Link to previous version of section 279"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tendering of expert's evidence at trial

- **[[280]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-280
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise,
  evidence in chief of an expert witness may be tendered at trial by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the witness reading into evidence all or part of an
    affidavit or statement referred to in paragraph 279(b); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the witness explaining any of the content of an
    affidavit or statement that has been read into evidence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other evidence with leave

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), an expert witness may
  tender other evidence in chief with leave of the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Affidavit taken as read

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} With leave of the Court, all or part of an affidavit
  or statement referred to in paragraph 279(b) may be taken as read into
  evidence by the witness.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition on pre-trial
  cross-examination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Except with leave of the Court, there shall be no
  cross-examination before trial on an affidavit or statement referred
  to in paragraph 279(b).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-219, s. 7
- SOR/2010-176, s. 8
- SOR/2013-18, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-280-20100803.html "Link to previous version of section 280"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[281]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-281 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/2006-219, s. 8\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-281-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 281"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Evidence at Trial]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014643 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination of witnesses

- **[[282]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-282
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise,
  witnesses at trial shall be examined orally and in open court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Witnesses to testify under oath

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} All witnesses shall testify under oath.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expert witness panel

**[[282.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-282.1 .sectionLabel}** The Court may
require that some or all of the expert witnesses testify as a panel
after the completion of the testimony of the non-expert witnesses of
each party or at any other time that the Court may determine.

::: HistoricalNote
