- SOR/2010-176, s. 9
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Testimony of panel members

- **[[282.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-282.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Expert witnesses shall give their
  views and may be directed to comment on the views of other panel
  members and to make concluding statements. With leave of the Court,
  they may pose questions to other panel members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination of panel members

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On completion of the testimony of the panel, the
  panel members may be cross-examined and re-examined in the sequence
  directed by Court.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-176, s. 9
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpreter

**[[283]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-283 .sectionLabel}** Rule 93 applies, with
such modifications as are necessary, to the use of an interpreter at
trial.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to appear

- **[[284]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-284
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where on the day of a trial, a party
  who intends to call witnesses does not produce them or justify their
  absence, the Court may declare the party's proof closed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjournment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), where a party demonstrates
  due diligence and the Court is satisfied that an absent witness is
  necessary and that the absence of the witness is not due to any
  contrivance on the party's part, the Court may adjourn the hearing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Avoidance of adjournment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An adverse party may require a party seeking an
  adjournment under subsection (2) to declare, or to produce some other
  person to declare, under oath the facts that, in the opinion of the
  party seeking the adjournment, the defaulting witness would have
  stated, and may avoid the adjournment by admitting the truth of those
  facts or that the witness would have stated those facts.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof by affidavit

**[[285]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-285 .sectionLabel}** The Court may, at any
time, order that any fact be proven by affidavit or that the affidavit
of a witness be read at trial.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order re giving evidence

**[[286]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-286 .sectionLabel}** The Court may, before
trial, order that evidence of any fact be given at the trial in such a
manner as may be specified in the order, including

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} by statement on oath of information or belief;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} by the production of documents or other material;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} by the production of copies of documents; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a fact that is or was a matter of
  common knowledge either generally or in a particular district, by the
  production of a specified publication containing a statement of that
  fact.

#### [Demonstrative Evidence]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014677 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Admissibility

**[[287]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-287 .sectionLabel}** Except with leave of
the Court, no plan, photograph, model or other demonstrative evidence
prepared or obtained for use at trial is admissible in evidence at
trial, other than in the course of cross-examination, unless at least 30
days before the commencement of the trial all other parties have been
given an opportunity to inspect it and consent to its admission without
further proof.

#### [Use of Examination for Discovery at Trial]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1014680 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reading in examination at trial

**[[288]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-288 .sectionLabel}** A party may introduce
as its own evidence at trial any part of its examination for discovery
of an adverse party or of a person examined on behalf of an adverse
party, whether or not the adverse party or person has already testified.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualifying answers

**[[289]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-289 .sectionLabel}** The Court may order a
party who uses part of an examination for discovery as its own evidence
to introduce into evidence any other part of the examination for
discovery that the Court considers is so related that it ought not to be
omitted.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unavailability of deponent

**[[290]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-290 .sectionLabel}** The Court may permit a
party to use all or part of an examination for discovery of a person,
other than a person examined under rule 238, as evidence at trial if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person is unable to testify at the trial because
  of his or her illness, infirmity or death or because the person cannot
  be compelled to attend; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} his or her evidence cannot be obtained on commission.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of examination to impeach credibility
at trial

**[[291]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-291 .sectionLabel}** A party may use any
part of its examination for discovery of a person as evidence to impeach
the credibility of that person as a witness at trial only if the party
first puts to the person the questions asked in that part of the
examination.

### [Simplified Action]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014691 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where mandatory

**[[292]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-292 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court orders
otherwise, rules 294 to 299 apply to any action in which

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} each claim is exclusively for monetary relief in an
  amount not exceeding \$100,000, exclusive of interest and costs;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an action [in rem]{.otherLang
  lang="la"} claiming monetary relief, no amount claimed, exclusive of
  interest and costs, exceeds \$50,000;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the parties agree that the action is to be conducted
  as a simplified action; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} on motion, the Court orders that the action be
  conducted as a simplified action.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-150, s. 7](#1302234-1305078){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1302234-1305078.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-292-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 292"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cost consequences of improper avoidance
of procedure

**[[293]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-293 .sectionLabel}** The Court may award
costs against any party, including a party who is successful in an
action, who it finds has exaggerated a claim, including a counterclaim
or third party claim, merely to avoid the operation of rules 292 and 294
to 299.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Style of cause

**[[294]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-294 .sectionLabel}** Every pleading in a
simplified action shall be prefaced by the heading "Simplified Action".

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List of documents

**[[295]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-295 .sectionLabel}** A party to a simplified
action may serve, in lieu of an affidavit of documents, a complete list
of all the documents in the party's possession, power or control that
are relevant to a matter in issue in the action.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limited examination for discovery

**[[296]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-296 .sectionLabel}** An examination for
discovery in a simplified action shall be in writing only, and shall not
exceed 50 questions.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion for summary judgment or summary
trial

**[[297]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-297 .sectionLabel}** No motion for summary
judgment or summary trial may be brought in a simplified action.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-331, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-297-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 297"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motions prior to pre-trial conference

- **[[298]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-298
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  a motion in a simplified action shall be returnable only at a
  pre-trial conference conducted in accordance with rules 258 to 267.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A motion may be brought, within the time set out in
  rule 204 for the service and filing of a statement of defence,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to object to the jurisdiction of the Court; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to strike a statement of claim, on the ground that
    it discloses no reasonable cause of action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A motion may be brought at any time

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to remove an action from the operation of rules 294
    to 299;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the release of arrested property in an action
    [in rem]{.otherLang lang="la"}; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for a default judgment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence adduced by affidavit

- **[[299]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-299 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  the trial of a simplified action, unless the Court directs otherwise,
  the evidence of each party shall be adduced by affidavit, which shall,
  subject to subsections (1.1) and (1.2), be served and filed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of evidence of a plaintiff, at least 20
    days before the trial; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of evidence of a defendant, at least 10
    days before the trial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Admissibility of expert's evidence

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise, no evidence in
  chief of an expert witness is admissible at the trial of an action in
  respect of any issue unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the issue has been defined by the pleadings or in
    an order made under rule 265;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an affidavit or statement of the expert witness
    prepared in accordance with rule 52.2 has been served on all other
    parties at least 60 days before the commencement of the trial; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the expert witness is available at the trial for
    cross-examination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Admissibility of rebuttal evidence

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} Except with leave of the Court, no expert witness's
  evidence to rebut evidence in an affidavit or statement served under
  paragraph (1.1)(b) is admissible unless an affidavit or statement of
  the expert witness prepared in accordance with rule 52.2 has been
  served on all other parties at least 30 days before the commencement
  of the trial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Witness to be made available

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless all adverse parties agree otherwise, a witness
  whose affidavit evidence is tendered at trial shall be made available
  for cross-examination at trial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reply evidence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Reply evidence, including that of an expert witness,
  may be provided orally at trial.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-219, s. 9
- SOR/2010-176, s. 10
:::

## [PART 5]{.HLabel1}[Applications]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1014775 .Part}

### [Application of this Part]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014776 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[300]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-300 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} applications for judicial review of administrative
  action, including applications under section 18.1 or 28 of the Act,
  unless the Court directs under subsection 18.4(2) of the Act that the
  application be treated and proceeded with as an action;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} proceedings required or permitted by or under an Act
  of Parliament to be brought by application, motion, originating notice
  of motion, originating summons or petition or to be determined in a
  summary way, other than applications under subsection 33(1) of the
  [Marine Liability Act](/eng/acts/M-0.7);

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2021-151, s. 9\]]{.Repealed}

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} appeals under section 56 of the [Trademarks
  Act](/eng/acts/T-13);

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} references from a tribunal under rule 320;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} requests under the Commercial Arbitration Code
  brought pursuant to subsection 324(1);

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} proceedings transferred to the Court under subsection
  3(3) or 5(3) of the [Divorce Act](/eng/acts/D-3.4); and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} applications for registration of a foreign judgment
  or recognition and enforcement of an arbitral award under rule 327.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 18(E)
- SOR/2004-283, s. 37
- [2014, c. 20, s. 366(E)](#1044114-1146039){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1044114-1146039.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 9](#1303078-1305170){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303078-1305170.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2021-245, s. 2](#1333991-1336768){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1333991-1336768.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-300-20210617.html "Link to previous version of section 300"){.PITLink}
:::

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014789 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of application

**[[301]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-301 .sectionLabel}** An application shall be
commenced by a notice of application in Form 301, setting out

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the court to which the application is
  addressed;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the names of the applicant and respondent;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the application is an application for judicial
  review,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the tribunal in respect of which the application is
    made, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the date and details of any order in respect of
    which judicial review is sought and the date on which it was first
    communicated to the applicant;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a precise statement of the relief sought;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} a complete and concise statement of the grounds
  intended to be argued, including a reference to any statutory
  provision or rule to be relied on; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} a list of the documentary evidence to be used at the
  hearing of the application.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 36
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limited to single order

**[[302]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-302 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court orders
otherwise, an application for judicial review shall be limited to a
single order in respect of which relief is sought.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Respondents

- **[[303]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-303
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), an
  applicant shall name as a respondent every person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} directly affected by the order sought in the
    application, other than a tribunal in respect of which the
    application is brought; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} required to be named as a party under an Act of
    Parliament pursuant to which the application is brought.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for judicial review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where in an application for judicial review there are
  no persons that can be named under subsection (1), the applicant shall
  name the Attorney General of Canada as a respondent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Substitution for Attorney General

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On a motion by the Attorney General of Canada, where
  the Court is satisfied that the Attorney General is unable or
  unwilling to act as a respondent after having been named under
  subsection (2), the Court may substitute another person or body,
  including the tribunal in respect of which the application is made, as
  a respondent in the place of the Attorney General of Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of notice of application

- **[[304]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-304
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court directs otherwise,
  within 10 days after the issuance of a notice of application, the
  applicant shall serve it on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} all respondents;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an application for judicial review or
    an application appealing the order of a tribunal,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an application other than one
      relating to a decision of a visa officer, the tribunal in respect
      of which the application is brought,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any other person who participated in the
      proceeding before the tribunal in respect of which the application
      is made, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the Attorney General of Canada;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the application is made under the [Access to
    Information Act](/eng/acts/A-1), Part 1 of the [Personal Information
    Protection and Electronic Documents Act](/eng/acts/P-8.6), the
    [Privacy Act](/eng/acts/P-21) or the [Official Languages
    Act](/eng/acts/O-3.01), the Commissioner named for the purposes of
    that Act; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other person required to be served under an Act
    of Parliament pursuant to which the application is brought.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion for directions as to service

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where there is any uncertainty as to who are the
  appropriate persons to be served with a notice of application, the
  applicant may bring an [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} motion for
  directions to the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of service

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Proof of service of a notice of application shall be
  filed within 10 days after service of the notice of application.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of appearance

**[[305]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-305 .sectionLabel}** A respondent who
intends to appear in respect of an application shall, within 10 days
after being served with a notice of application, serve and file a notice
of appearance in Form 305.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-18, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-305-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 305"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicant's affidavits

**[[306]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-306 .sectionLabel}** Within 30 days after
issuance of a notice of application, an applicant shall serve its
supporting affidavits and documentary exhibits and file proof of
service. The affidavits and exhibits are deemed to be filed when the
proof of service is filed in the Registry.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 12(F)
- SOR/2010-177, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-306-20071213.html "Link to previous version of section 306"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Respondent's affidavits

**[[307]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-307 .sectionLabel}** Within 30 days after
service of the applicant's affidavits, a respondent shall serve its
supporting affidavits and documentary exhibits and shall file proof of
service. The affidavits and exhibits are deemed to be filed when the
proof of service is filed in the Registry.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 12(F)
- SOR/2010-177, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-307-20071213.html "Link to previous version of section 307"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cross-examinations

**[[308]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-308 .sectionLabel}** Cross-examination on
affidavits must be completed by all parties within 20 days after the
filing of the respondent's affidavits or the expiration of the time for
doing so, whichever is earlier.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicant's record

- **[[309]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-309 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  applicant shall serve and file the applicant's record within 20 days
  after the day on which the parties' cross-examinations are completed
  or within 20 days after the day on which the time for those
  cross-examinations is expired, whichever day is earlier.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Number of copies

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The applicant shall file

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the application is brought in the Federal Court,
    an electronic copy of or, subject to rule 72.4, three paper copies
    of the record; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the application is brought in the Federal Court
    of Appeal, an electronic copy of or, subject to rule 72.4, five
    paper copies of the record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of applicant's record

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An applicant's record shall contain, on consecutively
  numbered pages and in the following order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a table of contents giving the nature and date of
    each document in the record;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the notice of application;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any order in respect of which the application is
    made and any reasons, including dissenting reasons, given in respect
    of that order;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} each supporting affidavit and documentary exhibit;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the transcript of any cross-examination on
    affidavits that the applicant has conducted;

  - [(e.1)]{.lawlabel} any material that has been certified by a
    tribunal and transmitted under Rule 318 that is to be used by the
    applicant at the hearing;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the portions of any transcript of oral evidence
    before a tribunal that are to be used by the applicant at the
    hearing;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a description of any physical exhibits to be used
    by the applicant at the hearing; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's memorandum of fact and law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention of original affidavits

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an original affidavit is not filed as part of an
  applicant's record, it shall be retained by the applicant for one year
  after the expiry of all appeal periods.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, ss. 32, 33
- SOR/2006-219, s. 10
- SOR/2010-177, s. 4
- SOR/2013-18, s. 8
- SOR/2015-21, s. 18
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 10](#1303079-1305173){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303079-1305173.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-309-20150130.html "Link to previous version of section 309"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Respondent's record

- **[[310]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-310 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  respondent to an application shall, within 20 days after service of
  the applicant's record, serve and file the respondent's record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Number of copies

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The respondent shall file

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the application is brought in the Federal Court,
    an electronic copy of or, subject to rule 72.4, three paper copies
    of the record; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the application is brought in the Federal Court
    of Appeal, an electronic copy of or, subject to rule 72.4, five
    paper copies of the record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of respondent's record

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The record of a respondent shall contain, on
  consecutively numbered pages and in the following order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a table of contents giving the nature and date of
    each document in the record;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} each supporting affidavit and documentary exhibit;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the transcript of any cross-examination on
    affidavits that the respondent has conducted;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} any material that has been certified by a
    tribunal and transmitted under rule 318 that is to be used by the
    respondent at the hearing and that is not contained in the
    applicant's record in accordance with paragraph 309(2)(e.1);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the portions of any transcript of oral evidence
    before a tribunal that are to be used by the respondent at the
    hearing;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a description of any physical exhibits to be used
    by the respondent at the hearing; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the respondent's memorandum of fact and law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention of original affidavits

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an original affidavit is not filed as part of a
  respondent's record, it shall be retained by the respondent for one
  year after the expiry of all appeal periods.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, ss. 32, 33
- SOR/2010-177, s. 5
- SOR/2013-18, s. 9
- SOR/2015-21, s. 19
- [SOR/2021-150, s. 8](#1302236-1305080){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1302236-1305080.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 11](#1303081-1305176){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303081-1305176.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-310-20150130.html "Link to previous version of section 310"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preparation by Registry

- **[[311]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-311 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  motion, the Court may order the Administrator to prepare a record on a
  party's behalf.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents to be provided

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party bringing a motion for an order under
  subsection (1) shall provide the Administrator with the documents
  referred to in subsection 309(2) or 310(2), as the case may be.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional steps

**[[312]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-312 .sectionLabel}** With leave of the
Court, a party may

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} file affidavits additional to those provided for in
  rules 306 and 307;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} conduct cross-examinations on affidavits additional
  to those provided for in rule 308; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} file a supplementary record.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to file additional material

**[[313]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-313 .sectionLabel}** Where the Court
considers that the application records of the parties are incomplete,
the Court may order that other material, including any portion of a
transcript, be filed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requisition for hearing

- **[[314]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-314 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  applicant shall, within 10 days after service of the respondent's
  record or the expiration of the time for doing so, whichever is
  earlier, serve and file a requisition, in Form 314, requesting that a
  date be set for the hearing of the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of requisition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A requisition referred to in subsection (1) shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} include a statement that the requirements of
    subsection 309(1) have been satisfied and that any notice required
    under section 57 of the Act has been given;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} set out the place at which the hearing should be
    held;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} set out the maximum number of hours or days
    required for the hearing;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} list any dates within the following 90 days on
    which the parties are not available for a hearing;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} set out the name, address, telephone number and fax
    number of the solicitor for every party to the application or, where
    a party is not represented by a solicitor, the person's name,
    address, telephone number and any fax number; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} indicate whether the hearing will be in English or
    French, or partly in English and partly in French, and whether the
    materials in the requisition for hearing file will be in English or
    French, or partly in English and partly in French.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 12](#1303083-1305179){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303083-1305179.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-314-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 314"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pre-hearing conference

**[[315]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-315 .sectionLabel}** The Court may order
that a conference be held in accordance with rules 258 to 267, with such
modifications as are necessary.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Testimony regarding issue of fact

**[[316]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-316 .sectionLabel}** On motion, the Court
may, in special circumstances, authorize a witness to testify in court
in relation to an issue of fact raised in an application.

### [Exceptions to General Procedure]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014912 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"}
proceedings

**[[316.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-316.1 .sectionLabel}** Despite rules 304,
306, 309 and 314, for a proceeding referred to in paragraph 300(b) that
is brought [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"},

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the notice of application, the applicant's record,
  affidavits and documentary exhibits and the requisition for hearing
  are not required to be served; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's record and the requisition for
  hearing must be filed at the time the notice of application is filed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-18, s. 10
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Summary application under [Income Tax
Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3) or [Excise Tax Act](/eng/acts/E-15)

- **[[316.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-316.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except for rule 359, the procedures
  set out in Part 7 apply, with any modifications that are required, to
  a summary application brought under section 231.7 of the [Income Tax
  Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3) or section 289.1 of the [Excise Tax
  Act](/eng/acts/E-15).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commencing the application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application shall be commenced by a notice of
  summary application in Form 316.2.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-18, s. 10
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 13](#1303085-1305182){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303085-1305182.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-316.2-20130208.html "Link to previous version of section 316.2"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Material in the Possession of a Tribunal]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014926 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Material from tribunal

- **[[317]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-317 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party may request material relevant to an application that is in the
  possession of a tribunal whose order is the subject of the application
  and not in the possession of the party by serving on the tribunal and
  filing a written request, identifying the material requested.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request in notice of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An applicant may include a request under
  subsection (1) in its notice of application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of request

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an applicant does not include a request under
  subsection (1) in its notice of application, the applicant shall serve
  the request on the other parties.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 19
- SOR/2006-219, s. 11(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-317-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 317"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Material to be transmitted

- **[[318]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-318
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within 20 days after service of a
  request under rule 317, the tribunal shall transmit

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a certified copy of the requested material to the
    Registry and to the party making the request; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the material cannot be reproduced, the
    original material to the Registry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objection by tribunal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a tribunal or party objects to a request under
  rule 317, the tribunal or the party shall inform all parties and the
  Administrator, in writing, of the reasons for the objection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions as to procedure

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Court may give directions to the parties and to a
  tribunal as to the procedure for making submissions with respect to an
  objection under subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Court may, after hearing submissions with respect
  to an objection under subsection (2), order that a certified copy, or
  the original, of all or part of the material requested be forwarded to
  the Registry.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of material

**[[319]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-319 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court
directs otherwise, after an application has been heard, the
Administrator shall return to a tribunal any original material received
from it under rule 318.

### [References from a Tribunal]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014949 .Subheading}

Definition of [[reference]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

- **[[320]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-320 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  rules 321 to 323, [[reference]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a reference
  to the Court made by a tribunal or by the Attorney General of Canada
  under section 18.3 of the Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedures on applications apply

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to rules 321 to 323, rules 309 to 311 apply
  to references.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of application on reference

**[[321]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-321 .sectionLabel}** A notice of application
in respect of a reference shall set out

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the court to which the application is
  addressed;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the applicant; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the question being referred.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 36
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions on reference

**[[322]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-322 .sectionLabel}** Where the Attorney
General of Canada or a tribunal makes a reference, the Attorney General
or tribunal shall bring an [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} motion for
directions as to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} which persons shall be given notice of the reference;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the material that will constitute the case to be
  determined on the reference;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the preparation, filing and service of copies of the
  material;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the preparation, filing and service of memoranda of
  fact and law;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the procedure for the hearing of the reference;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the time and place for the hearing of the reference;
  and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the role, if any, of the tribunal in question.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of intention to become party

**[[323]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-323 .sectionLabel}** Any of the following
persons may become a party to a reference by serving and filing a notice
of intention to participate in Form 323:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Attorney General of Canada;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the attorney general of a province, for the purpose
  of adducing evidence or making submissions to the Court under
  subsection 57(4) of the Act; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person who participated in the proceeding before
  the tribunal in respect of which the reference is made.

### [Commercial Arbitrations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014976 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of application

- **[[324]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-324
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a request
  under the Commercial Arbitration Code, set out in Schedule 1 to the
  [Commercial Arbitration Act](/eng/acts/C-34.6), shall be brought by a
  notice of application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the subject matter of an arbitration to which a
  request under the Code relates is already the subject matter of a
  proceeding before the Court, the request may be brought as a motion in
  that proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Affidavit

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An affidavit in support of a notice of application
  under subsection (1) or a motion under subsection (2) shall be
  accompanied by a copy of the parties' arbitration agreement and state

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} all material facts;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} unless the request is brought pursuant to article
    8(1) or 9 of the Code, that the arbitration to which the request
    relates is governed by Canadian law or has been, is being or will be
    held within the jurisdiction of the Court; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the request is brought pursuant to article 27
    of the Code, the nature of the evidence to be obtained, the name and
    address of any person to be heard as a witness and the
    subject-matter of any testimony required from that person, and
    describe any document to be produced or property to be inspected.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 26, s. 27
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-324-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 324"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Divorce Proceedings]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014989 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure of province to apply

- **[[325]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-325
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise,
  where the Court makes a direction pursuant to subsection 3(3) or 5(3)
  of the [Divorce Act](/eng/acts/D-3.4), the rules made under section 25
  of that Act for the province specified in the direction pursuant to
  subsection 23(2) of that Act shall apply to the conduct of the
  proceeding in the Court, with such modifications as the circumstances
  require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion for modification of rules

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party to a proceeding referred to in subsection (1)
  may at any time, by motion, request a modification to the rules
  referred to in that subsection.

### [Foreign Judgments and Arbitral Awards]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1014995 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[326]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-326 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply to rules 327 to 334.

[[arbitral award]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[arbitral award]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an arbitral award to which subsection 5(2) of the
      [Commercial Arbitration Act](/eng/acts/C-34.6) applies; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a foreign arbitral award that may be recognized
      and enforced by a court in Canada in accordance with the [United
      Nations Foreign Arbitral Awards Convention Act](/eng/acts/U-2.4),
      or articles 35 and 36 of the Commercial Arbitration Code, set out
      in Schedule 1 to the [Commercial Arbitration
      Act](/eng/acts/C-34.6). ([sentence arbitrale]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[arbitration agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[arbitration agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an agreement in
    writing as defined in article II of the convention set out in the
    schedule to the [United Nations Foreign Arbitral Awards Convention
    Act](/eng/acts/U-2.4) or an arbitration agreement as defined in
    Article 7 of the Commercial Arbitration Code, set out in Schedule 1
    to the [Commercial Arbitration Act](/eng/acts/C-34.6). ([convention
    d'arbitrage]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[creditor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[creditor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the person in whose favour a
    foreign judgment is rendered or an arbitral award is
    made. ([créancier]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[debtor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[debtor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the person against whom a
    foreign judgment is rendered or an arbitral award is
    made. ([débiteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[foreign judgment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[foreign judgment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a judgment that may
    be registered in a court in Canada in accordance with

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} sections 80 to 89 of the [Marine Liability
      Act](/eng/acts/M-0.7); or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the [Canada-United Kingdom Civil and Commercial
      Judgments Convention Act](/eng/acts/C-30). ([jugement
      étranger]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 39
- 2012, c. 26, s. 27
- [SOR/2021-245, s. 3](#1333993-1336777){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1333993-1336777.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-326-20130401.html "Link to previous version of section 326"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form of application

**[[327]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-327 .sectionLabel}** An application for
registration of a foreign judgment shall be in Form 327A and an
application for recognition and enforcement of an arbitral award shall
be in Form 327B.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-245, s. 4](#1333998-1336781){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1333998-1336781.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-327-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 327"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"}
application

- **[[328]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-328 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  application under rule 327 may be brought [ex parte]{.otherLang
  lang="la"}.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions regarding service

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On an [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} application
  under subsection (1), the Court may direct that notice of the
  application be served on the debtor and may give any directions
  respecting the manner of service that it considers just.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-245, s. 5](#1334000-1336785){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1334000-1336785.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-328-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 328"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Affidavit

- **[[329]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-329 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  affidavit filed in an application under rule 327 shall contain the
  following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a statement confirming that the foreign judgment or
    arbitral award was not fully satisfied as at the filing of the
    application;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a statement confirming that the debtor appeared in
    the original proceeding;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an address in Canada for service on the creditor;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the name and usual or last known address of the
    debtor;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a statement indicating whether interest has accrued
    on the amount payable under the foreign judgment or arbitral award
    in accordance with the law of the state of the originating court or
    arbitral tribunal and, if interest has accrued, the rate of
    interest, the day from which it is payable, the amount due at the
    time of the filing of the application and, if applicable, the day on
    which interest ceases to accrue;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, the rate of exchange into Canadian
    currency prevailing on the day on which the foreign judgment was
    rendered or the arbitral award was made, as ascertained from a
    chartered bank in Canada;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a statement confirming that, having made careful
    and full inquiries, the applicant knows of no impediment to
    registration of the foreign judgment or recognition and enforcement
    of the arbitral award; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a statement confirming that the foreign judgment or
    arbitral award is executory, that no appeal or other form of
    judicial review is pending and that any time prescribed for the
    making of an appeal or application for judicial review has expired.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The affidavit shall be accompanied by an exemplified
  or certified copy of the foreign judgment or arbitral award, any
  reasons --- including dissenting reasons --- and, in the case of an
  arbitral award, a copy of the arbitration agreement under which the
  award was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional requirement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the debtor did not appear in the original
  proceeding, the affidavit referred to in subsection (1) shall be
  accompanied by an affidavit attesting that the document instituting
  the original proceeding was served on the debtor.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-219, s. 12
- [SOR/2021-245, s. 6](#1334002-1336801){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1334002-1336801.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-329-20060921.html "Link to previous version of section 329"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other evidence

**[[330]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-330 .sectionLabel}** The Court may accept
evidence on an application under rule 327 other than affidavit evidence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conversion to Canadian currency

**[[331]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-331 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court orders
otherwise, an amount payable under a foreign judgment or an arbitral
award shall be converted into the equivalent amount in Canadian currency
on the basis of the rate of exchange, ascertained from a chartered bank
in Canada, that was prevailing on the day on which the judgment was
rendered or the award was made.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-245, s. 7](#1334004-1336805){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1334004-1336805.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-331-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 331"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest

- **[[332]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-332 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  interest on the amount payable under a foreign judgment or an arbitral
  award that has accrued to the day on which the judgment is registered
  or the award is recognized shall be added to the amount payable under
  the judgment or award.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest rate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise, the amount payable
  under a foreign judgment that is registered or an arbitral award that
  is recognized as a result of an application under rule 327 bears
  interest from the day on which it is registered or recognized at the
  rate set out in section 3 of the [Interest Act](/eng/acts/I-15).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-245, s. 7](#1334004-1336812){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1334004-1336812.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-332-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 332"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service and translation of order

**[[333]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-333 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court orders
otherwise, a creditor who obtains an order for registration of a foreign
judgment or recognition and enforcement of an arbitral award shall
personally serve on the debtor the order, together with a translation of
the order in the language of the judgment or award, and an affidavit
attesting to the accuracy of the translation.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-245, s. 7](#1334004-1336816){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1334004-1336816.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-333-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 333"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Action prior to enforcement or execution

**[[334]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court orders
otherwise, a foreign judgment that is registered or an arbitral award
that is recognized as a result of an application under rule 327 shall
not be executed or enforced until proof of service of the order for
registration or recognition has been filed.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-245, s. 7](#1334004-1336820){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1334004-1336820.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-334-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 334"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 5.1]{.HLabel1}[Class Proceedings]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1015041 .Part}

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015042 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[334.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.1 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies
to actions and applications other than applications for judicial review
under section 28 of the Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicability of rules for actions and
applications

**[[334.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.11 .sectionLabel}** Except to the
extent that they are incompatible with the rules in this Part, the rules
applicable to actions and applications, as the case may be, apply to
class proceedings.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

### [Proceedings That May Be Certified as Class Proceedings]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015051 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By class member

- **[[334.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite rule 302, a member of a
  class of persons may commence an action or an application on behalf of
  the members of that class, in which case the originating document
  shall be prefaced by the heading "Proposed Class Proceeding".

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion for certification of proceeding

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The member shall bring a motion for the certification
  of the proceeding as a class proceeding and for the appointment of the
  member as representative plaintiff or applicant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Who may be representative

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The representative of a class shall be a person who
  may act as a plaintiff or an applicant under these Rules.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Originating document in immigration
  matters

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), in the case of an
  application for judicial review referred to in section 72 of the
  [Immigration and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5), the
  originating document is the application for leave referred to in
  subsection 72(1) of that Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By defendant or respondent

- **[[334.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A defendant to an action or a
  respondent to an application may, at any time, bring a motion for the
  certification of the proceeding as a class proceeding and for the
  appointment of a representative plaintiff or applicant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of rule 334.16

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Rule 334.16 applies to the certification of a
  proceeding referred to in subsection (1) as a class proceeding, with
  the exception, unless a judge orders otherwise, of subparagraphs
  334.16(1)(e)(ii) and (iv) and paragraphs 334.16(3)(b) and (d).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Counterclaims

- **[[334.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a defendant to an action that was
  commenced by a member of a class of persons on behalf of the members
  of that class makes a counterclaim against the class, the counterclaim
  may not proceed unless it is certified as a class proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defendant or respondent class
  proceeding

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party to an action or an application against two or
  more defendants or respondents may, at any time, bring a motion for
  the certification of the proceeding as a class proceeding and for the
  appointment of a representative defendant or respondent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Necessary modifications

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} This Part applies, with any necessary modifications,
  to a counterclaim referred to in subsection (1) and to a proceeding
  referred to in subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

### [Motion for Certification]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015079 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time of service and filing

- **[[334.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.15
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A notice of motion for the
  certification of a proceeding as a class proceeding and the affidavit
  in support of that motion shall be served and filed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in an application for judicial review referred to
    in section 72 of the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
    Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5), at the time fixed by the case management
    judge assigned to the proceeding; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other proceeding, at least 14 days before
    the day set out in the notice for the hearing of the motion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of motion --- actions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of an action, the motion shall be made
  returnable no later than 90 days after the later of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the last statement of defence was
    filed, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day on which, under rule 204, the last
    statement of defence is required to be served and filed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of motion --- applications

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of an application, the motion shall be
  made returnable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in an application for judicial review referred to
    in section 72 of the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
    Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5), at the time fixed by the case management
    judge assigned to the proceeding; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other application, no later than 30 days
    after the issuance of the notice of application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Affidavit in response

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person who serves and files an affidavit in
  response to a notice of motion and affidavit shall serve and file it
  at least five days before the day set out in the notice for the
  hearing of the motion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of affidavit

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A person filing an affidavit under subsection (1)
  or (4) shall set out in the affidavit

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the material facts on which the person intends to
    rely at the hearing of the motion;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that the person knows of no fact material to the
    motion that has not been disclosed in the person's affidavit; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to the best of the person's knowledge, the number
    of members in the proposed class.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

### [Certification]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015102 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

- **[[334.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), a judge
  shall, by order, certify a proceeding as a class proceeding if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the pleadings disclose a reasonable cause of
    action;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is an identifiable class of two or more
    persons;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the claims of the class members raise common
    questions of law or fact, whether or not those common questions
    predominate over questions affecting only individual members;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a class proceeding is the preferable procedure for
    the just and efficient resolution of the common questions of law or
    fact; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} there is a representative plaintiff or applicant
    who

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} would fairly and adequately represent the
      interests of the class,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has prepared a plan for the proceeding that sets
      out a workable method of advancing the proceeding on behalf of the
      class and of notifying class members as to how the proceeding is
      progressing,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} does not have, on the common questions of law
      or fact, an interest that is in conflict with the interests of
      other class members, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} provides a summary of any agreements respecting
      fees and disbursements between the representative plaintiff or
      applicant and the solicitor of record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Matters to be considered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} All relevant matters shall be considered in a
  determination of whether a class proceeding is the preferable
  procedure for the just and efficient resolution of the common
  questions of law or fact, including whether

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the questions of law or fact common to the class
    members predominate over any questions affecting only individual
    members;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a significant number of the members of the class
    have a valid interest in individually controlling the prosecution of
    separate proceedings;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the class proceeding would involve claims that are
    or have been the subject of any other proceeding;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} other means of resolving the claims are less
    practical or less efficient; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the administration of the class proceeding would
    create greater difficulties than those likely to be experienced if
    relief were sought by other means.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subclasses

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the judge determines that a class includes a
  subclass whose members have claims that raise common questions of law
  or fact that are not shared by all of the class members so that the
  protection of the interests of the subclass members requires that they
  be separately represented, the judge shall not certify the proceeding
  as a class proceeding unless there is a representative plaintiff or
  applicant who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} would fairly and adequately represent the interests
    of the subclass;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has prepared a plan for the proceeding that sets
    out a workable method of advancing the proceeding on behalf of the
    subclass and of notifying subclass members as to how the proceeding
    is progressing;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} does not have, on the common questions of law or
    fact for the subclass, an interest that is in conflict with the
    interests of other subclass members; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} provides a summary of any agreements respecting
    fees and disbursements between the representative plaintiff or
    applicant and the solicitor of record.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of order

- **[[334.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.17
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An order certifying a proceeding as
  a class proceeding shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} describe the class;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} state the name of the representative plaintiff or
    applicant;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} state the nature of the claims made on behalf of
    the class;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} state the relief claimed by or from the class;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} set out the common questions of law or fact for the
    class; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} specify the time and manner for class members to
    opt out of the class proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subclasses

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the judge determines that a class includes a
  subclass whose members have claims that raise common questions of law
  or fact that are not shared by all of the class members so that the
  protection of the interests of the subclass members requires that they
  be separately represented, the certifying order shall include the
  information referred to in subsection (1) in respect of the subclass.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grounds that may not be relied on

**[[334.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.18 .sectionLabel}** A judge shall not
refuse to certify a proceeding as a class proceeding solely on one or
more of the following grounds:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the relief claimed includes a claim for damages that
  would require an individual assessment after a determination of the
  common questions of law or fact;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the relief claimed relates to separate contracts
  involving different class members;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} different remedies are sought for different class
  members;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the precise number of class members or the identity
  of each class member is not known; or

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the class includes a subclass whose members have
  claims that raise common questions of law or fact not shared by all of
  the class members.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment and decertification

**[[334.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.19 .sectionLabel}** A judge may, on
motion, amend an order certifying a proceeding as a class proceeding or,
if the conditions for certification are no longer satisfied with respect
to the proceeding, decertify it.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation of action

**[[334.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.2 .sectionLabel}** If a judge refuses
to certify a proceeding as a class proceeding or decertifies a
proceeding, the judge may permit the proceeding to continue as one or
more proceedings and may make any appropriate order in that regard.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

### [Opting Out and Exclusion]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015160 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voluntary

- **[[334.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.21
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A class member involved in a class
  proceeding may opt out of the proceeding within the time and in the
  manner specified in the order certifying the proceeding as a class
  proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Automatic

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A class member shall be excluded from the class
  proceeding if the member does not, before the expiry of the time for
  opting out specified in the certifying order, discontinue a proceeding
  brought by the member that raises the common questions of law or fact
  set out in that order.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

### [Examination for Discovery]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015168 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave to examine others --- actions

- **[[334.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.22
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A party in an action that has been
  certified as a class proceeding may examine a class member, other than
  the representative plaintiff, for discovery only on leave granted by
  the Court and only after the examination of the representative
  plaintiff.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Considerations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In deciding whether to grant leave to examine class
  members, the Court shall consider all relevant matters, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the stage of the class proceeding and the issues to
    be determined at that stage;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the presence of subclasses;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the necessity of an examination in view of the
    claims or defences of the party seeking leave;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the approximate monetary value of any individual
    claims; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the possibility that the examination might result
    in undue burden or expense for the class members sought to be
    examined.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of sanctions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A class member is subject to the same sanctions under
  these Rules as a party for failure to submit to an examination.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

### [Participation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015183 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By class members

- **[[334.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.23
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} To ensure the fair and adequate
  representation of the interests of a class or any subclass, the Court
  may, at any time, permit one or more class members to participate in
  the class proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} When permitting a class member to participate in the
  proceeding, the Court shall give directions regarding the role of the
  participant, including matters relating to costs and to the procedures
  to be followed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

### [Judgments]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015191 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Separate judgments

- **[[334.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.24
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A judge may give a single judgment
  in respect of the common questions of law or fact and separate
  judgments in respect of any other questions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A judgment on questions of law or fact that are
  common to a class or subclass shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} set out the common questions of law or fact;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} name or describe the class or subclass members to
    the extent possible;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} state the nature of the claims asserted on behalf
    of the class or subclass; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} specify the relief granted.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Common questions

- **[[334.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.25
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A judgment on questions of law or
  fact that are common to a class or subclass binds every class or
  subclass member who has not opted out of or been excluded from the
  class proceeding, but only to the extent that the judgment determines
  common questions of law or fact that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are set out in the certifying order;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} relate to claims described in that order; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} relate to relief sought by the class or subclass as
    stated in that order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent actions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A judgment on common questions of law or fact of a
  class or subclass does not bind a party to the class proceeding in any
  subsequent proceeding between the party and a member who has opted out
  of or been excluded from the class proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Individual questions

- **[[334.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.26
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a judge determines that there are
  questions of law or fact that apply only to certain individual class
  or subclass members, the judge shall set a time within which those
  members may make claims in respect of those questions and may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} order that the individual questions be determined
    in further hearings;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} appoint one or more persons to evaluate the
    individual questions and report back to the judge; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} direct the manner in which the individual questions
    will be determined.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judge may give directions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In those circumstances, the judge may give directions
  relating to the procedures to be followed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Who may preside

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (1)(a), the judge who
  determined the common questions of law or fact, another judge or, in
  the case of a claim referred to in subsection 50(3), a prothonotary
  may preside over the hearings of the individual questions.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defendant's liability

**[[334.27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.27 .sectionLabel}** In the case of an
action, if, after determining common questions of law or fact in favour
of a class or subclass, a judge determines that the defendant's
liability to individual class members cannot be determined without proof
by those individual class members, rule 334.26 applies to the
determination of the defendant's liability to those class members.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment of monetary relief

- **[[334.28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.28
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A judge may make any order in
  respect of the assessment of monetary relief, including aggregate
  assessments, that is due to the class or subclass.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Distribution of monetary relief

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A judge may make any order in respect of the
  distribution of monetary relief, including an undistributed portion of
  an award that is due to a class or subclass or its members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special modes of proof

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this rule, a judge may order any
  special modes of proof.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

### [Settlements]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015238 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

- **[[334.29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.29
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A class proceeding may be settled
  only with the approval of a judge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Binding effect

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On approval, a settlement binds every class or
  subclass member who has not opted out of or been excluded from the
  class proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

### [Discontinuance]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015246 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

**[[334.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.3 .sectionLabel}** A proceeding
commenced by a member of a class of persons on behalf of the members of
that class may only be discontinued with the approval of a judge.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

### [Appeals]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015251 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Individual questions

- **[[334.31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.31
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A class member may appeal any order
  determining or dismissing the member's claim in respect of one or more
  individual questions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representative plaintiff or applicant
  failing to appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a representative plaintiff or applicant does not
  appeal an order, or does appeal and later files a notice of
  discontinuance of the appeal, any member of the class for which the
  representative plaintiff or applicant had been appointed may apply for
  leave to exercise the right of appeal of that representative within 30
  days after

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the expiry of the appeal period available to the
    representative, if the representative does not appeal; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the notice of discontinuance is
    filed, if the representative appeals and later files a notice of
    discontinuance of the appeal.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

### [Notices]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015261 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Who gives notice

- **[[334.32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.32
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notice that a proceeding has been
  certified as a class proceeding shall be given by the representative
  plaintiff or applicant to the class members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dispensation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A judge may dispense with the giving of notice after
  considering the factors set out in subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A judge shall order when and by what means notice is
  to be given after considering the following factors:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the cost of giving notice;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the relief sought;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the size of the individual claims of the class
    members;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the number of class members;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the presence of subclasses;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the likelihood that some or all of the class
    members will opt out of the class proceeding; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the places of residence of class members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}How given

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The order may provide that notice be given by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} personal delivery;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} mail;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} posting, publishing, advertising or the
    distribution of leaflets;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} individually notifying a sample group within the
    class; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any other appropriate means or combination of
    appropriate means.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of notice

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The notice shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} describe the proceeding, including the names and
    addresses of the representative plaintiff or applicant, and the
    relief sought;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} state the time and manner for a class member to opt
    out of the proceeding;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} describe the possible financial consequences of the
    proceeding to the class and subclass members;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} summarize any agreements respecting fees and
    disbursements

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} between the representative plaintiff or applicant
      and that representative's solicitor, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the recipient of the notice is a member of a
      subclass, between the representative plaintiff or applicant for
      that subclass and that representative's solicitor;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an action, describe any counterclaim
    being asserted by or against the class or any subclass, including
    the relief sought in the counterclaim;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} state that the judgment on the common questions of
    law or fact for the class or subclass, whether favourable or not,
    will bind all of the class members or subclass members who do not
    opt out of the proceeding;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} describe the right, if any, of the class or
    subclass members to participate in the proceeding; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} give an address to which class members may direct
    inquiries about the proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for contributions

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} With leave of the judge, the notice may include a
  solicitation of contributions from the class or subclass members to
  assist in paying the fees and disbursements of the solicitor of
  record.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of determination of common
questions

**[[334.33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.33 .sectionLabel}** If common
questions of law or fact are determined in favour of the class or a
subclass, the representative plaintiff or applicant for the class or
subclass shall give notice of that determination to the class or
subclass members in accordance with the directions of a judge in respect
of the content of and means of giving the notice.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of settlement

**[[334.34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.34 .sectionLabel}** Notice that an
offer to settle has been made or that a settlement has been approved
under rule 334.29 shall be given by the representative plaintiff or
applicant to the class or subclass members in accordance with the
directions of a judge in respect of the content of and means of giving
the notice.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to others

- **[[334.35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.35
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A judge may, at any time, order any
  party to give any notice that the judge considers necessary to protect
  the interests of any class member or party or to ensure the fair
  conduct of the proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of subsections 334.32(3)
  and (4)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsections 334.32(3) and (4) apply to a notice given
  under this rule.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

**[[334.36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.36 .sectionLabel}** A judge may order
any party to give a notice under rules 334.32 to 334.35.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prior approval of notices

**[[334.37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.37 .sectionLabel}** Notices referred
to in rules 334.32 to 334.35 shall not be given unless they have been
approved by a judge.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expenses

**[[334.38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.38 .sectionLabel}** The judge has
full discretion over the amount and allocation of expenses in respect of
notices and may determine who is to pay those expenses.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

### [Costs]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015326 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No costs

- **[[334.39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.39
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), no costs
  may be awarded against any party to a motion for certification of a
  proceeding as a class proceeding, to a class proceeding or to an
  appeal arising from a class proceeding, unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the conduct of the party unnecessarily lengthened
    the duration of the proceeding;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any step in the proceeding by the party was
    improper, vexatious or unnecessary or was taken through negligence,
    mistake or excessive caution; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} exceptional circumstances make it unjust to deprive
    the successful party of costs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Individual claims

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Court has full discretion to award costs with
  respect to the determination of the individual claims of a class
  member.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of payments

**[[334.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334.4 .sectionLabel}** No payments,
including indirect payments, shall be made to a solicitor from the
proceeds recovered in a class proceeding unless the payments are
approved by a judge.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 7
:::

## [PART 6]{.HLabel1}[Appeals]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1015341 .Part}

### [Application of this Part]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015342 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[335]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-335 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} appeals to the Federal Court of Appeal from the
  Federal Court, including appeals from interlocutory orders;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} appeals to the Federal Court of Appeal from the Tax
  Court of Canada under subsections 27(1.1) and (1.2) of the Act; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} appeals to the Court under an Act of Parliament,
  unless otherwise indicated in that Act or these Rules.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 17
:::

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015350 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1015351 .Subheading}

Definition of [[first instance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[336]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-336 .sectionLabel}** In this Part, [[first
instance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a proceeding in the Federal Court,
the Tax Court of Canada or the tribunal whose order is being appealed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 33
:::

#### [Commencement of Appeal]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1015356 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of general notice of appeal

**[[337]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-337 .sectionLabel}** An appeal, other than
an appeal from a final judgment of the Tax Court of Canada under
subsection 27(1.2) of the Act, shall be commenced by a notice of appeal,
in Form 337, setting out

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the court to which the appeal is taken;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the names of the parties;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a precise statement of the relief sought;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a complete and concise statement of the grounds
  intended to be argued, including a reference to any statutory
  provision or rule to be relied on;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name of the court or tribunal appealed from;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the date and details of the order under appeal; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the place proposed for the hearing of the appeal.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, ss. 18, 36
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of notice of appeal --- certain
judgments of Tax Court of Canada

**[[337.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-337.1 .sectionLabel}** An appeal from a
final judgment of the Tax Court of Canada under subsection 27(1.2) of
the Act shall be commenced by a notice of appeal, in Form 337.1, setting
out

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the names of the parties;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a precise statement of the relief sought;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a complete and concise statement of the grounds
  intended to be argued, including a reference to any statutory
  provision or rule to be relied on;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the date and details of the final judgment under
  appeal; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the place proposed for the hearing of the appeal.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 19
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons to be included as respondents

- **[[338]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-338
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise,
  an appellant shall include as a respondent in an appeal

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} every party in the first instance who is adverse in
    interest to the appellant in the appeal;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other person required to be named as a party by
    an Act of Parliament pursuant to which the appeal is brought; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where there are no persons that are included under
    paragraph (a) or (b), the Attorney General of Canada.
