- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Substitution for Attorney General

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On a motion by the Attorney General of Canada, where
  the Court is satisfied that the Attorney General is unable or
  unwilling to act as a respondent in an appeal, the Court may
  substitute another person or body, including a tribunal whose order is
  being appealed, as a respondent in the place of the Attorney General
  of Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of notice of appeal

- **[[339]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-339
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court directs otherwise
  or an Act of Parliament authorizing the appeal provides otherwise,
  within 10 days after the issuance of a notice of appeal, the appellant
  shall serve it on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} all respondents;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an appeal of an order of a tribunal,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the Attorney General of Canada, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the tribunal or its chief executive officer;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any person who is not a party and who participated
    in the first instance; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other person directly affected by the appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of service

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Proof of service of a notice of appeal shall be filed
  within 10 days after the notice of appeal is served.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Solicitor of record and address for
service

**[[340]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-340 .sectionLabel}** In an appeal from the
Federal Court to the Federal Court of Appeal, the solicitor of record
and the address for service of a party on the appeal shall be the same
as they were in the first instance, unless the solicitor of record in
the first instance provided limited-scope representation and they served
and filed a notice under subsection 124(5).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 20
- [SOR/2021-246, s. 10](#1333963-1337210){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1333963-1337210.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-340-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 340"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appearance or cross-appeal

- **[[341]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-341 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  respondent who intends to participate in an appeal shall, within 10
  days after service of the notice of appeal, serve and file

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a notice of appearance in Form 341A; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the respondent seeks a different disposition
    of the order appealed from, a notice of cross-appeal in Form 341B.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of notice of cross-appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice of cross-appeal shall set out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a precise statement of the relief sought; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a complete and concise statement of the grounds
    intended to be argued, including a reference to any statutory
    provision or rule to be relied on.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave for late cross-appeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a respondent has not filed a notice of
  cross-appeal under subsection (1), the cross-appeal may not be heard
  without leave of the Court.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-301, s. 12(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-341-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 341"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consolidation of appeals

- **[[342]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-342
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise,
  where more than one party appeals from an order, all appeals shall be
  consolidated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Court may give directions as to the procedure to
  be followed in a consolidation under subsection (1).

#### [Appeal Books]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1015418 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreement re appeal book

- **[[343]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-343
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within 30 days after the filing of a
  notice of appeal, the parties shall agree in writing as to the
  documents, exhibits and transcripts to be included in the appeal book
  and shall file a copy of that agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The parties shall include in an appeal book only such
  documents, exhibits and transcripts as are required to dispose of the
  issues on appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion to determine content of appeal
  book

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an agreement is not reached within the period
  referred to in subsection (1), the appellant shall, within 10 days
  after the expiry of that period, bring a motion in accordance with
  rule 369 or 369.2, as the case may be, requesting that the Court
  determine the content of the appeal book.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order for transcripts or reproductions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a transcript or the reproduction of exhibits is
  required, the appellant shall order it and shall file proof of the
  order within 10 days after filing an agreement under subsection (1) or
  obtaining an order under subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preparation of appeal book

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The appeal book shall be prepared by the appellant
  forthwith unless, on the motion of the appellant, the Court orders the
  Administrator to prepare an appeal book on the appellant's behalf from
  documents provided by the appellant.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 20
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 11](#1331696-1336706){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331696-1336706.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-343-20150130.html "Link to previous version of section 343"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of appeal book

- **[[344]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-344 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  appeal book shall contain, on consecutively numbered pages and in the
  following order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a table of contents describing each document;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the notice of appeal and any notice of
    cross-appeal;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the order appealed from, as signed and entered, and
    any reasons, including dissenting reasons, given in respect of that
    order;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the originating document, any other pleadings and
    any other document in the first instance that defines the issues in
    the appeal;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (2), all documents, exhibits
    and transcripts agreed on under subsection 343(1) or ordered to be
    included on a motion under subsection 343(3);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any order made in respect of the conduct of the
    appeal;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any other document relevant to the appeal;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} an agreement reached under subsection 343(1) as to
    the contents of the appeal book or an order made under subsection
    343(3); and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a certificate in Form 344, signed by the
    appellant's solicitor, stating that the contents of the appeal book
    are complete and legible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Colour of cover

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} An appeal book that is filed in paper copy shall
  have a grey cover.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transcripts separate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Transcripts may be reproduced in a separate document.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-219, s. 13
- SOR/2015-21, s. 21
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-344-20060921.html "Link to previous version of section 344"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal book

- **[[345]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-345 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  appellant shall serve and file the appeal book within 30 days after
  the day on which a copy of an agreement under subsection 343(1) is
  filed or an order under subsection 343(3) is obtained.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Number of copies

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The appellant shall file

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the appeal is brought in the Federal Court, an
    electronic copy of or, subject to rule 72.4, three paper copies of
    the book; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the appeal is brought in the Federal Court of
    Appeal, an electronic copy of or, subject to rule 72.4, five paper
    copies of the book.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, ss. 32, 33
- SOR/2015-21, s. 22
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 14](#1303087-1305185){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303087-1305185.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-345-20150130.html "Link to previous version of section 345"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Memoranda]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1015459 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appellant's memorandum

- **[[346]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-346
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within 30 days after filing an
  appeal book, the appellant shall serve and file a memorandum of fact
  and law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Respondent's memorandum

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Within 30 days after service of the appellant's
  memorandum of fact and law, the respondent shall serve and file the
  respondent's memorandum of fact and law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where cross-appeal filed

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a respondent has served a notice of
  cross-appeal under rule 341,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the respondent shall serve and file a memorandum of
    fact and law as appellant by cross-appeal, either separate from or
    as part of the respondent's memorandum of fact and law, within the
    time set out in subsection (2); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the appellant shall serve and file a memorandum of
    fact and law as respondent to cross-appeal, within 30 days after
    service of the respondent's memorandum of fact and law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Colour of memorandum

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A memorandum of fact and law that is in paper copy
  shall have

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the appellant's memorandum, a beige
    cover;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the respondent's memorandum, a green
    cover; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an intervener's memorandum, a blue
    cover.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Number of memoranda to be filed

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Memoranda of fact and law shall be filed in the same
  number as are appeal books.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 23
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 15](#1303089-1305187){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303089-1305187.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-346-20150130.html "Link to previous version of section 346"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Requisition for Hearing]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1015478 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requisition for hearing

- **[[347]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-347
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within 20 days after service of the
  respondent's memorandum of fact and law or 20 days after the
  expiration of the time for service of the respondent's memorandum of
  fact and law, whichever is the earlier, an appellant shall serve and
  file a requisition in Form 347 requesting that a date be set for the
  hearing of the appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Default by appellant

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an appellant fails to comply with subsection
  (1), a respondent may, in lieu of bringing a motion under rule 167,
  serve and file a requisition in Form 347 to request that a date be set
  for the hearing of the appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of requisition

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A requisition referred to in subsection (1) shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} include a statement that the requirements of
    subsections 346(1) and (5) have been satisfied and that any notice
    required under section 57 of the Act has been given;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} set out the location at which the hearing should be
    held;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} set out the maximum number of hours or days
    required for the hearing;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} list any dates within the following 90 days on
    which the parties are not available for a hearing;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} set out the name, address, telephone number and fax
    number of the solicitor for every party to the appeal or, where a
    party is not represented by a solicitor, the person's name, address,
    telephone number and any fax number; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} indicate whether the hearing will be in English or
    French, or partly in English and partly in French, and whether the
    materials in the requisition for hearing file will be in English or
    French, or partly in English and partly in French.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 20(E)
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 16](#1303091-1305189){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303091-1305189.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-347-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 347"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Book of Authorities]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1015494 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2021-150, s. 9(F)

\]
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Joint book

- **[[348]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-348
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within the time for serving and
  filing the requisition for the hearing set out in subsection 347(1),
  the parties shall file

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the appeal is brought in the Federal Court, an
    electronic copy of or, subject to rule 72.4, three paper copies of a
    joint book of statutes, regulations and authorities; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the appeal is brought in the Federal Court of
    Appeal, an electronic copy of or, subject to rule 72.4, five paper
    copies of a joint book of statutes, regulations and authorities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Separate books

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the parties cannot agree on a joint book of
  statutes, regulations and authorities, they shall each file a separate
  book, without reproducing documents that are included in the book of
  another party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Enactments in both official languages

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Extracts of federal statutes and regulations in a
  book of statutes, regulations and authorities shall be reproduced in
  both official languages.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons for judgment

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} In respect of any reasons for judgment, a book of
  statutes, regulations and authorities shall contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case where the book is filed in paper copy
    and the reasons are available from an electronic database that is
    accessible to the public at no charge, the relevant extracts of the
    reasons --- including the head note, if any, and the paragraphs
    immediately preceding and following the extracts --- with a
    reference to the database clearly marked on the page containing the
    extract; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the reasons for judgment in full
    with the relevant extracts clearly marked.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Colour of cover

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A book of statutes, regulations and authorities that
  is in paper copy shall have

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the book is filed jointly, a burgundy cover; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the book is filed separately, a cover that is
    the same colour as the filing party's memorandum of fact and law.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, ss. 32, 33
- SOR/2015-21, s. 24
- [SOR/2021-150, s. 10](#1302240-1305092){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1302240-1305092.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 17](#1303093-1305192){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303093-1305192.html"
  role="button"}
:::

Version](section-348-20150130.html "Link to previous version of section 348"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Condensed Book]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1305093 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies and content

**[[348.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-348.1 .sectionLabel}** A party may file
five paper copies of a condensed book that contains the extracts from
the appeal book and the book of statutes, regulations and authorities
that the party will refer to in oral argument.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-150, s. 11](#1302247-1305097){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1302247-1305097.html"
  role="button"}
:::

### [Consent to Reversal or Variation of Judgment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015510 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent to reversal or variation of
judgment

- **[[349]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-349 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  respondent may consent to the reversal or variation of an order
  appealed from by serving and filing a notice to that effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judgment on consent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Court may pronounce judgment in accordance with a
  notice filed under subsection (1) if the resultant judgment is one
  that could have been given on consent.

### [Material in the Possession of a Tribunal]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015516 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Material in possession of a tribunal

**[[350]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-350 .sectionLabel}** Rules 317 to 319 apply
to appeals and motions for leave to appeal, with such modifications as
are necessary.

### [New Evidence on Appeal]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015519 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}New evidence on appeal

**[[351]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-351 .sectionLabel}** In special
circumstances, the Court may grant leave to a party to present evidence
on a question of fact.

### [Motions for Leave to Appeal]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015522 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave to appeal

- **[[352]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-352
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise,
  where leave to appeal is required, it shall be obtained on a motion
  brought in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Respondents and service

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On a motion under subsection (1) the moving party
  shall name as respondents all persons referred to in rule 338 and
  personally serve all persons referred to in rule 339.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion record

- **[[353]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-353
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise, a
  party bringing a motion for leave to appeal shall serve the motion
  record and file an electronic copy of or, subject to rule 72.4, three
  paper copies of that record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of motion record

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A motion record referred to in subsection (1) shall
  contain, on consecutively numbered pages and in the following order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the order in respect of which leave to appeal is
    sought and any reasons, including dissenting reasons, given in
    respect of that order;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the pleadings and any other material that is
    necessary for the hearing of the motion;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an affidavit that sets out any facts relied on in
    the motion that do not appear on the Court file; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a memorandum of fact and law.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-219, s. 14
- SOR/2015-21, s. 25
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 18](#1303095-1305194){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303095-1305194.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-353-20150130.html "Link to previous version of section 353"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Respondent's memorandum of fact and law

**[[354]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-354 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court orders
otherwise, a respondent to a motion for leave to appeal shall serve a
memorandum of fact and law and any supporting affidavits and file an
electronic copy of or, subject to rule 72.4, three paper copies of each
of them no later than 20 days after the day on which the motion record
is served.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 26
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 19](#1303097-1305197){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303097-1305197.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-354-20150130.html "Link to previous version of section 354"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reply

**[[355]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-355 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court orders
otherwise, a party bringing a motion for leave to appeal shall serve any
reply to the respondent's memorandum of fact and law and file an
electronic copy of or, subject to rule 72.4, three paper copies of the
reply no later than 10 days after the day on which it is served.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2015-21, s. 26
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 19](#1303097-1305200){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303097-1305200.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-355-20150130.html "Link to previous version of section 355"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of motion

**[[356]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-356 .sectionLabel}** On the filing of a
reply under rule 355 or the expiration of the period allowed for a
reply, the Court may dispose in writing of a motion for leave to appeal.

### [Leave to Appeal to the Supreme Court of Canada]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015549 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion for leave to appeal to Supreme
Court

- **[[357]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-357
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding rule 352, where a
  judgment of the Federal Court of Appeal is delivered from the bench, a
  motion under section 37.1 of the [Supreme Court Act](/eng/acts/S-26)
  for leave to appeal from the judgment to the Supreme Court of Canada
  may be made at the time the judgment is delivered and without prior
  notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grounds for motion for leave

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A motion for leave to appeal under section 37.1 of
  the [Supreme Court Act](/eng/acts/S-26) shall, unless the Court
  permits otherwise, be argued on the case, and on the reasons for
  judgment, from which leave to appeal is sought.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Number of judges

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A motion for leave to appeal under section 37.1 of
  the [Supreme Court Act](/eng/acts/S-26) shall be heard before not
  fewer than three judges, who need not be the judges who heard the
  matter under appeal.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, ss. 21(F), 32
:::

## [PART 7]{.HLabel1}[Motions]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1015559 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[358]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-358 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies to
motions other than motions for leave to appeal under Part 6.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of motion

**[[359]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-359 .sectionLabel}** Except with leave of
the Court, a motion shall be initiated by a notice of motion, in Form
359, setting out

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a motion other than one brought under
  rule 369 or 369.2, the time, place and estimated duration of the
  hearing of the motion;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the relief sought;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the grounds intended to be argued, including a
  reference to any statutory provision or rule to be relied on; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a list of the documents or other material to be used
  for the purposes of the motion.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 12](#1331698-1336710){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331698-1336710.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-359-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 359"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of motion

**[[360]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-360 .sectionLabel}** A notice of motion
shall not be filed unless it is made returnable

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} at sittings fixed under rule 34;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} at a time and place appointed under subsection 35(2);
  or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with rule 369 or 369.2, as the case may
  be.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 13](#1331703-1336715){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331703-1336715.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-360-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 360"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service on [ex parte]{.otherLang
lang="la"} motion

**[[361]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-361 .sectionLabel}** Notwithstanding rules
362, 364, 367 and 370, a party bringing an [ex parte]{.otherLang
lang="la"} motion need not comply with the service requirements set out
in those rules.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service and filing of notice

- **[[362]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-362
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), on a
  motion other than a motion under rule 369, a notice of motion and any
  affidavit required under rule 363 shall be served and filed at least
  three days before the day set out in the notice for the hearing of the
  motion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion on less than three days notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Court may hear the motion on less than three
  days' notice

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the motion is made on notice, if all parties
    consent; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any case, if the moving party satisfies the
    Court of the urgency of the motion.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-18, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-362-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 362"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence on motion

**[[363]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-363 .sectionLabel}** A party to a motion
shall set out in an affidavit any facts to be relied on by that party in
the motion that do not appear on the Court file.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 21(F)
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 14(F)](#1331708-1336716){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331708-1336716.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-363-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 363"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion record

- **[[364]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-364
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise, a
  person bringing a motion shall serve a motion record and file an
  electronic copy of or, subject to rule 72.4, three paper copies of
  that record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of motion record

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A moving party's motion record shall contain, on
  consecutively numbered pages arranged in the following order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a table of contents;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the notice of motion;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} all affidavits and other material served by the
    moving party for use on the motion;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} subject to rule 368, the portions of any
    transcripts on which the moving party intends to rely;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} subject to rule 366, written representations; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any other filed material that is necessary for the
    purposes of the motion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service and filing of motion record

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections 51(2), 163(2) and 213(3), on a
  motion other than a motion under rule 369, the motion record shall be
  served and filed at least three days before the day set out in the
  notice of motion for the hearing of the motion.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-331, s. 5
- SOR/2013-18, s. 12
- SOR/2015-21, s. 27
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 20](#1303104-1305202){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303104-1305202.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 15](#1331710-1336726){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331710-1336726.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-364-20210617.html "Link to previous version of section 364"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Respondent's motion record

- **[[365]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-365 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  respondent to a motion shall serve a respondent's motion record and
  file one electronic copy or, subject to Rule 72.4, three paper copies
  of the record no later than

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a motion brought in the Federal
    Court, and subject to subsections 213(4) and 369(2), 2:00 p.m. on
    the day that is two days before the day fixed for the hearing of the
    motion; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a motion brought in the Federal
    Court of Appeal, 10 days after the day on which they are served with
    the moving party's motion record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of motion record

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The motion record of a respondent to a motion shall
  contain, on consecutively numbered pages and in the following order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a table of contents;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all affidavits and other material to be used by the
    respondent on the motion that is not included in the moving party's
    motion record;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to rule 368, the portions of any
    transcripts on which the respondent intends to rely;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} subject to rule 366, written representations; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any other filed material not contained in the
    moving party's motion record that is necessary for the purposes of
    the motion.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2009-331, s. 6
- SOR/2013-18, s. 13
- SOR/2015-21, s. 28
- [SOR/2021-151, s. 21](#1303113-1305204){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1303113-1305204.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 16](#1331712-1336727){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331712-1336727.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-365-20210617.html "Link to previous version of section 365"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Memorandum of fact and law required

**[[366]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-366 .sectionLabel}** On a motion for summary
judgment or summary trial, for an interlocutory injunction, for the
determination of a question of law or for the certification of a
proceeding as a class proceeding, or if the Court so orders, a motion
record shall contain a memorandum of fact and law instead of written
representations.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 22
- SOR/2007-301, s. 8
- SOR/2009-331, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-366-20071213.html "Link to previous version of section 366"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents filed as part of motion record

**[[367]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-367 .sectionLabel}** A notice of motion or
any affidavit required to be filed by a party to a motion may be served
and filed as part of the party's motion record and need not be served
and filed separately.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transcripts of cross-examinations

**[[368]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-368 .sectionLabel}** Transcripts of all
cross-examinations on affidavits on a motion shall be filed before the
hearing of the motion.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motions in writing

- **[[369]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-369 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party may, in a notice of motion, request that the motion be decided
  on the basis of written representations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for oral hearing

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A respondent to a motion brought in accordance with
  subsection (1) shall serve and file a respondent's record within 10
  days after being served under rule 364 and, if the respondent objects
  to disposition of the motion in writing, indicate in its written
  representations or memorandum of fact and law the reasons why the
  motion should not be disposed of in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reply

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A moving party may serve and file written
  representations in reply within four days after being served with a
  respondent's record under subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of motion

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On the filing of a reply under subsection (3) or on
  the expiration of the period allowed for a reply, the Court may
  dispose of a motion in writing or fix a time and place for an oral
  hearing of the motion.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motions in the Federal Court of Appeal

**[[369.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-369.1 .sectionLabel}** Rule 362,
subsection 364(3) and rules 366 to 369 do not apply to a motion that is
brought in the Federal Court of Appeal.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 17](#1331717-1336731){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331717-1336731.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Written representations only --- Federal
Court of Appeal

- **[[369.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-369.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise ordered by the
  Court and subject to subsection (2), all motions brought in the
  Federal Court of Appeal shall be decided on the basis of written
  representations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for oral hearing

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party to a motion may make a written request that
  the motion be heard orally. The request, together with the reasons why
  the motion should be heard orally, shall be attached as a separate
  page at the end of the party's motion record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reply by moving party

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Unless the motion is to be heard orally, the moving
  party may serve and file written representations in reply within four
  days after the day on which they are served with the respondent's
  motion record.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 17](#1331717-1336740){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331717-1336740.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Abandonment of motion

- **[[370]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-370 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party who brings a motion may abandon it by serving and filing a
  notice of abandonment in Form 370.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed abandonment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a moving party fails to appear at the hearing
  of a motion without serving and filing a notice of abandonment, it is
  deemed to have abandoned the motion.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Testimony regarding issue of fact

**[[371]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-371 .sectionLabel}** On motion, the Court
may, in special circumstances, authorize a witness to testify in court
in relation to an issue of fact raised on a motion.

## [PART 8]{.HLabel1}[Preservation of Rights in Proceedings]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1015639 .Part}

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015640 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion before proceeding commenced

- **[[372]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-372 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  motion under this Part may not be brought before the commencement of a
  proceeding except in a case of urgency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Undertaking to commence proceeding

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A party bringing a motion before the commencement of
  a proceeding shall undertake to commence the proceeding within the
  time fixed by the Court.

### [Interim and Interlocutory Injunctions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015646 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Availability

- **[[373]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-373 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  motion, a judge may grant an interlocutory injunction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Undertaking to abide by order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless a judge orders otherwise, a party bringing a
  motion for an interlocutory injunction shall undertake to abide by any
  order concerning damages caused by the granting or extension of the
  injunction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expedited hearing

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where it appears to a judge that the issues in a
  motion for an interlocutory injunction should be decided by an
  expedited hearing of the proceeding, the judge may make an order under
  rule 385.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence at hearing

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A judge may order that any evidence submitted at the
  hearing of a motion for an interlocutory injunction shall be
  considered as evidence submitted at the hearing of the proceeding.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim injunction

- **[[374]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-374 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  judge may grant an interim injunction on an [ex parte]{.otherLang
  lang="la"} motion for a period of not more than 14 days where the
  judge is satisfied

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in a case of urgency, that no notice is possible;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that to give notice would defeat the purpose of the
    motion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A motion to extend an interim injunction that was
  granted on an [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} motion may be brought
  only on notice to every party affected by the injunction, unless the
  moving party can demonstrate that a party has been evading service or
  that there are other sufficient reasons to extend the interim
  injunction without notice to the party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a motion to extend an interim injunction under
  subsection (2) is brought [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"}, the
  extension may be granted for a further period of not more than 14
  days.

### [Appointment of a Receiver]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015665 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion to appoint receiver

- **[[375]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-375 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  motion, a judge may appoint a receiver in any proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration and security

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order under subsection (1) shall set out the
  remuneration to be paid to, and the amount of security to be given by,
  the receiver.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of receiver's accounts

**[[376]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-376 .sectionLabel}** A receiver appointed
under rule 375 shall, by motion to the Court, seek approval of the
receiver's accounts on an annual basis.

### [Preservation of Property]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015673 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion for order in respect of property

- **[[377]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-377 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  motion, the Court may make an order for the custody or preservation of
  property that is, or will be, the subject-matter of a proceeding or as
  to which a question may arise therein.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Rule 374 applies to interim orders for the custody or
  preservation of property referred to in subsection (1), with such
  modifications as the circumstances require.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to identify property

- **[[378]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-378 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  order under subsection 377(1) shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} identify the property to be kept or preserved;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} state where, by whom, for how long and at whose
    cost the property is to be kept or preserved; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the property is to be insured, state at whose
    expense it shall be insured.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order under subsection 377(1) shall be directed
  solely to the protection of the property in question.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sale of perishable or deteriorating
property

**[[379]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-379 .sectionLabel}** Where any property,
other than real property or immoveables, that is the subject-matter of a
proceeding or the subject of a question that may arise in a proceeding

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is of a perishable nature,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} is likely to deteriorate if kept, or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} should for any other reason be sold without delay,

on motion, the Court may order the sale of the property, in such a
manner and on such conditions as may be specified in the order.

## [PART 9]{.HLabel1}[Case Management and Dispute Resolution Services]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1015693 .Part}

### [Case Management]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015694 .Subheading}

#### [Status Review --- Federal Court]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1015695 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Actions --- Federal Court

- **[[380]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-380 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If,
  in an action commenced in the Federal Court,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 180 days have elapsed since the issuance of the
    statement of claim and no statement of defence has been filed and no
    motion for default judgement is pending, the Administrator shall
    issue a notice of status review, in Form 380, to the parties; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 360 days have elapsed since the issuance of the
    statement of claim and no requisition for a pre-trial conference has
    been filed, the Court shall order that the action continue as a
    specially managed proceeding and may make an order under rule 385.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applications --- Federal Court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If, in an application commenced in the Federal Court,
  180 days have elapsed since the issuance of the notice of application
  and no requisition for a hearing date has been filed, the Court may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} issue a notice of status review, in Form 380, to
    the parties; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} order that the application continue as a specially
    managed proceeding and may make an order under rule 385.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) and (2) do not apply to an action or
  an application continued as a specially managed proceeding or in
  respect of which a motion to continue the proceeding as a specially
  managed proceeding is pending.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-106, s. 380
- err.(F), Vol. 132, No. 12
- SOR/2007-214, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-380-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 380"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing of timetable

**[[381]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-381 .sectionLabel}** If the Court orders
that an action or an application continue as a specially managed
proceeding under paragraph 380(1)(b) or (2)(b), and no order under rule
385 has been made in accordance with that paragraph, the plaintiff or
applicant, within 20 days of the date of the order, shall serve and file
a proposed timetable for the completion of the steps necessary to
advance the proceeding in an expeditious manner.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-214, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-381-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 381"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations of plaintiff or applicant

- **[[382]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-382 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  notice of status review is issued in respect of an action or an
  application, the plaintiff or applicant, within 15 days of the date of
  the notice of status review, shall serve and file representations
  stating the reasons why the proceeding should not be dismissed for
  delay. The representations shall include a justification for the delay
  and a proposed timetable for the completion of the steps necessary to
  advance the proceeding in an expeditious manner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations of defendant or
  respondent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The defendant or respondent may serve and file
  representations within seven days after being served with the
  representations of the plaintiff or applicant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reply

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The plaintiff or applicant may serve and file a reply
  within four days after being served with the representations of the
  defendant or respondent.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-214, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-382-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 382"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review to be in writing

- **[[382.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-382.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court directs otherwise,
  a status review of a proceeding commenced in the Federal Court shall
  be conducted on the basis of the written representations of the
  parties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review by the Court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A judge or prothonotary shall conduct a status review
  and may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if he or she is not satisfied that the proceeding
    should continue, dismiss the proceeding; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if he or she is satisfied that the proceeding
    should continue, order that it continue as a specially managed
    proceeding and may make an order under rule 385.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-214, s. 1
:::

#### [Status Review --- Federal Court of Appeal]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1015731 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application or appeal --- Federal Court
of Appeal

**[[382.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-382.2 .sectionLabel}** If, in an
application or appeal commenced in the Federal Court of Appeal, 180 days
have elapsed since the issuance of the notice of application or appeal
and no requisition for a hearing date has been filed, the Court may
issue a notice of status review in Form 382.2 to the parties.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-214, s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations when applicant or
appellant in default

- **[[382.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-382.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the party in default is the
  applicant or the appellant, that party, within 30 days after the
  issuance of the notice of status review, shall serve and file
  representations stating the reasons why the proceeding should not be
  dismissed for delay. The representations shall include a justification
  for the delay and a proposed timetable for the completion of the steps
  necessary to advance the proceeding in an expeditious manner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations when respondent in
  default

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the party in default is the respondent, that
  party, within 30 days after the issuance of the notice of status
  review, shall serve and file representations stating the reasons why
  default judgment should not be entered. The representations shall
  include a justification for the delay and a proposed timetable for the
  completion of the steps necessary to advance the proceeding in an
  expeditious manner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations of the other party

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The other party may serve and file representations
  within 10 days after being served with the representations of the
  party that is in default.

- Definition of [[party in default]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In this rule and rule 382.4, [[party in
  default]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the party that failed to take the
  next chronological step required by these Rules after the last step
  that was completed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-214, s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review to be in writing

- **[[382.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-382.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court directs otherwise,
  a status review of a proceeding commenced in the Federal Court of
  Appeal shall be conducted on the basis of the written representations
  of the parties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review by a judge

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A judge shall conduct a status review and may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if he or she is not satisfied that the proceeding
    should continue and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the party in default is the applicant or the
      appellant, dismiss the proceeding, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the party in default is the respondent, grant
      judgment in favour of the applicant or appellant or order the
      applicant or appellant to proceed to prove entitlement to the
      judgment claimed; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if he or she is satisfied that the proceeding
    should continue,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} give any directions that are necessary for the
      just, most expeditious and least expensive outcome of the
      proceeding, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} fix the period for completion of subsequent
      steps in the proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-214, s. 1
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 18(E)](#1331719-1336742){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331719-1336742.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-382.4-20070927.html "Link to previous version of section 382.4"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Specially Managed Proceedings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1015760 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Case management judges --- Federal Court

**[[383]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-383 .sectionLabel}** The Chief Justice of
the Federal Court may assign

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} one or more judges to act as a case management judge
  in a proceeding;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} one or more prothonotaries to act as a case
  management judge in a proceeding; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a prothonotary to assist in the management of a
  proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 22
- SOR/2007-214, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-383-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 383"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Case management judges --- Federal Court
of Appeal

**[[383.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-383.1 .sectionLabel}** The Chief Justice
of the Federal Court of Appeal may assign one or more judges to act as a
case management judge in a proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 23
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order for special management

**[[384]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-384 .sectionLabel}** The Court may at any
time order that a proceeding continue as a specially managed proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2007-214, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-384-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 384"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class proceedings

**[[384.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-384.1 .sectionLabel}** A proceeding
commenced by a member of a class of persons on behalf of the members of
that class shall be conducted as a specially managed proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 23
- SOR/2007-301, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-384.1-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 384.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of case management judge or
prothonotary

- **[[385]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-385
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court directs otherwise,
  a case management judge or a prothonotary assigned under paragraph
  383(c) shall deal with all matters that arise prior to the trial or
  hearing of a specially managed proceeding and may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} give any directions or make any orders that are
    necessary for the just, most expeditious and least expensive outcome
    of the proceeding;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} notwithstanding any period provided for in these
    Rules, fix the period for completion of subsequent steps in the
    proceeding;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} fix and conduct any dispute resolution or pre-trial
    conferences that he or she considers necessary; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection 50(1), hear and determine all
    motions arising prior to the assignment of a hearing date.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order for status review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A case management judge or a prothonotary assigned
  under paragraph 383(c) may, at any time, order that a status review be
  held in accordance with this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to cease special management

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A case management judge or a prothonotary assigned
  under paragraph 383(c) may order that a proceeding, other than a class
  proceeding, cease to be conducted as a specially managed proceeding,
  in which case the periods set out in these Rules for taking any
  subsequent steps apply.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 24
- SOR/2007-214, s. 4
- SOR/2007-301, s. 10(E)
- SOR/2013-18, s. 14
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 19(E)](#1331721-1336744){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331721-1336744.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-385-20130208.html "Link to previous version of section 385"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Dispute Resolution Services]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015793 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order for dispute resolution conference

- **[[386]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-386 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Court may order that a proceeding, or any issue in a proceeding, be
  referred to a dispute resolution conference, to be conducted in
  accordance with rules 387 to 389 and any directions set out in the
  order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit for dispute resolution
  conference

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise, a dispute
  resolution conference shall be completed within 30 days.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

**[[387]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-387 .sectionLabel}** A dispute resolution
conference shall be conducted by a case management judge or prothonotary
assigned under paragraph 383(c), who may

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} conduct a mediation, to assist the parties by meeting
  with them together or separately to encourage and facilitate
  discussion between them in an attempt to reach a mutually acceptable
  resolution of the dispute;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} conduct an early neutral evaluation of a proceeding,
  to evaluate the relative strengths and weaknesses of the positions
  advanced by the parties and render a non-binding opinion as to the
  probable outcome of the proceeding; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} conduct a mini-trial, presiding over presentation by
  counsel for the parties of their best case and rendering a non-binding
  opinion as to the probable outcome of the proceeding.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality

**[[388]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-388 .sectionLabel}** Discussions in a
dispute resolution conference and documents prepared for the purposes of
such a conference are confidential and shall not be disclosed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of settlement

- **[[389]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-389
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a settlement of all or part of
  a proceeding is reached at a dispute resolution conference,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it shall be reduced to writing and signed by the
    parties or their solicitors; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a notice of settlement in Form 389 shall be filed
    within 10 days after the settlement is reached.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report of partial settlement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a settlement of only part of a proceeding is
  reached at a dispute resolution conference, the case management judge
  shall make an order setting out the issues that have not been resolved
  and giving such directions as he or she considers necessary for their
  adjudication.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of failure to settle

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where no settlement can be reached at a dispute
  resolution conference, the case management judge shall record that
  fact on the Court file.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay of proceedings

**[[390]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-390 .sectionLabel}** On motion, a case
management judge or a prothonotary assigned under paragraph 383(c) may,
by order, stay a proceeding, including a proceeding that has previously
been stayed, for a period of not more than six months, on the ground
that the parties have undertaken to refer the subject-matter of the
proceeding to an alternative means of dispute resolution, other than a
dispute resolution conference referred to in rule 386.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Case management judge not to preside at
hearing

**[[391]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-391 .sectionLabel}** A case management judge
who conducts a dispute resolution conference in an action, application
or appeal shall not preside at the hearing thereof unless all parties
consent.

## [PART 10]{.HLabel1}[Orders]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1015819 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of hearing

- **[[392]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-392 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Court may dispose of any matter that is the subject-matter of a
  hearing by signing an order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective time of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless it provides otherwise, an order is effective
  from the time that it is endorsed in writing and signed by the
  presiding judge or prothonotary or, in the case of an order given
  orally from the bench in circumstances that render it impracticable to
  endorse a written copy of the order, at the time it is made.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

**[[393]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-393 .sectionLabel}** The Court may deliver
reasons for judgment

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} orally from the bench at the conclusion of the
  hearing of a proceeding; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} after having reserved judgment at the conclusion of a
  hearing, by depositing in the Registry written reasons, signed by the
  judge or prothonotary who delivered them.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Drafting of order

- **[[394]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-394 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  the Court gives reasons, it may direct one of the parties to prepare
  for endorsement a draft order to implement the Court's conclusion,
  approved as to form and content by the other parties or, if the
  parties cannot agree on the form and content of the order, to bring a
  motion for judgment in accordance with rule 369 or 369.2, as the case
  may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pronouncement of judgment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On the return of a motion under subsection (1), the
  Court shall settle the terms of and pronounce the judgment, which
  shall be endorsed in writing and signed by the presiding judge or
  prothonotary.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2021-244, s. 20](#1331723-1336747){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1331723-1336747.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-394-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 394"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies to be sent

- **[[395]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-395
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 36(3), the
  Administrator shall send without delay a copy of every order made and
  of any reasons given other than in open court to all parties

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by registered mail;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by electronic means, including facsimile and
    electronic mail; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} by any other means, as directed by the Chief
    Justice, likely to bring the order and any reasons to the attention
    of the party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of receipt

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an order and any reasons are transmitted by
  electronic means, the Administrator shall confirm receipt by the party
  and place proof of that receipt on the Court file.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2010-177, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-395-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 395"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recording of orders

**[[396]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-396 .sectionLabel}** Every order shall be
recorded by the Administrator forthwith after it is made.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion to reconsider

- **[[397]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-397
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within 10 days after the making of
  an order, or within such other time as the Court may allow, a party
  may serve and file a notice of motion to request that the Court, as
  constituted at the time the order was made, reconsider its terms on
  the ground that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the order does not accord with any reasons given
    for it; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a matter that should have been dealt with has been
    overlooked or accidentally omitted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mistakes

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Clerical mistakes, errors or omissions in an order
  may at any time be corrected by the Court.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay of order

- **[[398]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-398 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  the motion of a person against whom an order has been made,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the order has not been appealed, the court
    that made the order may order that it be stayed; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where a notice of appeal of the order has been
    issued, a judge of the court that is to hear the appeal may order
    that it be stayed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} As a condition to granting a stay under subsection
  (1), a judge may require that the appellant

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide security for costs; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} do anything required to ensure that the order will
    be complied with when the stay is lifted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Setting aside of stay

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A judge of the court that is to hear an appeal of an
  order that has been stayed pending appeal may set aside the stay if
  the judge is satisfied that the party who sought the stay is not
  expeditiously proceeding with the appeal or that for any other reason
  the order should no longer be stayed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 40
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Setting aside or variance

- **[[399]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-399 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  motion, the Court may set aside or vary an order that was made

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"}; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the absence of a party who failed to appear by
    accident or mistake or by reason of insufficient notice of the
    proceeding,

  if the party against whom the order is made discloses a [prima
  facie]{.otherLang lang="la"} case why the order should not have been
  made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Setting aside or variance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On motion, the Court may set aside or vary an order

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by reason of a matter that arose or was discovered
    subsequent to the making of the order; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the order was obtained by fraud.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise, the setting aside
  or variance of an order under subsection (1) or (2) does not affect
  the validity or character of anything done or not done before the
  order was set aside or varied.

## [PART 11]{.HLabel1}[Costs]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1015878 .Part}

### [Awarding of Costs Between Parties]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015879 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discretionary powers of Court

- **[[400]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-400 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Court shall have full discretionary power over the amount and
  allocation of costs and the determination of by whom they are to be
  paid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Crown

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Costs may be awarded to or against the Crown.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors in awarding costs

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In exercising its discretion under subsection (1),
  the Court may consider

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the result of the proceeding;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the amounts claimed and the amounts recovered;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the importance and complexity of the issues;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the apportionment of liability;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any written offer to settle;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any offer to contribute made under rule 421;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the amount of work;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} whether the public interest in having the
    proceeding litigated justifies a particular award of costs;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any conduct of a party that tended to shorten or
    unnecessarily lengthen the duration of the proceeding;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the failure by a party to admit anything that
    should have been admitted or to serve a request to admit;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} whether any step in the proceeding was

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} improper, vexatious or unnecessary, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} taken through negligence, mistake or excessive
      caution;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} whether more than one set of costs should be
    allowed, where two or more parties were represented by different
    solicitors or were represented by the same solicitor but separated
    their defence unnecessarily;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} whether two or more parties, represented by the
    same solicitor, initiated separate proceedings unnecessarily;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} whether a party who was successful in an action
    exaggerated a claim, including a counterclaim or third party claim,
    to avoid the operation of rules 292 to 299;

  - [(n.1)]{.lawlabel} whether the expense required to have an expert
    witness give evidence was justified given

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the litigation, its public
      significance and any need to clarify the law,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the number, complexity or technical nature of
      the issues in dispute, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the amount in dispute in the proceeding; and

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} any other matter that it considers relevant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tariff B

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Court may fix all or part of any costs by
  reference to Tariff B and may award a lump sum in lieu of, or in
  addition to, any assessed costs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions re assessment

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where the Court orders that costs be assessed in
  accordance with Tariff B, the Court may direct that the assessment be
  performed under a specific column or combination of columns of the
  table to that Tariff.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further discretion of Court

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding any other provision of these Rules,
  the Court may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} award or refuse costs in respect of a particular
    issue or step in a proceeding;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} award assessed costs or a percentage of assessed
    costs up to and including a specified step in a proceeding;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} award all or part of costs on a
    solicitor-and-client basis; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} award costs against a successful party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Award and payment of costs

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Costs shall be awarded to the party who is entitled
  to receive the costs and not to the party's solicitor, but they may be
  paid to the party's solicitor in trust.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-417, s. 25(F)
- SOR/2010-176, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-400-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 400"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs of motion

- **[[401]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-401 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Court may award costs of a motion in an amount fixed by the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs payable forthwith

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Court is satisfied that a motion should not
  have been brought or opposed, the Court shall order that the costs of
  the motion be payable forthwith.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs of discontinuance or abandonment

**[[402]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-402 .sectionLabel}** Unless otherwise
ordered by the Court or agreed by the parties, a party against whom an
action, application or appeal has been discontinued or against whom a
motion has been abandoned is entitled to costs forthwith, which may be
assessed and the payment of which may be enforced as if judgment for the
amount of the costs had been given in favour of that party.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion for directions

- **[[403]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-403 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party may request that directions be given to the assessment officer
  respecting any matter referred to in rule 400,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by serving and filing a notice of motion within 30
    days after judgment has been pronounced; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in a motion for judgment under subsection 394(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motion after judgment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A motion may be brought under paragraph (1)(a)
  whether or not the judgment included an order concerning costs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Same judge or prothonotary

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A motion under paragraph (1)(a) shall be brought
  before the judge or prothonotary who signed the judgment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability of solicitor for costs

- **[[404]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-404
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where costs in a proceeding are
  incurred improperly or without reasonable cause or are wasted by undue
  delay or other misconduct or default, the Court may make an order
  against any solicitor whom it considers to be responsible, whether
  personally or through a servant or agent,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} directing the solicitor personally pay the costs of
    a party to the proceeding; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} disallowing the costs between the solicitor and the
    solicitor's client.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Show cause by solicitor

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No order under subsection (1) shall be made against a
  solicitor unless the solicitor has been given an opportunity to be
  heard.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to client

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Court may order that notice of an order against a
  solicitor made under subsection (1) be given to the solicitor's client
  in a manner specified by the Court.

### [Assessment of Costs]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015947 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment by assessment officer

**[[405]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-405 .sectionLabel}** Costs shall be assessed
by an assessment officer.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obtaining appointment

- **[[406]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-406 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  party who is entitled to costs may obtain a notice of appointment for
  assessment by filing a bill of costs, a copy of the order or other
  document giving rise to the party's entitlement to costs and any
  reasons, including dissenting reasons, given in respect of that order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of appointment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice of appointment for assessment and the bill
  of costs to be assessed shall be served on every other interested
  party at least 10 days before the date fixed for the assessment.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-219, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-406-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 406"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment according to Tariff B

**[[407]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-407 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Court orders
otherwise, party-and-party costs shall be assessed in accordance with
column III of the table to Tariff B.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions

- **[[408]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-408 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  assessment officer may direct the production of books and documents
  and give directions for the conduct of an assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Set-off of costs

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where parties are liable to pay costs to each other,
  an assessment officer may adjust those costs by way of set-off.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs of assessment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An assessment officer may assess and allow, or refuse
  to allow, the costs of an assessment to either party.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors in assessing costs

**[[409]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-409 .sectionLabel}** In assessing costs, an
assessment officer may consider the factors referred to in subsection
400(3).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs of amendment

- **[[410]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-410
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise,
  the costs occasioned by an amendment to a pleading made without leave
  shall be borne by the party making the amendment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs of motion to extend time

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise, the costs of a
  motion for an extension of time shall be borne by the party bringing
  the motion.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs of abandoned motion

**[[411]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-411 .sectionLabel}** The costs of a motion
that is abandoned or deemed to be abandoned may be assessed on the
filing of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the notice of motion, together with an affidavit
  stating that the notice was not filed within the prescribed time or
  that the moving party did not appear at the hearing of the motion; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where a notice of abandonment was served, the notice
  of abandonment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs of discontinued proceeding

**[[412]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-412 .sectionLabel}** The costs of a
proceeding that is discontinued may be assessed on the filing of the
notice of discontinuance.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accounts of solicitor for Crown

- **[[413]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-413
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where requested by the Attorney
  General of Canada, a prothonotary shall assess any costs payable by
  the Crown to a solicitor acting for the Crown in a proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Existing rights

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) shall not be construed so as to
  prejudice any rights between a solicitor and a client in respect of
  the recovery of the solicitor's costs in any competent court.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of assessment

**[[414]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-414 .sectionLabel}** A party who is
dissatisfied with an assessment of an assessment officer who is not a
judge may, within 10 days after the assessment, serve and file a notice
of motion to request that a judge of the Federal Court review the award
of costs.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-283, s. 33
:::

### [Security for Costs]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1015988 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[415]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-415 .sectionLabel}** Rules 416 to 418 apply,
with such modifications as are necessary, to parties bringing and
defending counterclaims and third party claims, to applicants and
respondents in an application and to appellants and respondents in an
appeal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where security available

- **[[416]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-416
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where, on the motion of a defendant,
  it appears to the Court that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the plaintiff is ordinarily resident outside
    Canada,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the plaintiff is a corporation, an unincorporated
    association or a nominal plaintiff and there is reason to believe
    that the plaintiff would have insufficient assets in Canada
    available to pay the costs of the defendant if ordered to do so,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the plaintiff has not provided an address in the
    statement of claim, or has provided an incorrect address therein,
    and has not satisfied the Court that the omission or misstatement
    was made innocently and without intention to deceive,

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the plaintiff has changed address during the course
    of the proceeding with a view to evading the consequences of the
    litigation,

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the plaintiff has another proceeding for the same
    relief pending elsewhere,

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the defendant has an order against the plaintiff
    for costs in the same or another proceeding that remain unpaid in
    whole or in part,

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} there is reason to believe that the action is
    frivolous and vexatious and the plaintiff would have insufficient
    assets in Canada available to pay the costs of the defendant, if
    ordered to do so, or

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} an Act of Parliament entitles the defendant to
    security for costs,

  the Court may order the plaintiff to give security for the defendant's
  costs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staging

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Court may order that security for the costs of a
  defendant be given in stages, as costs are incurred.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further steps

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Court orders otherwise, until the security
  required by an order under subsection (1) or (2) has been given, the
  plaintiff may not take any further step in the action, other than an
  appeal from that order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Party temporarily resident in Canada

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A party ordinarily resident outside Canada may be
  ordered to give security for costs, notwithstanding that the party may
  be temporarily resident in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voluntary payment into court

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In the absence of an order under subsection (1), a
  plaintiff may, at any time after filing a statement of claim, pay an
  amount into court as security for the defendant's costs and give
  notice of the payment to the defendant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Increase in security

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Court may, on the motion of a defendant, order a
  plaintiff who has paid an amount into court under subsection (5) to
  pay in an additional amount as security for the defendant's costs.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grounds for refusing security

**[[417]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-417 .sectionLabel}** The Court may refuse to
order that security for costs be given under any of paragraphs 416(1)(a)
to (g) if a plaintiff demonstrates impecuniosity and the Court is of the
opinion that the case has merit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}How security to be given

**[[418]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-418 .sectionLabel}** Where a person is
required under these Rules or an Act of Parliament to give security for
costs or for any other purpose, unless otherwise ordered by the Court or
required by that Act, the person may do so

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} by paying the required amount into court; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} by filing a bond for the required amount that has
  been approved by an order of the Court.

### [Offer to Settle]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1016019 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to other proceedings

**[[419]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-419 .sectionLabel}** Rules 420 and 421
apply, with such modifications as are necessary, to parties bringing and
defending counterclaims and third party claims, to applicants and
respondents in an application and to appellants and respondents in an
appeal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consequences of failure to accept
plaintiff's offer

- **[[420]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-420
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise ordered by the
  Court and subject to subsection (3), where a plaintiff makes a written
  offer to settle and obtains a judgment as favourable or more
  favourable than the terms of the offer to settle, the plaintiff is
  entitled to party-and-party costs to the date of service of the offer
  and costs calculated at double that rate, but not double
  disbursements, after that date.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consequences of failure to accept
  defendant's offer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise ordered by the Court and subject to
  subsection (3), where a defendant makes a written offer to settle,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the plaintiff obtains a judgment less favourable
    than the terms of the offer to settle, the plaintiff is entitled to
    party-and-party costs to the date of service of the offer and the
    defendant shall be entitled to costs calculated at double that rate,
    but not double disbursements, from that date to the date of
    judgment; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the plaintiff fails to obtain judgment, the
    defendant is entitled to party-and-party costs to the date of the
    service of the offer and to costs calculated at double that rate,
    but not double disbursements, from that date to the date of
    judgment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) and (2) do not apply unless the offer
  to settle

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is made at least 14 days before the commencement of
    the hearing or trial; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is not withdrawn and does not expire before the
    commencement of the hearing or trial.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-340, s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offers without costs

- **[[420.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-420.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In circumstances where a written
  offer to settle does not provide for the settlement of the issue of
  costs, if a party requests the Court to consider rule 420, the Court,
